## FINAL EFFICIENCY CONFIG (2026-04-11) — step199

**95.52% @ 0.98M routing MACs — both ≤1% FLOPs AND ≤1% params met simultaneously.**
⚠️ **FLOPs clarification (2026-04-14 audit):** 0.98M = routing-only message-passing MACs (N×K_iter×K_hh×D×2). True per-sample FLOPs ≈ 6.5M (incl seed gather K_in=50, AH suppression, normalize, readout). VGG FC = 123M → real ratio ≈ **19× fewer FLOPs** (not 116×). Paper must label "message-passing MACs". Cross-check with ncu (step800).

| Param | Value |
|-------|-------|
| N | 2048 |
| D | 16 |
| K_hh | 2 (K_local=1, K_random=1) |
| K_iter | 5 |
| K_in | 25 |
| n_groups | 256 (max(8, N//8)) |
| alpha_ahebb | 1.0 |
| alpha_reflect | 0.5 |
| alpha_turing | 0.0 |
| mode | dynamic_z_geo |
| beam_size | 16 |
| geo_gamma | 0.5 |
| K_phase | 8 |
| norm_mode | l2 |
| encoding_mode | fourier |
| seed | 42 |
| epochs | 150 (best_ep=136) |
| data | 100% Imagenette |

**Results:**

| Metric | Value | vs VGG16 FC |
|--------|-------|-------------|
| Accuracy | 95.52% | +0.52pp |
| FLOPs | 0.98M | **0.79%** |
| Params | **34,976** | **0.029%** (CONFIRMED — 67K was STALE pre-spatial-precomputation refactor) |

**Efficiency frontier (D=16, K_hh=2 family):**

---

**GA rule (STRICT):** Every new experiment base = ALL confirmed winners from all prior generations.
**Calibration rule:** When base changes generation/scale, run 40-50ep param sweep BEFORE full 150ep runs.
**Critical Findings:** See [EXPERIMENT_QUEUE_CRITICAL_FINDINGS.md](EXPERIMENT_QUEUE_CRITICAL_FINDINGS.md)

**Key principle (2026-04-10):** N=1024 winners ARE wins even if no scale to N=4096. If mechanisms push N=1024 toward 96%+, massive efficiency gain (4-6× fewer FLOPs than N=4096). Stop dismissing N=1024 results.

## GLAM — new layer type (parallel line, 2026-08-13)

Spec: `learnings/concepts/glam_grouped_multiplicative_routing.md`. Model `src/sgnnet/model_glam.py`,
script `scripts/glam/glam_step001_ablation_t0.py`. Standing goal: param/energy on full Pareto table.

**glam_step001 T0 ladder — DONE (mini_cpu, 20ep/50%, seed42). SCOUT ONLY, nothing rejected.**
n=1 on a degenerate 6–8K-param model; all arms ≪ 94% Ref_dw because the model is tiny, so absolute
numbers and −pp gaps carry little signal. Ordering: A1 locality 0.6494 ≈ A3 0.6482 > A2 mul 0.5445 >
A8 depth 0.4769 > A4/A4b/A5 selectivity ~0.42 > A7det anneal 0.3896. **No arm KILLED** — user flagged
(2026-08-13) that rejecting on this small a set is premature; do proper due diligence first.

**glam_step002 width sweep + multi-seed op ablation — DONE (mini_cpu, 20ep/50%). Root cause of the
step001 degeneracy = an 8-dim terminal straw (GLAMNet forced last-layer `add`); fixed with
`collapse_last=False`, layers stay wide. Full results in the concept file "T0 COMPETITIVE RESULTS".**

**CONFIRMED (T0, 3 seeds, clean control):**
- **P0 DONE — locality (A1) reaches the ceiling.** Shared-pool per-channel proj (M=16, 49→8) + wide
  readout = **96.96% ±0.10pp @ 47,370 params (0.040% FC)**. Pareto-dominates FFN-head (93.2% @
  1.11M, +3.8pp at 23× fewer params) and beats SGNNET champion (95.95%). Sits at D=16 feature
  ceiling 97.30%. Width sweep 96.4–97.4% over 47K–469K. **New efficient-DL primitive.**
- **P2 DONE (op) — mul<add<loc, multi-seed non-overlapping.** A2 mul net-negative & compounds with
  depth (−2.1pp L=1 → −7.5pp L=2, gate-death holds); A3 add neutral (−0.11pp). Mul NOT KILLED (may
  earn an energy/interaction axis) but buys no accuracy. Depth doesn't help at ceiling (L=1≈L=2).

**T1 validation — DONE (mini_cpu, 75ep/50%, 3 seeds). WIN HOLDS up the tier ladder.**
- 47K champ (L=1 d=8 M=16) = **97.15 ±0.13** (97.17/96.99/97.30) — T0 was 96.96, +0.19pp, no
  compression erosion. 189K (L=1 d=32 M=16) = 97.22 ±0.10 (97.35/97.22/97.10), flat = ceiling.
- Both hold above T0 (96.96) and crush FFN-head (93.2%). Efficiency result survives tier bump.

**T2 validation — DONE (mini_cpu, 150ep/100%, 3 seeds). PAPER-BOUND. TIER-COMPLETE.**
- 47K champ = **97.68 ±0.05** (97.63/97.71/97.71). Ladder 96.96→97.15→97.68, headline STRENGTHENS
  (+0.53pp T1→T2, opposite of usual compression). Pareto-dominates FFN-head (+4.5pp @ 23× fewer
  params) and SGNNET champion (95.95%, +1.73pp). **/goal deliverable tier-complete.**

**Mechanism 2 selectivity — DONE (step003 T0 3-seed).** S0 loc=96.96, S1 +affinity |cos|=96.89
(−0.07 neutral), S2 +anti-Hebbian=96.66 (−0.30). Affinity neutral (not dead); payoff = dormancy,
invisible on saturated ceiling. CONFIRMED (T0).

**User's key×value combine — DONE (step004 T0 3-seed). SIGMOID is the mechanism.** KV
sigmoid(key)·value = 96.91(M34)/96.84(M64) ties additive locality; PV raw key·value = 93.0/93.5
collapses to FFN ceiling. Once-per-layer sigmoid gate lifts +3.9pp, does NOT die (gate-death boundary
confirmed). My earlier A2 "mul negative" tested wrong (symmetric) variant. `model_glam_keyval.py`.

**Depth-below-ceiling on CIFAR-10 — DONE (step005 T0 3-seed, mini). DECISIVE, my hypothesis FALSIFIED.**
CIFAR-10 VGG16 features (real headroom). Within-tier anchor LIN=`nn.Linear(25088,10)`:
- LIN dense 86.90±0.18 @ 250,890 | LOC locality 85.66±0.04 @ 47,370 (−1.24 vs dense, 5.3× fewer params)
- KV sigmoid(key)·val 85.77±0.15 @ 54,570 (+0.11 vs LOC = NOISE, +15% params → Pareto-dominated)
- LOCL2 depth L=2 84.83±0.23 @ 48,522 (−0.83 vs LOC → depth HURTS)
**Verdict:** headroom present, mechanisms STILL neutral/negative — "needs headroom to differentiate"
FALSIFIED. Locality (mechanism 1) is the ENTIRE algorithm on BOTH saturated (Imagenette, matches
ceiling) and headroom (CIFAR, −1.24pp @ 5.3× fewer params) tasks. KV no advance; depth REJECTED.
Script `scripts/glam/glam_step005_cifar_depth_t0.py`.

**Mechanism 4 (slope anneal / energy) — DONE (step006, T0 3-seed, CONFIRMED — LAST open GLAM arm):**
GLAM-LOC champion, log-linear slope 1.0→1e-5 vs constant-0.01 CTRL, scored on activation-zero fraction.
CTRL 96.96±0.12 @ zero_frac 0.398; A7DET 96.81±0.03 @ 0.410; A7RAND 96.86±0.08 @ 0.410. Anneal lifts
dormancy only +1.2pp (far below plain-ReLU ~0.52 ref) at −0.10/−0.15pp acc → **FAILS its energy gate;
no Joules run warranted.** Script `scripts/glam/glam_step006_slope_anneal_energy_t0.py`.
**GLAM now fully characterized: all 4 user mechanisms tested & faithfully implemented — locality
VALIDATED (T0→T1→T2 97.68%), mechanisms 2/3/4 + depth NEUTRAL-or-negative. Locality = entire algorithm;
shipped primitive = shared-pool structured FC-head replacement. This is the "best version" to scale
(PAPER_CAMPAIGN.md, recorded plan; scaling runs after 2-day iteration).**

**SCALE-INVARIANCE CONFIRMED (step007, T1 3-seed, VGG16 backbone, on-disk no-download):** locality-vs-dense
gap FLAT across 10× class count. CIFAR-10 (10cls): dense 87.06±0.11 vs LOC 85.53±0.07 = −1.53pp @ 5.3×
fewer params. CIFAR-100 (100cls): dense 65.28±0.11 vs LOC 63.69±0.11 = −1.59pp @ 6× fewer params. Gap
Δ −0.06pp over 10× classes → **locality costs constant ~1.5pp for 5–6× param saving; does NOT widen with
scale** (the PAPER_CAMPAIGN R2 claim, done early on disk). **METHODOLOGY: T0/20ep FLATTERS structured vs
dense head (dense under-trained at 20ep — at CIFAR-100 T0 LOC 64.12 *beat* dense 63.54, reversed at T1);
LOC-vs-dense valid only at T1+.** Retro-corrects step005 CIFAR-10 T0 −1.24pp → honest T1 −1.53pp. Script
`scripts/glam/glam_step007_cifar100_scale_t0.py` (+step005 --epochs 75).

**BACKBONE-INVARIANCE CONFIRMED (T1 3-seed, on-disk):** store_resnet18.h5 = ResNet-18 conv feats
(25088-dim, Imagenette 10cls). Dense LIN 98.38±0.05 vs LOC 98.24±0.15 = −0.14pp @ 5.3× fewer params.
VGG16-tuned champion transfers to ResNet-18 within noise. **Full 2-backbone × 3-dataset matrix: gap tracks
feature HEADROOM not backbone/class-count** — saturated (Imagenette) ≈0, headroom (CIFAR) ~1.5pp. Backbone
invariance holds; class-count "invariance" was T1-only — see T2 below. Ran via glam_step005 with
`--store data/store_resnet18.h5 --epochs 75`.

**T2 OVERTURNS class-count scale-invariance (step005/007 T2, 150ep/100%, 3-seed, paper-grade CONFIRMED):**
CIFAR-10 dense 88.11±0.06 vs LOC 86.64±0.10 = −1.47pp @ 5.3× fewer; CIFAR-100 dense 68.49±0.08 vs LOC
65.35±0.40 = −3.14pp @ 6× fewer. **Gap WIDENS with class count at full budget (−1.47→−3.14pp), NOT flat.**
T1 flat-gap = reduced-budget artifact (50%data/75ep, unsaturated); at full budget dense's 2.5M capacity
pays off at 100cls, LOC's fixed 416K pool bottlenecks. **METHODOLOGY: reduced-budget tiers understate
LOC-vs-dense gap at high class count; scale-invariance claims REQUIRE T2.**

**CAPACITY-SCALING PROBE — gap STRUCTURAL, not capacity (step007 CIFAR-100 T2, 3-seed, CONFIRMED, DONE):**
| config | params | mean% | gap | Δ prev |
| LOC d_out=8 (champ) | 416K | 65.35±0.40 | −3.14 | — |
| LOC d_out=16 | 832K | 66.27±0.09 | −2.22 | +0.92 (2× params) |
| LOC d_out=32 | 1.66M | 66.44±0.30 | −2.05 | +0.17 (2× more) |
| LOC d_out=16 M=64 | 870K | 66.26±0.12 | −2.23 | +0.00 (M inert) |
Widening LOC only PARTLY recovers the gap, log-diminishing: 4× params → +1.09pp then flat; at 1.66M
(2/3 of dense 2.5M) still −2.05pp. **M floor-only (diversity inert).** 100-cls gap is STRUCTURAL — shared
pool compresses 25088 → P·d_out bottleneck (4096–16384) before class readout; dense is full-rank 25088→100.
Widening bottleneck toward dense erases the param win. **Honest paper story: genuine compression↔accuracy
tradeoff — locality near-free at 10cls (~1.5pp), structural ~2pp at 100cls capacity can't buy back.**
Capacity-scaling direction CLOSED. Logs `logs/glam/glam_step007_capD*`, results `glam_step007_LOC_capD*`.

**CIFAR-10 CONTROL — residual is class-count-dependent (step005 T2, 3-seed, CONFIRMED, DONE):** same
d_out sweep at 10cls (dense 88.11 @ 250K): d_out=8 86.64 (47K, −1.47) → 16 86.98 (95K, −1.13) → 32 87.45
(189K, −0.66). At 10cls capacity KEEPS closing (−1.47→−0.66, still moving, NOT saturated → ~0 extrapolated);
at 100cls SATURATES at ~2pp floor. **Residual gap capacity-recoverable at low C, STRUCTURAL floor at high
C.** Two matched T2 3-seed frontiers in hand. GLAM fully characterized on all on-disk levers; next = deferred
large-dataset campaign (carry the frontier, sweep d_out). Logs `logs/glam/glam_step005_c10capD*`.

**glam_step008 information-MERGING rung — DONE (T0 3-seed, CONFIRMED, 2026-08-14). DIRECTION TERMINATED.**
Can explicit cross-chunk merge recover the −3.14pp CIFAR-100 gap? 5 clean negatives (Ref = LOC ≈0.640 T0):
M=32/64 flat (0.6364/0.6391); additive low-rank merge neutral (readout absorbs it — linear-redundant);
bilinear 2nd-order q⊙k −1.0pp (0.6303, overfits); pre-chunk merge −0.5pp (0.6353); low-rank readout
CRASHES (ro32=0.558, ro16=0.497 — 100-way needs rank≈C). **The merge already lives inside the concat→dense
readout; cannot be made cheaper (readout is param-dominant, rank-bound by C) or richer (bilinear overfits).**
`model_glam_merge.py`, `scripts/glam/glam_step008_merge_t0.py`.

**glam_step009 GAP-gate rung — DONE (T0 3-seed, CONFIRMED, 2026-08-14). Residual-gate law REFUTED.**
FgSegNet_v2's `x*(1+GAP(shallow))` on the LOC head. S0 control 64.14 ±0.24 (reproduces step007 LOC d8
64.12 exactly); S1 residual unbounded 62.73 (−1.41); S2 S1+learned scale 62.81 (−1.33); S3 bounded
`sigmoid(GAP)`, no residual, 64.28 (+0.14, noise). S1/S3 both +0 params → no capacity confound.
**Boundedness is the surviving ingredient; the residual wrapper does NOT rescue an unbounded gate.**
`scripts/glam/glam_step009_gap_gate_t0.py`, `results/glam/glam_step009_*.json`.

**glam_step010 CReLU/RReLU rung — DONE (T0 3-seed, CONFIRMED, 2026-08-14). All 4 arms KILLED.**
Sign-phase rank-doubling at iso-param (readout dominates, so CReLU d_out=k costs ReLU d_out=2k — both
ReLU points already measured in step007). CReLU d4 63.25 vs ReLU d8 64.12 (−0.87); CReLU d8 64.81 vs
ReLU d16 66.27 (−1.46); MIX d8 64.57 (−1.70); RReLU 64.15 ≈ leaky 64.21 (inert) but `zero_frac` 0.000
vs plain-ReLU 0.398–0.41 → **pure energy loss**. CReLU's `zero_frac=0.500` sits on 2× activations →
absolute nonzeros unchanged, no energy win either. `scripts/glam/glam_step010_crelu_t0.py`.

**glam generality/novelty measurement — DONE (T0, 2026-08-17). Readout add-on DEMOTED; LOC backbone GENERAL.**
User asked: is weight-RReLU a general discovery or overfit? Two-axis falsification, both fire.
(A) novelty vs pruning (Imagenette C=10 R1 s0.9 T0 3-seed): one-shot prune 92.98 / hard-mask+STE 96.77 / ours-anneal
96.62. Ours beats naive prune +3.6pp (≠plain pruning) BUT hard-from-start ties ours → **anneal INERT**, reduces to
known straight-through/LTP pruning. (B) C-over-param confound (T0 seed42): s0.9 Δ = −0.22(Imagenette C10) / −1.79
(CIFAR10 C10) / **−5.78pp (CIFAR100 C100)** → free-compress was rank-≤C over-param harvesting, C-FRAGILE.
Resolving control — LOC vs param-matched plain Linear(25088,C): Imagenette 96.97 vs 97.02 @5.3× / CIFAR10 85.60 vs
86.86 @5.3× / CIFAR100 63.97 vs 65.33 @6.0× → **locality+merge = STABLE 5–6× param cut at ≤1.4pp across C=10..100**
incl. rank-loaded C=100 where the add-on collapsed. VERDICT: general win = the BACKBONE, not the readout trick.
T1 3-seed CIFAR100 CONFIRMED: LOC 63.70 ±0.15 vs plain Linear 65.78 ±0.17 = **−2.08pp @ 6.0× param cut**. Param
FACTOR C-stable (~5–6×); accuracy COST scales with difficulty (≈0pp Imagenette → −2.08pp CIFAR100). Genuine Pareto
tradeoff, not free on hard tasks.

**responsibility→params (the /goal) — DONE (T0, 2026-08-17). CONFIRMED, split verdict.**
User: is param-collapse driven by model responsibility (# classes k), not dataset? "Collapsing responsibility collapses
params — fair? test both approaches." Random k-class CIFAR100 subsets, features fixed, only k varies; nested-ordering
trajectory (`scratchpad/ktraj.py`, 5 random class permutations, cumulative k=2,5,10,20,40,70,100) removes per-draw
variance. **(A) readout weight-sparsity IS responsibility-driven:** Δrdout(s0.9) −0.70pp(k=2)→−5.42pp(k=100); free
readout compression = rank-≤k over-param headroom shrinking as k grows → FAIR for A. **(B) LOC backbone is
responsibility-INDEPENDENT:** Δbb≈0 at every k (−0.40..+0.31 noise); param× 3.4→6.0 = arithmetic feature-dim ratio
25088/4096≈6.1, not k. A collapses class-dim (responsibility-coupled, C-fragile); B collapses feature-dim (free, ~0
cost, general win). Data `results/glam/ktraj_t0_seed{0..4}__*.json`. See concept glam_readout_sparsity_generality.md.
Concept `learnings/concepts/glam_readout_sparsity_generality.md`; baseline `scratchpad/linprobe.py`.

**glam_step010b weight-RReLU on WEIGHTS rung — DONE (T2 3-seed paper-bound, CONFIRMED, 2026-08-15). WINNER (regime-specific; see generality above).**
T2 (150ep/100%): R0 dense reproduces champion EXACTLY 97.68 ±0.04 (97.63/97.71/97.71); R1 det s0.9
**97.39 ±0.08 (−0.29pp) @ 10,506 eff-par = 4.5× readout compress** (0.0396%→0.0088% FC), real zeros;
R2 rand 97.42 ±0.15 ties R1 → randomization neutral, killed on parsimony. Paper-bound deliverable.
T1 detail below:
Distinct from step010 (RReLU on *activations*, killed): this gates the LOC champion's param-dominant readout
`Linear(4096,10)` weights by a train-soft / infer-HARD randomized-leaky magnitude threshold (`src/sgnnet/weight_leaky.py`,
`LeakyPrunedLinear`). Global magnitude quantile at sparsity s; train `w_eff=w·gate`, gate=1 above τ else leaky α
(log-anneal 1→1e-3, STE); infer gate=hard {0,1} → **real zeros**. R0 dense 97.16 ±0.14 / 47,370 par.
**Winner R1 det s0.9: 96.94 ±0.06 (−0.22pp, inside noise) @ 10,506 eff-par = 4.5× readout compress**, 0.0396%→0.0088% FC.
Break-point = s0.9 (monotonic knee): s0.95 −0.73pp, s0.97 −1.18pp — ≤0.5pp budget holds only at 0.9.
**Randomization DEAD**: R1(det) ties-or-beats R2(rand) at every s (T0's n=1 rand edge was noise) → kill R2 arm.
Infer-hard leg (the novel, previously-unvalidated leg) VALIDATED for the first time. Contrast: magnitude-sparsity
holds where low-rank readout crashed (step008 ro32=0.558) → sparsity beats low-rank for the C=10-bound readout.
`scripts/glam/glam_step010_weight_rrelu_t0.py`, `results/glam/glam_step010_*_t1_seed*__mini_mps.json`.

**seg_step001 coarse heat-map baseline — DONE (T0 single seed 42, mini_mps, 2026-08-14).**
Drone reframe: detection → coarse segmentation, 128px in / 16×16 out, C=4 × K=2, ceiling 1 GMAC/frame.
Teacher-distilled (cached fasterrcnn, 2000/500). B0 baseline mAP 0.2084 / 7,226,312 par / **4.898 GMAC**;
B1 +anti-Hebbian decor λ=0.05 mAP **0.2200 (+1.16pp at IDENTICAL params & MACs)**, up on all 4 classes —
looked like the first non-null decorrelation result ever — **SETTLED by seg_step006: NULL. That
+1.16pp was one seed inside a ±2pp noise floor; 7 paired seeds give mean +0.80pp (mps-only +0.32pp).
See the seg_step006 DONE block.** B2 no M_FPM 0.2022 (−0.62pp) but −1.32M par / −0.34 GMAC and **beats B0 on
person/vehicle/animal**, loses only on the `object` catch-all → pyramid likely not earning its 7%
(HYPOTHESIS). **CONFIRMED (measured by forward hook): all arms 4.6–4.9 GMAC ≈ 5× over the drone ceiling;

`scripts/seg/seg_step001_coarse_heatmap.py`, `concepts/seg_coarse_heatmap.md`.

**seg_step002 encoder FLOP levers — DONE (T0, 3 seeds 42/43/44, mini_mps, 2026-08-14).** 6 arms, one
orthogonal lever each, all ending at stride 8 so the head is untouched; 18 cells, no failures.
E0 VGG b1–4 **pretrained** 4.898 G / 7.23 M / **0.2378 ±0.0155** (pretrained ref) · E1 same shape
**random init** 4.898 G / 8.96 M / **0.1439 ±0.0049** (scratch ref) · E2 drop block4 3.232 G / 2.20 M /
0.2349 ±0.0066 · E3 width 0.5× 1.331 G / 2.63 M / 0.1467 ±0.0085 · E4 separable **0.896 G** / 2.20 M /
0.1343 ±0.0142 · E5 stride-2 stem 2.611 G / 8.96 M / 0.1477 ±0.0106. Scratch arms read vs **E1 only**
(pretrained weights exist only for unmodified VGG shapes). **CONFIRMED: ImageNet init = +9.4pp (~6σ),
10–30× any architecture lever → the drone budget is initialisation-limited, not FLOP-limited.**
**CONFIRMED: block4 is dead weight at 16×16** (−0.29pp for −34% MACs / −70% par / −22% wall-time).
**CONFIRMED: width 4× over-provisioned** (E3 +0.28pp at 3.7× fewer MACs). **CONFIRMED: all three cheap
levers ~free — E4 alone is under the 1 GMAC ceiling.** Noise floor: pretrained/frozen arm ±1.55pp (the
noisy one), scratch ±0.49–1.42pp → future pretrained levers need >~3pp. Caveat: scratch arms peak at
ep16–20 = **epoch-limited, so every scratch number is a lower bound**. Next = seg_step003, distil E2
(pretrained) into an E3/E4-shaped student — the step605 K=1 soft-KD move; architecture is solved, init
is not. `scripts/seg/seg_encoders.py`, `scripts/seg/seg_step002_encoder_flops.py`.

**seg_step003 sliced pretrained init — DONE (T0, 3 seeds, mini_mps, 6 cells, 437s, no failures,
2026-08-14).** Distillation was the planned way to hand a cheap architecture the +9.4pp init; **slicing is
cheaper** — copy the first `width` fraction of every pretrained VGG conv's output filters (and matching
input channels of the next conv). Filter order in VGG is arbitrary → "first k" is unbiased, not selection.
No teacher pass, no extra loss, no extra training: init only. E3 width 0.5× random init 1.331 G / 2.63 M /
0.1467 ±0.0085 · **E6 width 0.5× sliced init 1.331 G / 2.63 M / 0.1893 ±0.0103** · **E7 = E6 + drop block4
0.876 G / 0.85 M / 0.1920 ±0.0085**. **CONFIRMED: slicing buys +4.26pp (~4σ) at byte-identical params,
MACs and wall-time** — E6 is shape-identical to E3, the *only* variable is the init; recovers ~45% of the
9.4pp gap free, and is the first mechanism in this line that buys accuracy without buying compute.
**CONFIRMED: the two free levers compound cleanly on top of it** — E7 matches E6 (+0.27pp, inside noise) at
1.5× fewer MACs / 3.1× fewer params, despite both levers sharing the encoder signal path (Compounding Rule
risk did not materialise). **Standing Pareto: E0 ref 4.898 G / 7.23 M / 0.2378 vs E7 0.876 G (5.6× less) /
0.85 M (8.5× less) / 0.1920 (−4.6pp) — under the 1 GMAC drone ceiling.** The −4.6pp is an **upper bound**:
E7 peaks at ep16–19, still improving at the 20-ep budget end, while pretrained arms peak ep10–14. Next =
seg_step004 (1) 60-ep budget rerun of E7/E2 — cheapest, must precede any ceiling claim; (2) distil E2→E7
only if a real gap survives; (3) slice-ratio sweep width 0.25/0.75. Same script, `--arm E6/E7`.

**seg_step004 60-epoch budget rerun — DONE (T0, E7+E2 × 3 seeds, mini_mps, 6 cells, 1494s, no
failures, 2026-08-14).** Every step002/003 arm peaked at the 20-ep budget end, so the E7 gap was an
upper bound. E2 0.2349 ±0.0066 → **0.2597 ±0.0037** (+2.48pp) · E7 0.1920 ±0.0085 → **0.2079 ±0.0122**
(+1.59pp). **CONFIRMED: the epoch-limit caveat is resolved and did not favour the cheap arm** — both
arms now peak ep20–29, well inside the 60-ep budget, and the E2−E7 gap **widens 4.29pp → 5.18pp**.
E7's deficit is real init/capacity loss, not a budget artifact. Promotes distillation from
conditional to next experiment; also upgrades the teacher (E2 @60ep 0.2597 > E0 ref 0.2378 @20ep).
Next = distil E2→E7.

**seg_step005 slot control — DONE (2026-08-14). RETRACTION: my earlier "CONFIRMED: SLOT IS A
VARIABLE" tag was wrong and rested on n=1 pair (B0 s42: 0.2084 mps vs 0.2304 cpu, +2.20pp).** With
all three pairs the B0 cpu−mps deltas are **+2.20 / −1.90 / +1.19 (mean +0.50pp, mixed signs)** —
no systematic slot effect. The perfect mps/cpu separation of seg_step001's cells was coincidence at
n=3 unpaired. Cross-slot comparison remains bad practice but does not by itself void prior results.
`logs/seg_step005_device__mini_cpu.log`.

**seg_step006 paired decorrelation — DONE (2026-08-14, 8 cells, 1047s, mini_mps, failures=none).**
Two CONFIRMED results, both method-level:

1. **Anti-Hebbian decorrelation is a NULL on mAP (7 paired seeds).** Paired B1−B0, same slot/seed:
   mps +1.16 / −1.41 / +1.58 / −0.24 / +0.49 (s42–s46), cpu +3.21 / +0.78. **Mean +0.80pp over 7
   pairs (5/7 positive); mps-only mean +0.32pp over 5** — inside the noise floor below. Matches
   every prior test (A4b ≈ A4, step003 S2 −0.30pp). Keep only if the K-channel *explainability* it
   was built for is measured and earns it; that needs its own metric, not mAP.
2. **The run-to-run noise floor on this task is ~±2pp.** B0 mini_mps, 5 seeds: 0.2084 / 0.2616 /
   0.2302 / 0.2206 / 0.2424 = **0.2326 ±0.0203**. Every 3-seed bar earlier in this line understates
   noise. Sliced-init (+4.26pp) is ~2σ and survives; **no delta under ~2pp is readable at n=3.**
   Budget ≥5 seeds for all future seg cells. Method lesson: do not stamp CONFIRMED on n=1.

`logs/seg_step006_decor_paired__mini_mps.log`, `results/seg/seg_step001_B{0,1}_t0pair_seed4*__mini_mps.json`.

**seg_step007 E2→E7 soft-KD distillation — DONE (2026-08-14, 10 cells, 2097s, mini_mps, failures=none).**
Teacher E2 @60ep cached once (0.2650), students S1 (KD) / S0 (control) × seeds 42–46, α=0.5 T=2.0,
sigmoid-KD (multi-label head). Student = **0.8757 GMAC / 851,816 par — inside the 1 GMAC drone ceiling**.

1. **HYPOTHESIS — soft-KD is worth ~+1.7pp, NOT confirmed.** S1 0.2307 ±0.0151 vs S0 0.2141 ±0.0136;
   paired **+2.99 / +2.98 / +2.71 / +1.09 / −1.44 → mean +1.67 ±1.91pp**. The spread is the full ±2pp
   floor and one pair is negative. Settling it needs ~15 seeds or a bigger mechanism (higher α,
   feature-level term, stronger teacher). Gap to teacher remains −3.4pp.
2. **Method (second floor confirmation):** at n=3 this read as a clean +2.9pp; the last two seeds
   dropped it to +1.67 ±1.91. **Do not read a seg delta before all seeds are in.**
3. **CONFIRMED — new script is not a confound.** Cached teacher reproduces seg_step004 E2 s42 exactly
   and all three overlapping S0 controls bit-match their seg_step004 E7 twins (same mAP + best_ep).

`logs/seg_step007_distill__mini_mps.log`, `results/seg/seg_step007_S{0,1}_t0kd_seed4*__mini_mps.json`.

**seg_step008 KD strength sweep — DONE (2026-08-14, 10 cells, 1819s, mini_mps, failures=none).**
Same script, α ∈ {2.0, 8.0} × seeds 42–46, compared against the seg_step007 S0 controls (α=0 arm).

| α | student mAP (5 seeds) | paired vs same-seed S0 |
|---|---|---|
| 0.5 (step007) | 0.2307 ±0.0151 | +1.67 ±1.91 |
| 2.0 | 0.2285 ±0.0134 | +1.44 ±1.74 |
| 8.0 | 0.2292 ±0.0066 | +1.51 ±1.59 |
| 0 (S0 control) | 0.2141 ±0.0136 | — |

1. **CONFIRMED — the KD effect is FLAT in α over a 16× range.** 0.5 / 2.0 / 8.0 give +1.67 / +1.44 /
   +1.51pp, indistinguishable. The three arms are therefore *replicates*, not a sweep, and the
   "turn KD up until it clears the floor" plan is dead — there is no strength knob to turn.
2. **CONFIRMED — the noise is SEED-level, not run-level.** Seed 42 is good for KD at every α
   (+2.99/+2.21/+3.96), seed 46 bad at every α (−1.44/+0.37/−0.16). Averaging the 3 α replicates
   within a seed does not shrink the spread. **Only more seeds can settle a seg delta**; re-running
   the same seed is wasted compute. This sharpens seg_step006's ±2pp floor into a mechanism.
3. **HYPOTHESIS — soft-KD is worth ~+1.5pp.** Pooled 15 paired deltas: mean +1.54pp, 12/15 positive.
   Controls are shared across arms, so the honest n is 5: per-seed means +3.05 / +2.73 / +0.94 /
   +1.38 / −0.41 = **+1.54 ±1.41pp, t=2.45, df=4, p≈0.07**. Positive, not yet significant.

`logs/seg_step008_kd_alpha__mini_mps.log`, `results/seg/seg_step007_S1_a{2,8}_seed4*__mini_mps.json`.

**seg_step009 KD seed extension — DONE (mini_mps, 10 cells, 1798s, failures=none).** S1 α=2.0 + S0
controls, seeds 47–51, pooled with 42–46 for n=10 paired. Pre-registered prediction ("sem ~0.6pp →
p≈0.03") **met exactly**: sem 0.58, p≈0.029.

1. **CONFIRMED — soft-KD = +1.51pp.** Paired deltas +2.21 / +3.20 / −1.05 / +2.49 / +0.37 / +3.51 /
   +1.71 / −1.48 / +3.60 / +0.55; sd 1.84, sem 0.58, t=2.60, df=9, **p≈0.029**, 8/10 positive.
   S1 0.2276 ±0.0121 vs S0 0.2125 ±0.0162. Resolves the step007/008 HYPOTHESIS tags (both point
   estimates agree within 0.2pp). First significant effect in the seg line.
2. Cost of a +1.5pp claim on this task = 30 student cells (~95 min). Price of admission for every
   future seg lever of this size.
3. Pareto unchanged: 0.8757 GMAC / 851,816 par (inside 1 GMAC drone ceiling) at 0.2276 vs teacher
   3.2324 G / 2,195,656 par at 0.2650. KD recovers 1.5 of the 5.2pp deficit — halves, not closes.

`logs/seg_step009_kd_seeds__mini_mps.log`, `results/seg/seg_step007_S{0,1}_*_seed4[7-9]|5[01]*.json`.

**seg_step010 feature hint — DONE (mini_mps, 11 cells, 2979s, failures=none).** α is flat (step008),
so the logit channel is saturated; next orthogonal channel is the *features*. FitNets normally needs
a learned projection (student 128ch vs teacher 256ch) — here it does NOT, because E7 is built by
slicing E2's convs, so student channel j IS teacher channel j by construction. Hint =
`MSE(s_enc, t_enc[:, :128])`, β=1, zero extra params, no projection to confound it.

1. **CONFIRMED — hint = +3.67pp over logit KD** (n=10 paired seeds 42–51, sd 1.08, sem 0.34,
   t=10.77, df=9, **p<1e-5, 10/10 positive**); vs the no-KD control **+5.18pp** (sem 0.59, t=8.74).
   Largest effect in the seg line and the first to clear the ±2pp floor unaided.
2. **CONFIRMED — the student REACHES the teacher.** H1 0.2644 ±0.0056 vs teacher 0.2650, at 0.8757
   GMAC / 851,816 par: 3.7× fewer MACs, 2.6× fewer params, **zero extra student params** (the hint
   is a training-time loss term, so inference is byte-identical to S0). The 5.2pp deficit is closed,
   not halved. **This is the drone target: 1 GMAC-class student at teacher accuracy.**
3. **CONFIRMED — the hint kills the seed noise too:** sd ±0.0056 vs ±0.0121 (logit KD) / ±0.0162
   (none), 2.9× tighter. The ±2pp floor is a property of under-constrained training, not the task.
4. **CONFIRMED — no code-path confound:** H0 repro cell seed42 = 0.2181, bit-matches seg_step008 a2
   seed42 from the cached-logits path, so H1 pairs directly against the existing a2/S0 cells.
5. **HYPOTHESIS — alignment vs generic regularisation.** Discriminator = seg_step011 below.

Artifacts: `logs/seg_step010_feature_hint__mini_mps.log`,
`results/seg/seg_step010_H{0,1}_b*_seed4*|5[01]*__mini_mps.json`. Write-up:
`learnings/concepts/seg_distillation.md` (split out of `seg_coarse_heatmap.md` at the 200-line cap).

**seg_step011 hint mechanism — DONE (mini_mps, 15 cells, 4475s, failures=none).** Is the +3.67pp from *channel
alignment* (the sliced init making student channel j = teacher channel j) or is `MSE(features)` just
a generic regulariser? Arms, 5 seeds each: (a) H1 β=1 aligned [have, 10 seeds]; (b) **shuffled** —
hint against a fixed random permutation of the teacher's first 128 channels, identical statistics,
alignment destroyed; (c) β ∈ {0.25, 4} to check whether the effect is flat in β like α was.
Pre-registered: if shuffled ties H1, alignment is dead and the claim becomes "feature-MSE
regularises"; if shuffled falls back toward a2 (0.2276), alignment is the mechanism. Given the H1
sd of 0.0056, 5 seeds gives sem ≈0.25pp — enough to separate 0.2644 from 0.2276 many times over.

Result (all 15 cells, paired on seeds 42–46 vs β=1 aligned = 0.2649 ±0.0081):
1. **CONFIRMED — alignment is NOT the mechanism.** Shuffled 0.2596 ±0.0121 = **−0.53pp, sd 1.48,
   t=−0.80, 2/5 = NULL**, while shuffled−a2 = **+3.11pp** (sem 0.77, t=4.02, 5/5). Pre-registered
   "shuffled ties H1" branch fires; the seg_step010 alignment HYPOTHESIS is **falsified**. Result
   unchanged, explanation changed: matching the teacher's feature *distribution* is what constrains.
2. **CONFIRMED — flat in β over 16×.** β 0.25/1/4 = 0.2675 / 0.2649 / 0.2602, a 0.73pp spread, no
   trend. Second knobless mechanism in this line (α was the first, step008). No tuning needed.
3. **Robustness:** all four hint variants inside 0.2596–0.2675, every one +3.1–3.9pp over logit KD
   and +4.6–5.3pp over no-KD. Survives a 16× weight sweep AND deliberate target scrambling.
4. **Method:** pre-registration prevented re-reading the null as "alignment helps a little".
5. **Tooling lesson:** `--smoke_test` skips `fit()`, so it cannot validate loss-path edits — a
   `perm`/`ch_perm` name collision killed 5 cells in 17s. Verify loss changes with `--epochs 1`.
Write-up: `learnings/concepts/seg_feature_hint.md` (010+011 split out of `seg_distillation.md`).

**seg_step014 iterated distillation — DONE (mini_mps, 1 teacher run + 5 cells, failures=none).**
**CONFIRMED NULL — the hint saturates at the student's capacity; teacher quality is NOT the binding
constraint.** E7←E10+hint (teacher 0.2884) = 0.2659 ±0.0046 vs E7←E2 (teacher 0.2650) = 0.2649:
paired **+0.10pp, sd 0.59, sem 0.26, t=+0.38, 2/5** — a null. A teacher **+2.34pp better bought
+0.10pp**. Pre-registered branch (b) fires: no third round, iteration does not compound. Consequence
for the whole line: seg_step013's ≈1pp/octave is a **student-capacity law, not a supervision law** —
supervision is saturated, so **MACs are the only remaining lever at this student size**, and the
1 GMAC ceiling costs the confirmed 1.45pp. Caveat: the teacher checkpoint is E10 *seed 42* (0.2884),
E10's best seed vs its 0.2795 arm mean, so the teacher advantage was if anything overstated — which
only strengthens the null. Determinism check passed: the `--save_ckpt` run reported best_mAP=0.2884,
identical to step013's E10 seed42 cell, so the checkpoint is that exact model. Pre-registration below.

**seg_step014 pre-registration (kept for audit):** seg_step013 showed **the student beats its teacher** (E10 0.2795 vs E2 0.2650), so
the obvious question is whether that compounds: use the E10+hint student as the *teacher* for an E7
student at the fixed 0.8757G drone budget. This is the only lever identified so far that improves the
drone-budget model **without spending a single extra inference MAC** — teacher cost is training-time
only. Arms, 5 seeds: E7 student, β=1 hint, teacher = E10+hint checkpoint (0.2795); control is the
existing E7←E2 hint cells (0.2644, same seeds 42–46). Pre-registered: (a) if R2 beats 0.2644 by
≥1pp, **teacher quality is a live lever at fixed student cost** and iteration should be pushed to a
third round; (b) if it is null, **the hint saturates at the student's capacity** — supervision
quality is not the binding constraint, and seg_step013's ~1pp/octave is a *student-capacity* law
rather than a supervision law, which would make MACs the only remaining lever in this line.
Requires `--save_ckpt` (added to the driver, checkpoint save+reload validated with a real
`--epochs 1` run per the step011 lesson).

**seg_step020 — DONE (mini_mps, 10 cells, 2981s, failures=none). Literal pre-registered verdict:
branch (b). But the B clause decided it on noise — flagged for the user, NOT overturned.**
Floor (random init) bal 0.6122 / spec 0.9479. Per seed, dec05 vs dec00:
dS +0.1096 / +0.0542 / +0.1422 / +0.1419 / +0.1572 -> **mean +0.1210, sd 0.0412, t=+6.57, 5/5
positive**. dB +0.0482 / +0.0338 / -0.1303 / -0.0760 / +0.0540 -> **mean -0.0141, sd 0.0839,
t=-0.38, 3/5 positive**.
**The specialisation claim is CONFIRMED and is not marginal:** +0.121 is 2.4x the +0.05 threshold at
t=6.57 with every seed positive. **The balance clause is what fired branch (b)**, via a -0.0141
point estimate whose t is -0.38 — i.e. **indistinguishable from zero, not evidence of harm**. The
rule was written as `dB >= 0`, a sign test on a knife-edge, and a noisy null lands on the wrong side
of it half the time by construction. **That is a defect in my rule specification, found only after
seeing the data, so the rule as written STANDS and the recorded verdict is (b).** I am not
rewriting a pre-registered rule to reach the answer I now prefer. Flagged for the user to decide:
the disambiguating test is an **equivalence** check on B (is dB within a pre-set indifference band?)
rather than a sign test, which is what the clause meant — "decor must not kill a channel" — and
neither arm shows gate death (both ~0.7 balance, *above* the 0.61 random-init floor).
**Independent of the verdict, the K-channel design finding stands: training collapses
specialisation** from the 0.9479 random floor to 0.4750-0.5960 without decor, and decor holds it at
0.6232-0.7056. Two random projections start near-uncorrelated; supervision drives both channels
onto the same evidence. The penalty slows that, it does not create structure.

**seg_step020 K-channel explainability — the last open claim for anti-Hebbian decorrelation —
pre-registration retained below for audit.** Decorrelation is a **CONFIRMED NULL on mAP** (7 paired
seeds, `seg_coarse_heatmap.md`), and it was kept only on the explicit condition that *"the K-channel
explainability it was built for is measured and earns it — that is now the only open claim, and it
needs its own metric, not mAP."* This measures it. `seg_step010_feature_hint.py --arm H1` at the
frozen E7/H1 config, `--decor_lambda 0.05` vs `0.0`, seeds 42-46 paired, `--save_ckpt` with the new
`--ckpt_suffix` (the old ckpt name keyed only on arm/epochs/seed/slot, so a loss-term sweep would
have silently overwritten its own cells — fixed generally, empty default, `load_teacher()`
untouched). Analysis is offline in `scripts/seg/seg_step020_kchannel.py` so the metric can be
re-run on the same checkpoints without retraining.
**Metric, fixed before any checkpoint exists.** Per class c, over val cells the teacher marks
positive for c, using the K=2 pre-max channel logits:
- **balance** B_c = min(p, 1-p) x 2 where p = fraction of those cells whose argmax over K is
  channel 0. 1.0 = both channels used, 0.0 = one channel dead (the gate-death failure mode).
- **specialisation** S_c = 1 - |pearson(ch0, ch1)| over those cells. This is the actual claim:
  decorrelation exists to make the K channels encode *distinct* sub-patterns, so an operator can
  read "which sub-pattern fired" off the argmax. Report mean over the 4 classes, paired per seed.
- **floor control:** the same two numbers on an untrained random-init E7, so a high S is not read
  as structure when it is just noise being uncorrelated.
**Pre-registered rule:** (a) decor beats no-decor by **mean dS >= +0.05 AND t >= 2.78** (p<0.05,
df=4) **and does not reduce B** -> the mechanism earns its keep on explainability despite the mAP
null, and stays in the paper as an interpretability feature; (b) **anything else** -> decorrelation
is a **CONFIRMED NULL on both of its claims** and is dropped from the architecture. Note (b) is the
expected outcome given five prior nulls; the run exists to close the claim, not to rescue it, and
the threshold is set where it was set because an effect too small to see at n=5 is too small to sell
as an operator-facing feature.
**Floor measured BEFORE the checkpoints landed (smoke test, random-init E7, 32 val images):
balance 0.5579, spec 0.9533.** Recorded now so it cannot be claimed as hindsight. It matters for
reading the result: an untrained net is *already* 0.95 specialised, because two random projections
of the same features are near-uncorrelated by default. So there is only **0.047 of headroom above
the floor**, i.e. **less than the pre-registered +0.05 threshold** — under this metric branch (a) is
essentially unreachable *upward*. That does not invalidate the rule; it reframes what the run can
show. The informative direction is now **downward**: training normally *destroys* specialisation
(both channels converge on the same evidence), and the live question is whether decorrelation
**preserves** it. dS is still the right statistic and the rule stands as written; if both arms land
far below 0.95 the honest report is "training collapses the K channels and decor does/does not slow
it", which is a finding about the K-channel design itself, not only about the penalty.
**Dry run on the first completed pair (seed 42 only, `--tag dryrun`, run at 6/10 cells to validate
the script against real checkpoints): floor spec 0.9479, dec05 0.7056, dec00 0.5960 -> dS +0.1096,
dB +0.0482.** **NO VERDICT — n=1.** Recorded because it is exactly the downward pattern predicted
above and the prediction was logged first: training *does* collapse specialisation from the 0.95
random-init floor, and decorrelation appears to slow the collapse rather than raise S above floor.
If that holds over 5 pairs it clears the +0.05 threshold, so branch (a) is live after all — but the
seed-to-seed sd is completely unknown at n=1 and this line must not be cited until the sweep ends.
The dry run also caught a real defect: with sd=0 at n=1 the t-statistic is +inf and the script
printed a branch verdict off one seed. Fixed generally — it now refuses to name a branch below the
5 pairs the df=4 rule was registered at.

**seg_step021 — DONE (5060ti_cuda, free card, 6 arms, b1+b32). The step019 re-run. It OVERTURNS
step019's latency branch (a); the memory result stands bit-identical.** Same command, same arms,
`--tag step021_clean`, co-tenant idle at 804 MiB / **0% util** (step019 ran against 13006 MiB at
100%). Clean b1: E9 **0.376** / E7 **0.428** / E11 0.420 / E12 0.406 / E10 0.645 / E2 0.758 ms —
against step019's contaminated 2.553–3.012, so **the co-tenant inflated the fixed term ~6×, exactly
the bias direction the HYPOTHESIS tag flagged.** **E9/E7 = 0.878×, i.e. E9 is 12% FASTER than E7**,
failing the pre-registered ≥0.95× branch-(a) rule; E9's p90 (0.386) sits below E7's p10 (0.426), so
this is outside noise. Script verdict: b1 E2/E7 = 1.7708× = **48.0% of the 3.6912× MAC ratio ->
branch b, partially compute-bound**; b32 2.6236× = 71.1% -> branch b. Fit over the six clean points:
**0.129 ms/GMAC, intercept ≈0.342 ms — 80% of E7's b1 is fixed overhead** (63% on MPS). Consequences:
**"b1 flat below 1 GMAC" was an MPS measurement-resolution artifact, not a property of the ladder**,
and **step015's "E9 is Pareto-dominated by E7" does not hold on CUDA** — with E9's −1.69pp (step013)
it becomes an accuracy/latency trade that is live again for a drone at batch 1. **Memory is
bit-identical to step019** (4/12/10/12/19/27 MB), confirming the per-process claim made at the time.
Result `results/seg/seg_step015_step021_clean__5060ti_cuda.json`; write-up
`learnings/concepts/seg_latency_slot_dependence.md` (split out at the 200-line cap, with a retraction
pointer left in `seg_latency_pareto.md`). **Method lesson: a branch that fires under a known bias
pointing the same way is not evidence** — and note the b32 column was fine under contention (69% vs
71%), so contention corrupts the *fixed* term and batch-1 numbers are the fragile ones. Any future b1
timing must record card state alongside the result.

**seg_step024 encoder block family at iso-GMAC — RUNNING (mini_mps, launched 2026-08-17,
`scripts/seg/seg_step024_blocks.py --tag t0`, log `logs/seg/seg_step024_blocks_t0__mini_mps.log`).**
5 cells x 5 seeds x 60ep, ~2h. First time the ENCODER BLOCK has been varied in this line — E0-E12 are
all VGG-shaped dense 3x3, an inherited constraint, never a measured choice. Blocks live in the new
`scripts/seg/seg_blocks.py`; the driver swaps `SegNet.enc` rather than adding arms, so nothing in
`seg_encoders.py` changes.
**The init confound dictates the design.** The sliced ImageNet init is worth +4.26pp (seg_step003) and
no family here can inherit it (no VGG counterpart to a 7x7 depthwise kernel), so every cell is SCRATCH
and the paired base is `e8` = the lean architecture at random init — never `lean` itself. This answers
"best family at scratch" only; a winner still needs an init found for it.
Iso-GMAC not iso-width: each family's interior width is bisected onto e8's 0.8450G, so a cheap block
is spent on width instead of banked (comparing at iso-width is E4, already a LOSS). Cells landed at
+1.1 / +0.8 / -0.9 / +0.2% of budget — the residual is integer channel quantisation, not a solver bug.
Cells: `e8` (base, ties to seg_step022), `vgg` (same dense 3x3 in this harness, differing ONLY by a
residual on the c->c repeats, so vgg-e8 isolates the skip), `sep` (w=3.26), `ir` (MobileNetV2 inverted
residual, w=0.92), `cnx` (ConvNeXt 7x7 dw + 4x MLP, w=0.40). Held fixed: reps [2,2,3], stride 8,
encoder out PINNED at 128ch so the feature hint is identical across cells, `lean` pyramid, E2 teacher,
arm H1, 60ep, seeds 42-46. Bar +1.2pp over `e8`, same as seg_step022; cheaper-and-neutral = Pareto win.

**seg_step022/023 M_FPM pyramid — DONE (mini_mps, 2026-08-16, `scripts/seg/seg_step022_pyramid.py
--tag t0`, log `logs/seg/seg_step022_pyramid_t0__mini_mps.log`).** 8 cells x 5 seeds x 60ep, E2->E7, arm H1
(alpha=2.0 beta=1.0), 128px/16x16 held fixed. **Why now: seg_step014's CONFIRMED NULL** (a better teacher
buys nothing) **means the binding constraint is the student's CAPACITY**, so reallocating it inside the
1 GMAC ceiling is the lever — and the pyramid is the only part of the net that is a design choice rather
than a slice of VGG. 022 = branch ablation (`no_pool`/`no_d1`/`no_d4`/`no_d8`/`no_d16`); each drop frees
~111K params and 0.029 GMAC (0.8757 -> 0.8472G), and at a 16x16 grid a rate-16 kernel already spans the
whole map so `d16` is a candidate for pure cost. 023 = **true global context**: `M_FPM.pool` is a 1x1 conv
on a 3x3 max-pool, i.e. LOCAL despite its name, so there was no image-level vector anywhere in this net —
`gap_cat` adds one by GAP+concat for **+8,896 params and +0.0001 GMAC**. `gap_se` feeds the SAME vector in
multiplicatively and is a **pre-registered CONTROL, not a second bet**: gate-death (steps 873-916) and
GLAM T0 (mul hurts, add neutral) both predict cat >= se, and an se win falsifies both on a dense spatial
task. Bar **+1.2pp** (~2 sigma of the hint arm's +-0.56pp seed spread, seg_step011); a cell within +-1.2pp
at LOWER cost is scored WIN(pareto). Enabling refactor verified INERT twice — `seg_train.py` extraction
and the MFPM rewrite each reproduce seg_step010 bit-identically (best_mAP=0.1056 @ep1, same per-class AP,
same 851,816p / 0.8757G); MFPM state_dict keys unchanged so existing checkpoints still load.

**RESULT (40 runs, 3.4h).** Control VALID: `full` = 0.2649 ±0.0081, reproducing seg_step010's H1
(0.2644) and sitting at the E2 teacher (0.2650). Cells, Δpp vs full:
`no_pool` **+0.22** (842,920p / 0.8735G) · `no_d1` −0.41 (740,520p / 0.8472G) · `no_d16` −1.14 ·
`no_d8` −1.60 · `no_d4` **−2.09** · `gap_cat` −0.12 · `gap_se` +0.32 (±0.0121).
**No cell cleared the +1.2pp bar — 022 and 023 are both NULL for accuracy, and the whole answer is
on the cost axis.** (1) **`pool` is dead weight — CONFIRMED**: dropping the 1x1-on-maxpool branch is
+0.22pp at −8,896p, i.e. free in both directions; it never was a pyramid branch. (2) **`d1` is nearly
free — CONFIRMED Pareto win**: −0.41pp buys −111,296p (−13% of the student) and −0.0285 GMAC.
(3) **Ablation order is NOT monotonic in dilation rate** (d4 −2.09 > d8 −1.60 > d16 −1.14 > d1 −0.41):
the MIDDLE of the cascade carries the work, not the widest receptive field. HYPOTHESIS —
depth-of-cascade matters more than rate coverage; the clean test is a same-depth/different-rate cell
this design does not contain. (4) `no_d16` at −1.14pp is UNDER the bar, so the pre-registered "d16 is
pure cost at 16x16" prediction is **NOT supported** — scoring it WIN(pareto) would be reading the bar,
not the data. (5) Secondary HYPOTHESIS: the three LOSS cells halved the seed spread (±0.0040-0.0048 vs
±0.0081), consistent with losing a degree of freedom rather than losing signal at random.
**seg_step023 — TRUE GLOBAL CONTEXT IS A NULL, both ways.** `gap_cat` −0.12pp and `gap_se` +0.32pp are
both inside noise. The gate-death theorem and GLAM T0 predicted cat >= se; observed se >= cat by
+0.44pp, which is well inside `gap_se`'s own ±0.0121 spread (the widest in the run), so **neither the
prediction nor its falsification is supported — this is an underpowered tie, not a result.** What IS
CONFIRMED: at a 16x16 grid an image-level vector adds nothing for 8,896p, in either polarity. Most
economical reading (HYPOTHESIS): the d4->d8->d16 cascade already spans the map, so a GAP vector is
redundant rather than useless in principle.
**Actionable: a `no_pool + no_d1` student is the new default candidate at 731,624p / 0.8450G, ~14%
params and 3.5% MACs off E7 for ~0.2pp.** MEASURED in t1 (2026-08-17, 10 runs): `lean` = 0.2618
±0.0056 vs `full` 0.2649 ±0.0081, **-0.31pp at 731,624p / 0.8450G — WIN(pareto)**. The Compounding
Rule check passes: naive sum of the two t0 deltas is -0.19pp (no_pool +0.22, no_d1 -0.41), observed
-0.31pp, a 0.12pp gap well inside either cell's seed spread. **CONFIRMED: `pool` and `d1` drops are
additive; lean is adopted as the new default student.** (The t1 process finished all 10 runs but its
JSON write died with EPERM during the volume fault; the file was rebuilt verbatim from the log —
`elapsed_s` and per-class finals are unrecoverable, noted in the file.)
Results `results/seg/seg_step022_t0__mini_mps.json` + `..._t1__mini_mps.json`;
write-up `learnings/concepts/seg_mfpm_pyramid.md`
(split out of `seg_encoder_levers.md` at the 200-line cap, pointer left behind).

**seg_step019 — DONE (5060ti_cuda, 6 arms, b1+b32). Memory branch (b); latency branch (a) —
RETRACTED by seg_step021 above, which re-ran it on a free card.**
b1 peak mem: E9 4 / **E7 12** / E11 10 / **E12 12** / E10 19 / E2 27 MB. **E12/E7 = 1.00x -> branch
(b): memory is flat to allocation too.** With latency (step016) and accuracy (step017/018) already
flat, **the seg ladder has no architectural headroom left below 1 GMAC** — only the budget moves any
metric. Also **falsifies the MPS 53-vs-45 MB hint** that E12 was heavier: that was the driver pool,
as declared. b1 latency: E9 2.553 / E7 2.670 / E11 2.662 / E12 2.644 / E10 2.893 / E2 3.012 ms ->
**E9/E7 = 0.956x, branch (a)**: the flat-below-1-GMAC floor generalises to CUDA, and the cpu-derived
branch-(b) prior was wrong (E12 is 1.01x E7 here, not 1.32x faster). **Caveat, and it is why this is
tagged HYPOTHESIS not CONFIRMED: PID 204307 was co-resident, contention inflates the fixed term, and
that biases toward the branch that fired.** Memory is unaffected (per-process counter). Needs one
re-run on a free GPU. b32 is the clean half: 0.233/0.511/0.492/0.509/0.916/1.307 ms/frame, E2/E7 =
2.56x vs 3.69x MACs (69%, matching MPS's 74%) — **at batch 32 MACs do buy throughput on both slots.**
Ran via `--no_pretrained` (new bench-only flag) on `~/solarflare/venv/bin/python`; the shared
`~/sgnnet_bench/venv` has a torch-cu128 / torchvision-cu130 mismatch and was **not** touched.

**seg_step019 pre-registration (retained for audit) — was QUEUED (5060ti_cuda, ~3 min).**
Script `scripts/seg/seg_step015_latency.py` with a new bench-only `--no_pretrained` flag (sliced/pre
arms rebuilt as `shaped` at IDENTICAL shapes — GMAC verified bit-equal locally: E7 0.8757, E11
0.8461, E12 0.8164 — because weight *values* cannot change latency or peak memory; never for a
training script). The 5060ti has no cached vgg16 checkpoint and this avoids a 553 MB copy. Arms
`E9,E7,E11,E12,E10,E2` at b1/b32. Two things are being closed at once, both keyed on the **cheapest**
arm per the step015 method lesson, and both fixed **before the run**:
- **(1) MEMORY — the last unmeasured Pareto column below 1 GMAC.** MPS reports a driver-wide pool
  (all arms read 45–53 MB, declared *not a result*); CUDA `max_memory_allocated` is per-process, so
  it is valid even with the teammate's PID 204307 co-resident. Rule: **(a)** E12 peak ≥ 1.10× E7 ->
  memory breaks the E7/E11/E12 tie the same way params did, E7 wins the operating point on a third
  axis; **(b)** within ±10% -> **memory is flat to allocation too**, and with latency (step016) and
  accuracy (step017/018) already flat, the seg ladder has **no architectural headroom left below
  1 GMAC** — only the budget itself moves any metric.
- **(2) LATENCY — tests `seg_latency_pareto.md`'s standing HYPOTHESIS** that the dispatch-bound /
  flat-below-1-GMAC finding is *stronger* on faster silicon. Rule: **(a)** E9 (0.26 G) ≥ 0.95× E7's
  b1 ms -> the floor generalises, CONFIRMED cross-slot, MAC reduction below 1 G buys no time on any
  slot; **(b)** E9 ≤ 0.85× E7 -> the floor is **MPS-specific**, MACs do buy time on CUDA, and the
  drone conclusion becomes slot-dependent — which would matter, since the drone will not ship on an
  M-series GPU; **(c)** 0.85–0.95× -> inconclusive, report as such.
**Prior evidence that (b) is live, recorded before the run so it is not hindsight:** the local
shape-check pass above ran on **cpu** and there E12 is **1.32× FASTER** than E7 (2.876 vs 3.795 ms),
the exact opposite of the MPS ordering (E12 1.065× slower). Conv count already behaves differently
off MPS, so branch (b) is the honest prior, not (a). Joules stay blocked regardless — power
telemetry is device-wide and would read the teammate's job.

**seg_step018 E12 seed extension — DONE (mini_mps, 5 cells, 1567s, failures=none). Branch (b).**
**CONFIRMED NULL at n=10: mean +0.38pp, sd 0.67, t=+1.79, 7/10 positive** — under the +0.5pp bar and
well under the t>=2.26 the rule required. E7 0.2644 vs E12 0.2682 over the same 10 seeds. Extending
the sample **halved the effect** (+0.55 -> +0.38pp) while barely moving t (+1.59 -> +1.79), which is
what regression to the mean looks like: the n=5 read was a favourable draw, not a small true effect
being under-powered. Had branch (a) been called on the n=5 mean, this line would now carry a
"width beats depth at iso-cost" claim its own extension does not support.
**The result, stated positively: accuracy-per-MAC at ~0.9 GMAC is FLAT TO ARCHITECTURE.** Combined
with E11 (-0.31pp, n=5), 3 / 4 / 7 convs at matched MACs all land inside the post-hint noise floor.
VGG's depth is not load-bearing *and* width does not substitute for it — the budget, not its
allocation, is what sets accuracy here. **E7 remains the operating point** and now wins on the
tiebreak it always had: same accuracy, same b1 latency, **0.85M params vs E12's 1.09M (-22%)**.
**Consequence for the seg line:** with latency retired below 1 GMAC (step016) and now allocation
retired at ~0.9 G (step017/018), the ladder is at a genuine ceiling on every axis measured so far.
The remaining headroom is **memory and Joules**, both still unmeasured and both blocked on the 5060ti.
Results: `results/seg/seg_step010_H1_a_e12_seed4[7-9]__mini_mps.json`, `..._seed5[01]__...`.

**seg_step018 pre-registration (retained for audit) — QUEUED (mini_mps, 5 seeds).**
seg_step017 came back with E11 a clean branch-(b) null (mean **-0.31pp**, sd 1.20, t=-0.57, 2/5
positive) but E12 **on the line**: mean **+0.55pp** (sd 0.77, **t=+1.59**, 4/5 positive, deltas
+1.06/+0.17/+1.61/+0.15/-0.26). That crosses the pre-registered +0.5pp branch-(a) threshold while
**failing significance at n=5** (t=1.59 vs the 2.78 needed for p<0.05 at df=4). Declaring branch (a)
off a mean that its own spread does not support would be exactly the post-hoc move pre-registration
exists to prevent, so the call is **deferred, not made**, and the sample is extended instead. E7's
hint baseline **already has seeds 47-51**, so only E12 needs running — the pairing stays exact and
the extension costs 5 cells, not 10. **No re-analysis of 42-46 alone will be reported as a verdict.**
**Pre-registered rule at the full n=10** (fixed now, before any of 47-51 exists): (a) **mean >=
+0.5pp AND t >= 2.26** (p<0.05, df=9) -> depth is NOT load-bearing, width reallocation wins at
iso-cost, operating point moves off E7 — **and it is still bought with +30% params (1.09M vs 0.85M)
and must be reported on the Pareto table, not as a free lunch**; (b) **anything else** -> null,
allocation at ~0.9G is flat to architecture, E7 stays the operating point and the seg line's
remaining headroom is only memory/Joules. Note the asymmetry is deliberate: the marginal n=5 result
is currently *favourable*, so the burden is put on the favourable branch.

**seg_step017 depth-vs-width at iso-latency — DONE (mini_mps, 10 cells, 3103s, failures=none).**
**E11 = branch (b), CONFIRMED null.** Paired vs E7 on the same seeds 42-46: -1.06 / -1.88 / +0.98 /
-0.31 / +0.73 -> mean **-0.31pp**, sd 1.20, **t=-0.57**, 2/5 positive. Inside the +/-0.56pp post-hint
noise floor, so 4 convs at w0.75 buys nothing on accuracy — and it carries **+30% params** (1.11M vs
0.85M) at the same latency, so E11 is not tied with E7, it is **Pareto-dominated**.
**E12 = deferred to seg_step018, NOT called.** Mean +0.55pp but t=+1.59 at n=5; see the block above.
Reported here so the number is on record without a verdict attached to it.
Results: `results/seg/seg_step010_H1_a_e11_seed4*__mini_mps.json`, `..._a_e12_seed4*...`.

**seg_step017 pre-registration (retained for audit) — QUEUED (mini_mps, H1 hint, 5 seeds x 2 arms).**
seg_step016 retired latency as a selection criterion below 1 GMAC (everything from 0.26G to 0.88G
lands in 0.81-0.96 ms at b1), which **removes the reason E11/E12 were blocked from accuracy runs**
and simultaneously makes them the only free sample points on the one surviving axis: **accuracy at
iso-cost**. E7 / E11 / E12 are within 8% on MACs and within 6.5% on b1 latency but allocate that
budget completely differently — 7 convs at w0.5 vs 4 at w0.75 vs 3 at w1.0 — so this asks whether
VGG's depth is load-bearing or whether the same cost buys more accuracy as width. Config is
seg_step010's H1 exactly (hint beta=1, logit KD alpha=2, T=2, 60ep, sliced init, E2 teacher), so the
**E7 baseline is the existing 0.2644 +/-0.0056 on seeds 42-46** and only E11/E12 need running; paired
per seed. Hint path validated on E11 with a real 1-epoch run (student 192ch vs teacher 256ch slice
works, artifact deleted) per the step011 lesson that --smoke_test misses the real path.
**Pre-registered decision rule** on the better of E11/E12 vs E7, using the post-hint noise floor
(+/-0.56pp, so +/-0.5pp is readable at n=5): (a) **>= +0.5pp** -> depth is NOT load-bearing, width
reallocation wins at iso-cost, and the operating point moves off E7; (b) **within +/-0.5pp** ->
allocation is neutral and **accuracy-per-MAC at ~0.9G is flat to architecture**, which would mean the
ladder is at a genuine ceiling and the seg line's remaining headroom is only memory/Joules;
(c) **both <= -0.5pp** -> VGG's 7-conv depth IS load-bearing and cannot be traded for width.
**Stated confound, not discovered later:** E11/E12 carry ~1.11M/1.09M params against E7's 0.85M, so a
branch-(a) win is bought with **+30% params** and must be reported on the Pareto table, not as a free
lunch. Branch (b) is the likeliest and the least interesting, which is why the rule is written before
the numbers.

**seg_step016 dispatch-count lever — DONE (mini_mps, 6 cells, 200 reps each, failures=none).**
**Pre-registered branch (c) fires — the prediction is FALSIFIED, and it was this project's own
prediction.** At iso-MAC (within 8%) inside the same 3-block stride-8 stack, b1 ms/frame: **E7
(7 convs, 0.8757G) 0.855**, E11 (4 convs, 0.8461G) 0.871 (**1.018x**), E12 (3 convs, 0.8164G) 0.910
(**1.065x SLOWER**). The rule needed E12 <= 0.65 ms to confirm and called >= 0.79 ms a falsification.
Cutting kernel launches 2.3x bought nothing and cost 6.5%. **CONFIRMED: step015's 0.51 ms fixed term
is per-MODEL, not per-launch** — it lives in the Python/dispatch entry and MPS graph submission for
the whole forward, so no fusing or thinning of convs can reach it; the step015 fit stands, its
per-launch *interpretation* was an unlabelled HYPOTHESIS stated too confidently and is retracted in
`seg_latency_pareto.md`. **Larger CONFIRMED result: b1 latency is architecture-independent below
~1 GMAC on this slot** — pooling both runs, every arm from 0.26G to 0.88G lands in **0.81-0.96 ms**
(E9 0.958, E7 0.810/0.855, E11 0.871, E12 0.910), a 3.3x MAC range and 2.3x conv-count range
compressed into 1.18x of time; only E10 (1.87G, 1.209) and E2 (3.23G, 1.617) climb out. **There is a
~0.85 ms floor and E7 sits on it**, now confirmed from two directions: narrower is slower (E9),
fatter-and-shallower is slower (E11/E12). **Consequence: below ~1 GMAC, further MAC reduction buys
no wall-time at all** — both proxies this line has optimised (MACs, then dispatch count) are
exhausted for latency, and the remaining levers are accuracy-per-MAC at ~0.9G plus the still-unmeasured
memory/Joules. E12's 53 MB vs 45 MB read is NOT a memory result (MPS counter is a driver-wide pool).
No accuracy runs launched for E11/E12, per the rule. Method: keying the rule on the extreme arm (the
step015 lesson) worked — refuted a claim this project authored for the price of one 2-minute latency
run instead of five training seeds. Original pre-registration below.

**seg_step016 pre-registration (kept for audit) — QUEUED (mini_mps, latency-only first).** Tests the prediction
`seg_latency_pareto.md` went **on record** for: with 63% of E7's batch-1 latency being fixed
per-launch cost, **dispatch count and not width is the remaining lever**, so fewer/fatter kernels at
iso-MAC should beat E7 — the opposite of what the MAC objective recommends. Two new arms trade depth
for width inside the same 3-block, stride-8 stack (head and 16×16 grid untouched, only the encoder
differs): **E11** = w0.75 reps [1,1,2] → **4 convs, 0.8461 GMAC**; **E12** = w1.0 reps [1,1,1] →
**3 convs, 0.8164 GMAC**; both against **E7** = w0.5 reps [2,2,3] → **7 convs, 0.8757 GMAC**. All
three within 8% on MACs, so conv COUNT is the only variable that moves much. Latency is measured
*first*, with `seg_step015_latency.py --arms E7,E11,E12` — it is the cheap falsification, and there
is no reason to spend training time on an accuracy run for an arm that is not faster.
**Pre-registered decision rule, keyed on E12 (the fewest-conv arm)** per the step015 method lesson
that a fixed-cost effect shows up first on the extreme arm, not the middling pair. E7 = 0.810 ms at
b1 with 0.51 ms fixed; if that fixed cost is genuinely per-launch it is ~0.073 ms/conv, so 3 convs
should cost ~0.22 ms fixed → predicted E12 ≈ 0.52 ms, ~1.55×. Branches on measured E12 b1 ms/frame:
(a) **≤ 0.65 ms** (≥1.25× faster than E7) → **prediction CONFIRMED**, dispatch count is the lever,
and the seg line should optimise launches rather than MACs from here; (b) **0.65–0.79 ms** → partial,
the fixed cost is only partly per-conv; (c) **≥ 0.79 ms** (within 4% of E7, or slower) →
**prediction FALSIFIED** — the 0.51 ms is per-*model* overhead (Python/dispatch entry, MPS graph
submission), not per-conv, no reordering of convs can reach it, and `seg_latency_pareto.md`'s
on-record claim must be retracted in that file. Branch (c) is the one written to hurt us.
Prerequisite DONE: the `reps` kwarg forced `build_vgg_sliced` to pair source and destination convs
**by block** instead of positionally — verified that E7/E9/E10 rebuild **bit-exactly** against the
old positional path, so no prior seg result is disturbed. Correction on the way in: the arms were
hand-estimated at 0.7007G/0.632G encoder MACs; measured is 0.739G/0.709G — the estimate under-counted
block 3, and the measured numbers are what the rule above uses.

**seg_step015 latency Pareto — DONE (mini_mps, 8 cells, 200 reps each, failures=none).** Pre-registered
**branch (b) fires**: latency(E2)/latency(E7) = **1.99× at b1 / 2.72× at b32** vs a 3.6912× MAC ratio
(54% / 74%) — **partially compute-bound, the 1 GMAC ceiling is directionally right but overstated**.
b1 ms/frame: E9 0.958, **E7 0.810**, E10 1.209, E2 1.617 (p10/p90 within ±5%). Two CONFIRMED results:
(i) fitting `latency = fixed + slope·MACs` over E7/E10/E2 gives **fixed = 0.51 ms = 63% of E7's
batch-1 latency** (36% at b32) — the seg-line instance of step608's dispatch-bound finding, worst in
the drone's own batch-1 regime; (ii) **E9 is Pareto-dominated by E7** — 3.32× fewer MACs yet **1.18×
SLOWER** at b1, sitting 0.36 ms *above* the fitted line, and step013 already had it at −1.69pp. First
outright Pareto kill in the seg line, invisible to the MAC proxy that called E9 the cheapest arm.
Bounds step013 rather than overturning it: **E7 is the wall-time floor of this ladder at b1**. Next
lever is **dispatch count, not width** (fewer/fatter kernels) — the opposite of what MACs recommend.
NOT measured, flagged so it is not mistaken for a result: **memory** (MPS `driver_allocated_memory()`
is a driver-wide pool — every arm read an identical 53 MB / 1119 MB, so the column is unmeasured on
this slot) and **Joules** (needs nvidia-smi telemetry, 5060ti blocked by teammate job). Method lesson:
the rule keyed on E2/E7 and fired the middling branch, while the real finding came from E9, an arm the
rule never named — **key latency rules on the cheapest arm**, where a fixed-cost floor shows first.
Write-up: `learnings/concepts/seg_latency_pareto.md`. Original pre-registration below.

**seg_step015 pre-registration (kept for audit):** Every seg result so far is mAP + MACs. MACs are a **proxy**, and
bench_step608 already CONFIRMED this codebase can be dispatch-bound rather than FLOP-bound, so
"0.8757 GMAC fits the drone ceiling" does not establish that the model is fast or small in RAM.
Measures the arm ladder (E9 / E7 / E10 / E2) on wall-time per frame (**batch 1** — a drone runs one
frame at a time — and batch 32) plus peak memory and params, random weights (architecture alone sets
latency; no training needed). Joules deferred: it needs `nvidia-smi` power telemetry, so it is
5060ti-only and currently blocked. **Pre-registered decision rule**, keyed on measured
latency(E2)/latency(E7) against the MAC ratio **3.6917×**: (a) ratio ≥ 3.0× → MACs predict latency,
the 1 GMAC ceiling is a sound objective and seg_step013's law transfers to real time; (b) ratio
1.5–3.0× → partially compute-bound, the ceiling is directionally right but overstated;
(c) ratio ≤ 1.5× → **MACs do not predict latency on this stack**, the drone objective has been the
wrong one, and the whole seg line needs re-reading against wall-time. Branch (c) is the one written
to hurt us. Reported as the full Pareto table the project charter requires, not a single number.

**seg_step013 slice-ratio sweep WITH the hint — DONE (mini_mps, 10 cells, failures=none).**
Paired vs E7+hint (0.2644) on seeds 42–46: **E9** (w0.25, 0.2636G, 375K par) = 0.2480 ±0.0096,
**−1.69pp** (sd 0.69, t=−5.49, 0/5); **E10** (w0.75, 1.8653G, 1.55M par) = 0.2795 ±0.0053,
**+1.45pp** (sd 1.02, t=+3.19, 5/5). Verdicts: (i) neither E9 branch fires cleanly — −1.69pp is
between the "≤1pp" and ">2pp" thresholds, so the honest read is **width is NOT free under the hint**
(contrast seg_step002, where 0.5× width was free without it); (ii) branch (c) fires and it is the
one written to hurt us — **CONFIRMED the 1 GMAC drone ceiling is binding, worth ~1.45pp**;
(iii) three points give **≈1.0–1.4pp per octave of MACs** (0.95 E9→E7, 1.38 E7→E10) — a shallow
straight line with **no knee**, so E7 is not a sweet spot, just a budget choice; (iv) E10 **beats the
teacher** (0.2650) by +1.45pp at 1.87G vs the teacher's 3.23G. Original pre-registration below.

**seg_step013 pre-registration (kept for audit):** The hint is now confirmed knobless (step011) and architecture-general (step012), so the
remaining drone lever is width. Arms, β=1 hint, 5 seeds each: **E9** = sliced width 0.25, 3 blocks
(**0.2636 GMAC** — 3.3× under E7, 3.8× under the 1G ceiling) and **E10** = sliced width 0.75
(1.8653 GMAC, *over* the ceiling). E7 (0.8757G, 0.2644 mAP) is the incumbent middle point.
Pre-registered: (a) if E9 lands within ~1pp of E7, **the ceiling is not binding and the drone budget
should be cut to ~0.26G** — width is nearly free under the hint, mirroring seg_step002's finding that
0.5× width was free without it; (b) if E9 drops >2pp, width is the first lever the hint cannot
rescue and E7 is at the knee; (c) E10 exists only to bound the top — if it fails to beat E7 by >1pp,
**the 1G ceiling costs nothing** and no future work should trade MACs for accuracy in this line.
Note (c) is falsifiable in the direction that would hurt us: a big E10 win means the drone budget is
the binding constraint on this whole approach.

**seg_step012 hint without the sliced init — DONE (mini_mps, 10 cells, failures=none).**
**CONFIRMED: the hint is architecture-general — the sliced init is NOT load-bearing for it.** New arm
E8 = E7's exact architecture, random init. Paired on seeds 42–46: hint 0.2551 ±0.0046 vs logit-KD
0.2056 ±0.0068 → **+4.95pp, sd 0.61, sem 0.27, t=18.2, 5/5 positive**. The pre-registered first
branch fires (Δ same sign and magnitude class as the +3.67pp sliced result; in fact 1.28pp *larger*,
marginally outside the ±1pp band on the "bigger" side, nowhere near the zero-collapse branch). Two
corollaries: (i) a random-init student + hint (0.2551) beats a *sliced* student on logit KD alone
(0.2285) by +2.66pp — the hint is worth more than the ImageNet init it was supposed to depend on;
(ii) sliced + hint (0.2644) still edges random + hint (0.2551) by ~0.9pp, so the init and the hint
are additive, not redundant. Original pre-registration below.
 Direct consequence of the step011 shuffled null: if alignment is irrelevant, the
hint must also work on a student whose channels were **never** the teacher's — i.e. a *randomly*
initialised student. That is the difference between a trick specific to our sliced init and a
transferable method, and it is the claim the paper would actually make. Arms, 5 seeds: (a) E1-style
random-init student, logit KD only; (b) same + β=1 hint on the first 128 teacher channels. Both
arms need the same random init, so the only variable is the hint. Pre-registered: if (b)−(a) lands
within ~1pp of the +3.67pp measured on the sliced student, **the hint is architecture-general and
the sliced init is not load-bearing for it**; if (b)−(a) collapses toward zero, the sliced init is
required after all and step011's shuffled null only means alignment survives permutation *within* an
already-sliced student (a much weaker claim). Note this is a genuinely different question from
step011: shuffling permutes channels that still came from the teacher; random init removes the
teacher from the student's weights entirely.

**GLOBAL TERMINUS (2026-08-14, CONFIRMED): locality is the whole layer.** 2 datasets × 8 mechanisms mapped
— every non-locality lever (KV-mul, additive/bilinear/pre merge, selectivity, d_out, M, low-rank readout,
depth L=2, slope-anneal) neutral-or-negative. Significant param-efficient improvement = LOC champion itself
(Imagenette 96.96% @ 47K = 0.04% FC, Pareto-dominant). No cheap lever closes high-C gap. Full map:
`concepts/glam_results_scale_capacity.md` "GLOBAL TERMINUS". Direction CLOSED; next = deferred large-dataset
campaign only (carry d_out frontier), NOT more locality-side sweeps.

Readout slot transfers to detection.** bbox_mse 0.022→0.104 as params shrink (regression arm more sensitive).

B=1000 us/roi: dense 5.26 (14.36M) | lowrank_r256 1.79 / 2.94× (3.39M) | sgn_r128k32 1.94 / 2.70× (1.68M) | sgn_r64k16 1.61 / 3.26× (0.84M). **CONFIRMED (1): param cut converts to wall-time — NOT dispatch-bound at B=1000 (3.26× faster @ 17× fewer params). (2): top-k is a wall-time LIABILITY — sgn_r128k32 SLOWER than lowrank_r256 despite ½ params (top-k sort cost > rank-halving matmul saving; dense matmul ignores mask → MACs track rank not k). (3): wall-time gain (2.7–3.3×) lags MAC/param gain (8.5–17×) — sublinear.** Verdict: dense low-rank Pareto-dominates top-k on wall-time; top-k only pays accuracy(+det_step002)/param, not speed. Drone readout = dense low-rank unless sparse kernel available.

Accuracy tracks retained entropy; user's argmax_bucket most destructive.

SNR story validated at the extreme-low-capacity end and bounded there.**
- **data_step002 (seam-carve cost-benefit): DONE (T0).** At equal 576-px budget, content-aware seam carving LOSES to uniform resize: seam24=0.635 vs resize24=0.643 (−0.79pp), + 0.23ms/img DP cost + 10.75KB/img reclaim record (inverse map exact=True). seam_hi (784px) −0.40pp vs baseline. **Seam carving = net compute loss + no accuracy gain at equal budget (CIFAR proxy; direction, not absolute). Reclaim-record idea sound but carving doesn't earn its cost.** Uniform/foveated resize dominates for real-time.

By role: TRANSFORM_TEXT 41.39% | TRANSFORM_VISION 33.15% | EMBEDDING 11.60% | READOUT_LMHEAD 11.07% | READOUT_PROJECTOR 2.76% | NORM 0.03%. **SGNNET-addressable (readout) share = 13.83% → whole-model prune ceiling 1.118×/1.139×/1.150× at det_step003's MEASURED ratios (lowrank_r256 / topk_r128k32 / topk_r64k16). CONFIRMED: the head-replacement recipe that won 72% of the detector wins ≤13% here — readout-only VLM pruning is Amdahl-dead.** Same probe named the real lever: **1 image = 1135 tokens vs 4 text tokens** → VLM cost is TOKEN-bound, not param-bound. Redirect: move top-k off the WEIGHT axis (det_step003 liability) onto the TOKEN axis, where dropping an item genuinely removes work. → vlm_step002.

  **CONFIRMED — magnitude top-k beats stride at an identical 64-token budget: 0.440 vs 0.260 top-1 (+18pp), KL 1.96 vs 3.11.** *Which* image tokens survive matters, not just how many → the SGNNET selection rule transfers to the token axis. **CONFIRMED — token pruning alone is Amdahl-capped at ~1.13×:** prefill is only 13% of per-image latency (147/1125 ms); the vision encoder (978 ms, 87%) is untouched by dropping post-connector tokens. Cutting tokens 832→64 cuts prefill 7× and total latency 12%. **CONFIRMED — `nosplit_64` dominates: 11.71× total speedup AND +8pp top-1 over the full reference (0.740 vs 0.660), at KL 0.604 — better fidelity than topk_64 at the same budget.** Attacking tile count hits both stages (vision 978→78 ms) where token pruning hits one. HYPOTHESIS (untested): the accuracy *gain* is because 13-tile splitting feeds a 10-way global-object task with redundant high-res crops it cannot use; expect the sign to flip on tasks needing fine detail (OCR, small-object VQA). → vlm_step003 attacks the vision tower, the 87% that token pruning cannot reach.

- **vlm_step003 (vision-encoder depth): DONE (mini_mps, T0, n=50).** step002 relocated the target: the vision tower is **87% of latency and 33.15% of params**, and token pruning cannot touch it. SmolVLM-256M's tower = 12 SigLIP layers × 7,087,872 params, so **dropping 6 removes 42.5M = 16.6% of the whole model — larger than the entire readout share (13.83%) that vlm_step001 declared Amdahl-dead.** Legitimate here where SGNNET substitution is not: not asking a sparse head to imitate a TRANSFORM block (step989/llm_step002 KILLED that), only whether the block is needed. Connector consumes hidden_size=768, which every intermediate layer emits, so layer L feeds it unchanged. Arms on the nosplit champion (ref = d12_full, 64 tok): d9/d6/d3 truncate the tower | d12_topk{32,16} token axis | **d6_topk32 COMPOSES both** (Compounding Rule: depth and sequence length are different signal paths — this arm tests the composition rather than assuming it).   **CONFIRMED — zero-shot vision-tower truncation collapses to chance at every depth tested** (0.080 / 0.120 / 0.100 vs chance 0.100; KL 9.97–12.46; `same` 0.08–0.18; `agree` 0.000). Removing even 3 of 12 layers is fatal. The tower's **params are removable (16.6–24.9% of the model) but its function is not free** — post-layernorm + connector receive an unadapted feature distribution. **CONFIRMED — sharp axis asymmetry: the token axis degrades gracefully where the depth axis does not.** Halving tokens 64→32 costs 6pp at KL 0.138; quartering to 16 costs 14pp at KL 0.464; removing a quarter of the tower costs 62pp at KL 10.7. Cutting *what the tower already computed* is survivable; cutting *the computing* is not. **HYPOTHESIS → the truncated tower needs distillation, not deletion** — the step605/det_step002 recipe applied to a TRANSFORM stack's OUTPUT (not its internals, which step989/llm_step002 KILLED). → vlm_step004.

  **Two honest caveats.** (1) `d6_topk32` is **CONFOUNDED, not a compounding test** — d6 alone is already at chance, so this arm cannot say whether depth and token cuts compound; the Compounding Rule question stays OPEN until a working truncated tower exists. (2) On the nosplit champion the vision tower is 66.4/81.0 ms = **82% of latency**, so the token axis now buys only 1.02–1.04× wall-time. **Its value on this config is KV-cache/memory, not speed** — do not quote step002's 1.13× here; that figure was against the 832-token split reference.

- **vlm_step004 (vision-tower distillation): DONE — d6 / d9 / d3 at T0 (mini_mps, n_train=2000, 10ep, bs=8, lr=2e-4) plus long-budget d6 (n_train=6000, 15ep) which CONFIRMED the budget HYPOTHESIS at top-1 0.680 (gap to teacher 18pp → 6pp).** The gate vlm_step003 named. Freeze the full 12-layer SigLIP tower as TEACHER; train a 6-layer STUDENT initialised from its first 6 layers to match the teacher's **post-connector** features (relative MSE, cosine reported). Patch embeddings, post_layernorm, connector and the whole text model stay frozen — the student is the only thing that moves, so an accuracy change is attributable to it. **This does not contradict step989/llm_step002:** those KILLED replacing a TRANSFORM block's *internals* with a sparse head; here the student is the same architecture, only shorter, asked to reproduce the stack's *OUTPUT*. Different claim → needs its own evidence. Arms `teacher_d12` / `naive_d6` / `distil_d6` on the **same 50 val images** and the same constrained 10-way choice, so top-1 is directly comparable to 0.740 and 0.120. Script `scripts/frontier/vlm_step004_tower_distill.py`; shared protocol extracted to `scripts/frontier/vlm_eval.py` (step002/003 keep inline copies so DONE runs stay reproducible as run). Smoke test (cpu, 30 img, 1 ep) already moved KL 11.65 → 6.72, so the signal exists. **Decision rule:** if distillation cannot lift d6 above chance, the 16.58% param saving is unreachable and the tower stays whole. Runs are serial (studio unreachable; 5060ti venv has transformers 4.44 — no Idefics3, no `DynamicCache.crop`; mini_cpu would contend for cores and wall-time is a reported metric).
