  Train curve monotone, **not converged at ep10**: rel_mse 0.6092→0.2573, cosine 0.7598→0.8634 (ep1→ep10, still falling).

  **CONFIRMED — the step605/det_step002 distillation recipe recovers a truncated VLM vision tower.** Same 50 val images, same constrained 10-way choice, so directly comparable to vlm_step003: **top-1 0.120 → 0.560 (+44pp), KL 10.669 → 2.512 (−76%), `agree` 0.000 → 0.420**, at an unchanged 16.58% param saving and 1.62× latency. The decision rule is cleared: d6 is far above chance, so the 42.5M param cut is reachable. **This is the first evidence in the whole directive that a TRANSFORM stack can be shrunk** — and it does not contradict step989/llm_step002, which killed replacing a transform block's *internals* with a sparse head. Here the student is the same architecture, only shorter, trained on the stack's *OUTPUT*. Distinct claim, distinct evidence.

  **HYPOTHESIS (untested) — the remaining 18pp gap to the teacher is a budget artefact, not a capacity ceiling:** loss and cosine were still improving monotonically at ep10 on only 2000 images. Test = longer/larger run before any claim that d6 cannot reach 0.740. **Caveat:** `same`/`agree` (0.660/0.420) are well below top-1 0.560 — the student is not merely imitating the teacher, it is landing on the right label by a partly different route. Feature-MSE distillation does not preserve the teacher's function pointwise; on a 10-way task that is invisible, on an open-ended VLM task it may not be.

  **d9 results (mini_mps, identical budget — same n_train/epochs/lr/eval images):**

  | arm | params | %save | top1 | same | agree | KL | vis_ms | pre_ms | total | speed |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | teacher_d12 (ref) | 256,484,928 | 0.00 | **0.740** | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.000 | 66.2 | 14.6 | 80.9 | 1.00× |
  | naive_d9 | 235,221,312 | 8.29 | 0.080 | 0.180 | 0.000 | 9.973 | 50.5 | 14.3 | 64.8 | 1.25× |
  | distil_d9 | 235,221,312 | 8.29 | 0.540 | 0.580 | 0.380 | 2.881 | 50.4 | 14.3 | 64.8 | 1.25× |

  Final train state: rel_mse 0.2716, cosine 0.8551 — **worse than d6's 0.2573 / 0.8634 despite 3 more layers.**

  **CONFIRMED — recovery is depth-INDEPENDENT over 6..9 layers; the ceiling is the distillation budget, not student capacity.** d9 0.540 ≈ d6 0.560 (Δ = −2pp, within n=50 noise) at an identical budget, and d9 fit the teacher slightly *worse* on both loss metrics. Keeping 3 extra layers bought nothing. **Direct consequence: take the deeper cut — d6 dominates d9 on every axis** (16.58% vs 8.29% params saved, 1.62× vs 1.25× latency, equal accuracy). Kills the intuition that recovery degrades monotonically with depth removed, which would have argued for a conservative cut. Both arms plateauing at cosine ≈ 0.86 with loss still falling **strengthens the budget HYPOTHESIS above** — two different capacities converging to the same fidelity points at the objective/data, not the architecture. → d3 (does depth-independence extend to a 24.87% cut?), then a long-budget d6. **[DEMOTED after the long-budget d6 below: that shared plateau is now known to be a budget artefact, so "d9 ≈ d6" is only established AT the T0 budget. Retest = long-budget d9/d3.]**

  **d3 results (mini_mps, identical budget):**

  | arm | params | %save | top1 | same | agree | KL | vis_ms | pre_ms | total | speed |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | teacher_d12 (ref) | 256,484,928 | 0.00 | **0.740** | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.000 | 65.9 | 14.6 | 80.6 | 1.00× |
  | naive_d3 | 192,694,080 | 24.87 | 0.100 | 0.080 | 0.000 | 12.457 | 20.4 | 14.3 | 34.7 | 2.32× |
  | distil_d3 | 192,694,080 | 24.87 | 0.460 | 0.560 | 0.320 | 3.253 | 20.3 | 14.3 | 34.6 | **2.33×** |

  Final train state: rel_mse 0.3168, cosine 0.8284 — worse than d6 (0.2573/0.8634) and d9 (0.2716/0.8551), and here the ordering finally tracks depth.

  **Full depth-recovery curve (all at the same T0 budget; naive/chance = 0.100, teacher = 0.740):**

  | depth | %params saved | naive top1 | **distil top1** | distil KL | agree | speed | final cosine |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | d9 | 8.29 | 0.080 | 0.540 | 2.881 | 0.380 | 1.25× | 0.8551 |
  | **d6** | 16.58 | 0.120 | **0.560** | 2.512 | 0.420 | 1.62× | 0.8634 |
  | d3 | 24.87 | 0.100 | 0.460 | 3.253 | 0.320 | **2.33×** | 0.8284 |

  **CONFIRMED — depth-independence extends to the extreme cut; recovery is remarkably flat in depth.** Keeping only **3 of 12** layers (24.87% of the whole model removed, 2.33× vision latency) still reaches 0.460 — 4.6× chance and within 10pp of d6, from a naive arm that was exactly at chance (0.100, KL 12.457). Across a 3× range of surviving depth, distilled top-1 spans just 0.460–0.560. **Refines the d9 claim rather than repeating it:** d9→d6 was flat, d6→d3 finally bends, so the true shape is a plateau with a knee below d6, not a monotone slope. **d6 remains the Pareto pick** for accuracy-per-param; d3 is the pick where 2.33× latency outweighs 10pp. Note d3's train fit is the worst of the three *and* it has the fewest params — the first arm where the loss ordering tracks capacity, which is weak evidence that d3 is where capacity finally starts to bind.

  **Long-budget d6 DONE (mini_mps, n_train=6000, 15 ep — 3× data, 2.25× updates; identical code, flags only):**

  | d6 budget | rel_mse | cosine | top1 | same | agree | KL | vis_ms | speed |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | 2000 × 10 ep | 0.2573 | 0.8634 | 0.560 | 0.660 | 0.420 | 2.512 | 35.4 | 1.62× |
  | **6000 × 15 ep** | **0.2011** | **0.8951** | **0.680** | **0.760** | **0.500** | **1.988** | 35.4 | 1.63× |
  | teacher_d12 | — | 1.000 | 0.740 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.000 | 66.5 | 1.00× |

  **CONFIRMED — the residual gap was the distillation BUDGET, not student capacity.** +12pp top-1 from budget alone, gap to teacher 18pp → **6pp** (3 images at n=50), at unchanged 16.58% params and unchanged 1.63×. Every metric moved together (KL −21%, `agree` +8pp, `same` +10pp), so this is fidelity, not eval noise. Cosine rose monotonically through ep15 (0.7896 → 0.8951) — **still not converged.** Headline: *16.58% of the model deleted, 1.63× faster vision, 6pp cost, curve still descending.*

  **Two consequences, one of them a demotion.** (a) The "plateau at cosine ≈ 0.86" that motivated the depth-independence CONFIRMED above was an artefact of the 2000-image budget — d9 and d6 may simply have been budget-bound alike. That claim does **not** flip (d6 still dominates d9 at equal budget) but it now rests on weaker evidence; an honest retest is a long-budget d9/d3 pair. (b) A 6pp residual is below what n=50 resolves, so any *parity* claim needs n_eval ≥ 200. → **HYPOTHESIS: d6 reaches teacher parity at full budget** (9469 × 25–30 ep, n_eval ≥ 200, ~10 h on mini_mps) — deferred behind vlm_step005, which returns new information rather than more of the same.

  **Caveat SURVIVES the budget fix:** `agree` stayed ~18pp below top-1 at both budgets (0.420/0.560 → 0.500/0.680). Budget lifted both, closed neither gap — so the feature-MSE objective, not undertraining, is what fails to preserve the teacher's function pointwise. That is exactly vlm_step005's target. Results preserved per-budget at `results/frontier/vlm_step004_tower_distill_d6_10ep2k__mini_mps.json` and `_d6_15ep6k__mini_mps.json`.

- **vlm_step013 (does int4 survive at GROUP granularity? the last 28.4 MB): DONE — `int4_g64` SHIPS at BOTH depths; "int4 is dead" was wrong, one scale LAYOUT was dead. (5060ti_cuda, eval-only, 2 depths × 4 arms × 3859 images, ~1.7 h).** No new script — `scripts/frontier/vlm_step010_quant_tables.py` UNCHANGED with `--arms fp32 int4_row int4_g64 int4_g192`, so the pre-registered rule, the harness and the statistic are literally step010's rather than a re-derivation. The only code change is `vlm_quant.fake_quant/table_bytes` gaining a granularity axis. **This is a genuine open question, not a retry: step010 KILLED int4 at ROW granularity, where a single outlier column forces a coarse step across all 576 of them; a group confines that damage to its own block.** Part 5 §18 item 2 named it the only live route to the remaining 28.4 MB, and memory is the stubborn goal. **576's divisors include 64 and 192 but NOT the conventional 128** — the usual group size is silently wrong on this model, so `fake_quant` asserts divisibility instead of rounding. **Group scales are not free and are counted, never assumed:** g=64 is 9 fp32 scales/row = 4 + 32/64 = **4.5 effective bits** (15.97 MB/table) vs int4_row's 4.06 (14.39 MB), so a group scheme must beat int4_row on ACCURACY to justify 11% more bytes than it. Weight-space evidence that motivated the run (64×576 random matrix with one planted outlier): rel_mse int4_row 0.0304 → int4_g64 **0.0123**, a 2.5× reduction, with int4_g192 intermediate at 0.0192 — monotone in granularity, as the outlier-confinement account predicts. **Two validity gates, both free:** `fp32` reproduces step006's 0.711/0.713 for the fifth time, and `int4_row` must reproduce step010's own int4_row cell — a same-script, same-images control that catches any drift introduced by the granularity refactor. Old arms verified **bit-exact** vs the pre-edit code before launch (`torch.equal` on int4_row). Smoke PASSED on 5060ti (n=20, all 8 cells, byte accounting and both CI blocks clean). Launched while the foreign kernel still holds 13.7 of 16.3 GiB — eval-only fits, which is exactly why this rather than a training arm is what runs on that card now. **VERDICT.** d12: int4_row −10.39pp [−11.51, −9.30] KILLED → int4_g192 −5.08pp [−5.99, −4.17] KILLED → **int4_g64 −1.09pp [−1.84, −0.31] SHIP**. d6: int4_row −8.60pp KILLED → int4_g192 −2.44pp [−3.29, −1.58] **INCONCLUSIVE** (interval straddles the −2pp band; the rule declining to answer, not a soft kill) → **int4_g64 +0.67pp [+0.00, +1.35] SHIP**, top-1 **0.720** vs teacher 0.711. **Monotone in group size across three points at BOTH depths** — the outlier-confinement account made a quantitative prediction and the grid matched it, and the weight-space POC had the same ordering (rel_mse 0.0304 → 0.0192 → 0.0123) before any GPU time was spent. **Validity gates:** fp32 reproduced step006's 0.711/0.713 for the fifth time; int4_row reproduced step010 exactly at d12 (0.607, agree 0.790 vs 0.791) and **drifted by 6 images at d6 (0.627 vs 0.633)** — cross-device mps→cuda on quantized weights of the distilled tower, ~0.6pp, an order of magnitude under the 10.39pp effect, recorded rather than rounded away: int4_row cells reproduce to ~0.6pp across devices, not to the digit. **Interaction: all three arms INCONCLUSIVE and all three POSITIVE** (I(g64) = +1.76pp [+0.78, +2.80]) — sign says quantization costs the truncated tower *less* than the teacher, the opposite of a compounding risk, but every interval crosses the +2pp edge so nothing is called; consistent with step010's int4_row I = +2.33pp (this run: +1.79pp). **Byte case is real but smaller than the accuracy story suggests:** g64 = 4.5 effective bits (31.9 MB/table) vs int4_row 4.06 (28.8) vs int8_row 8.06 (57.2), so the marginal gain over the §16 shipping config is **25.3 MB of table bytes** — exact, from the run's own accounting — ≈669 MB vs ≈686 MB on the compound d6 config against a 1026 MB fp32 teacher. **What ships, and the caveat that decides it: `d6 + int4_g64` is the memory champion on bytes-on-disk, but NO int4 kernel exists in this stack** — the eval is quantize→dequantize, so today the win is storage only while int8 has a real `torch.ao` path. `d6 + int8_row` keeps the runtime story; do not collapse the two claims. Artifacts: `results/frontier/vlm_step010_quant_tables_d6_fp32-int4_row-int4_g64-int4_g192__5060ti_cuda.json` (995 KB, per-image). Write-up: part6 §21. **Consequence for the roadmap: the memory lever is now fully explored and the ENERGY lever is entirely untouched — a real int8/int4 kernel is promoted to the blocking next item (part6 §22 item 3).**

- **vlm_step014 (does the byte win convert to a LATENCY win? the blocking item step013 promoted): DONE — NO, and the fault is the KERNEL, not the theory. (5060ti_cuda, microbenchmark, ~2 min).** No script and no queue slot: three `python -c` probes at the real `lm_head` shape ([T, 576] @ [576, 49280]), because the question "is there an int8 kernel worth building against" is answerable in two minutes and would otherwise have been assumed. **Environment:** torch 2.11.0+cu128, sm_120 (cap 12,0); `torch._int_mm` **present** and dependency-free; `bitsandbytes` and `torchao` both ABSENT. **Result:** `_int_mm` gives **0.98× / 1.00× / 0.96×** vs the fp16 GEMM at T=64/128/576 — no speedup anywhere across a 9× span of token counts. **Three facts in the order that makes the conclusion unavoidable.** (1) CONFIRMED — the fp16 GEMM is *memory-bound*: 56.8 MB of weights in 0.147 ms = **386 GB/s, ~86% of the card's ~448 GB/s peak**, so halving the bytes should halve the time. (2) CONFIRMED — the hardware delivers exactly that: `clone` moves int8 at **386 GB/s vs fp16's 387 GB/s**, i.e. half the bytes in half the time (0.147 vs 0.293 ms). The 2× is physically available on this card. (3) CONFIRMED — `_int_mm` captures none of it. **HYPOTHESIS for the cause** (inference from the three measurements, not observed directly): `_int_mm`'s sm_120 path is unoptimized for this skinny-K/huge-N shape. What is NOT in doubt is the actionable half — the ceiling is real and the stock kernel forfeits it, so the route is a Triton int8 GEMM or `torchao`, not abandoning int8. **The result that changes a deployment decision: dequantize-then-matmul costs 0.431 ms, 2.9× SLOWER than just keeping fp16.** That is precisely what a fake-quant config does if shipped naively, so storing int8/int4 weights and expanding them at runtime is a latency REGRESSION, not a neutral trade. **Every §16/§21 byte saving is therefore bytes AT REST only, and any drone claim must say so until a real kernel lands — a quantized model with no quantized kernel spends energy rather than saving it.** Method note: a first bandwidth probe used `w.sum()` and reported int8 at 23 GB/s vs fp16's 383; that is PyTorch's slow int8 elementwise reduction, not a bandwidth reading — discarded and re-measured with `clone()`, which is a pure copy. Cost of the finding: ~2 min of GPU time against days of integration work predicated on a 2× the stock kernel never had. Write-up: part6 §22.

- **vlm_step015 (can a REAL int8 GEMM capture the 2× step014 proved is physically there?): DONE — YES, 1.98× at T=576, bit-exact. `torch._int_mm` was the WHOLE problem. (5060ti_cuda, microbenchmark, ~4 min).** Script: `scripts/frontier/vlm_step015_int8_kernel.py` (new, 168 lines). step014 prescribed "a Triton int8 GEMM or torchao, not abandoning int8"; this took that route. Triton ships INSIDE torch (3.6.0), so nothing was installed into the teammates' shared venv. **The control is the load-bearing design choice:** timing a Triton int8 kernel against cuBLAS fp16 confounds int8-vs-fp16 with triton-vs-cuBLAS, and a mediocre Triton kernel would then masquerade as "int8 doesn't help on this card" — the exact wrong conclusion step014 was at risk of. So `_gemm` is ONE kernel used for both dtypes (same tiling, same loop, same autotune grid, only the accumulator differs: `tl.int32`→IMMA vs `tl.float32`→HMMA), making the `8/16 tri` column a one-variable measurement. **Results** ([T,576]@[576,49280], sm_120, median of 50): T=64 cuBLAS16 0.1519 / _int_mm 0.1560 / tri16 0.1732 / **tri8 0.1012** = 1.71× matched, 1.50× vs cuBLAS. T=128 0.1770 / 0.1815 / 0.2229 / **0.1519** = 1.47×, 1.17×. T=576 0.6911 / 0.7187 / 0.8820 / **0.4458** = **1.98× matched**, 1.55× vs cuBLAS. **Correctness gate EXACT at all three T** — both sides are exact integer arithmetic so `torch.equal` against `torch._int_mm` is the right test, not a tolerance; a fast kernel computing something else would be worthless and this rules it out rather than assuming it. **T=576 recovers 1.98× of the 2.00× step014 derived from bandwidth — the ceiling was not theoretical and a competent kernel reaches it. step014's HYPOTHESIS (`_int_mm`'s sm_120 path is at fault) is now CONFIRMED by the direct method of writing a kernel that doesn't fail.** **The measurement understates itself:** the Triton kernel writes int32 output (4 B/elem vs the fp16 baseline's 2), so at T=576 it moves 142 MB against fp16's 113 MB — it wins 1.55× while moving MORE total bytes than its competitor. A fused dequant epilogue removes 56.7 MB of that traffic, so the quoted number is a FLOOR. **Weak cell reported not smoothed: T=128 gives only 1.17×** vs 1.50/1.55× at its neighbours — HYPOTHESIS: autotune gap (M=128 with BM=128 leaves grid_m=1 and no config suits it), untested; the speedup is NOT uniform in T and quoting 1.98× without naming its T would mislead. **HYPOTHESIS on mechanism:** at T=64 the win is bandwidth (step014: 386 GB/s ≈ 86% of peak), at T=576 it is throughput (fp16 arm is at ~47 TFLOP/s and int8 tensor cores run 2× the fp16 rate) — inferred from arithmetic, not ablated apart. **THE GAP THIS OPENS MATTERS MORE THAN THE SPEEDUP (part7 §24.1): the accuracy experiments and the kernel experiment are not measuring the same quantization scheme.** step010/013 measured **weight-only** (`fake_quant` dequantizes back to fp32, activations untouched) = W8A16/W4A16. `_int_mm` and the Triton kernel both need **two int8 operands** = **W8A8**, activations dynamically quantized per forward. So: −33.1% table bytes at parity is CONFIRMED for W8A16 with no runtime; `int4_g64` +0.67pp is CONFIRMED for W4A16 with **no int4 kernel at all**; 1.17–1.98× is CONFIRMED for W8A8 with **accuracy unmeasured**. **No single configuration has both halves — quoting step013's accuracy alongside step015's speed would assemble a result no experiment produced.** Further scope limits: microbenchmark of ONE GEMM, not end-to-end (of the two tables step010 quantizes only `lm_head` is a GEMM; the input embedding is a gather and no kernel applies), and activations are fed pre-quantized so the per-forward `[T,576]` quantization cost is excluded. Write-up: part7 §24. **Consequence: W8A8 ACCURACY is the new blocking item (part7 §29 item 2) and is cheap — an arm addition to `vlm_quant`, reusing the existing harness, images, seed and `call_ship` rule.**
- **vlm_step016 (does the config with the 1.98× kernel also hold step010's accuracy parity? W8A8 vs W8A16): DONE — YES. W8A8 SHIPS at both depths; quantizing ACTIVATIONS costs essentially nothing on top of quantizing weights. (5060ti_cuda, 6 cells × 3859 images, 1.3 h).** **Results:** `d12_fp32` 0.711 / `d12_int8_row` 0.711 (d +0.0000, [−0.0029,+0.0029], SHIP) / `d12_w8a8_row` 0.710 (d **−0.0010**, [−0.0041,+0.0021], SHIP); `d6_fp32` 0.713 / `d6_int8_row` 0.708 (d −0.0052, [−0.0086,−0.0018], SHIP) / `d6_w8a8_row` 0.708 (d **−0.0052**, [−0.0088,−0.0016], **SHIP**). **At d6 the W8A8 row is IDENTICAL to the weight-only row in every metric to three decimals** (top1 0.708, agree 0.698, KL 1.987) with the same paired point estimate; the CI widens by 0.0004 and that is the entire difference. At d12 activation quantization costs 0.1pp (agree 0.986→0.983, KL 0.001→0.003) — non-zero but far inside the ±2.0pp tolerance. Interaction ORTHOGONAL for both arms (`w8a8_row` I −0.0041, [−0.0091,+0.0005]), so quantization and truncation still compound. **Three validity gates passed and the third is the strong one:** (1) `d12_fp32`=0.711 / `d6_fp32`=0.713 for the sixth time; (2) `int8_row` reproduces step010's cells exactly; (3) its *paired lines are character-identical* to step010's — `+0.0000 [−0.0029,+0.0029] b=16 c=16 p=1.00000` at d12, `−0.0052 [−0.0086,−0.0018] b=12 c=32 p=0.00366` at d6, interaction `−0.0052 [−0.0096,−0.0008]` — **despite step010 running on mps and this on cuda**. The activation-axis refactor introduced zero drift, measured rather than assumed. **HYPOTHESIS (not ablated): the truncated tower MASKS the activation noise, which is why d6 pays less than d12** — d6's output is already far from the teacher (agree 0.699, KL 1.973 vs d12's 1.000/0.000), so the extra per-token rounding lands inside an error truncation already dominates; a d9 cell would test it. **What this licenses:** §24.1 is CLOSED — the scheme with the 1.17–1.98× kernel is the same scheme that holds parity, so the two may now be quoted about one model. **What it does NOT:** still TWO measurements, not one end-to-end run — this grid uses `fake_quant`, so `vis_ms` is unchanged at 13.6 ms *by construction* and no speedup is measured here; and it covers `lm_head` only (the other quantized table, `embed_tokens`, is a gather no kernel touches, so weight-only is correct there rather than a gap). **A REAL TRADE now exists where §21 read as a clean win:** `int4_g64` beats int8 on accuracy (+0.67pp at d6) and on bytes but has **no int4 kernel at all**, while `w8a8_row` has both halves — so it is `int4_g64` for smallest static footprint at fp16 speed, or `w8a8_row` for 1.17–1.98× on the `lm_head` GEMM at −0.52pp. The drone choice depends on storage-bound vs energy-bound; the trajectory must stop implying one config dominates. Write-up: part7 §26. No new script — this is the arm addition step015 promoted to blocking (part7 §29 item 2), run through `scripts/frontier/vlm_step010_quant_tables.py --depth 6 --arms fp32 int8_row w8a8_row`. **The gap it closes:** every §16/§21 accuracy claim was measured **weight-only** (activations fp32) = W8A16, while `torch._int_mm` and the step015 Triton kernel both need **two int8 operands** = W8A8. Quoting step013's accuracy alongside step015's speed would assemble a result no experiment produced; this measures the configuration that actually has the runtime. **Category fix, not a special case:** `vlm_quant.ARMS` values became 3-tuples `(bits, granularity, act_bits)` with `act_bits=None` meaning weight-only — the activation policy is now an explicit field of *every* arm rather than an unstated property of all of them, so a future arm cannot silently be read as having a runtime it has not been measured under. New `act_quant` is **per-token** (one scale per row of the last dim), not per-tensor, because that is what a dynamic-quant kernel computes: the scale comes from the activation being multiplied, no calibration set, and rows are independent so an outlier token cannot coarsen another token's grid (measured: outlier-row error 3.3106 vs clean-row 0.012386 = **267×** — per-tensor would have paid that on every row). **Only `lm_head` gets the hook.** The other table is `embed_tokens`, a gather whose input is token *indices* — "quantizing its activations" is not a defined operation, and doing it to both would be wrong rather than conservative. **`apply_arm` removes hooks FIRST, unconditionally**, because a leaked hook is the one failure that corrupts the grid invisibly: it would quantize activations inside the `fp32` reference cell that every paired delta is measured against. `table_bytes` deliberately ignores `act_bits` — activations are transient, so W8A8 stores exactly what W8A16 stores and any accuracy cost here buys speed, not bytes. **Five local validity gates passed before any GPU time:** (1) all six pre-existing arms bit-exact after the 3-tuple refactor, (2) weight-only arms install and leak no hooks, (3) the w8a8 hook installs on `lm_head` and demonstrably fires, (4) the `fp32` cell is clean *after* a w8a8 arm, (5) per-token isolation as above. **Smoke on 5060ti gave the right monotone signal** — d12 KL 0.000 (fp32) < 0.001 (int8_row) < 0.003 (w8a8_row): activation quantization adds a small extra distributional cost on top of weight quantization, rather than nothing (hook not firing) or chaos (hook wrong). **Two free pre-registered validity gates in the run itself:** `fp32` must reproduce 0.711 / 0.713 for the sixth time, and `int8_row` must reproduce step010's own int8_row cell — a same-script same-images control that catches any drift from the activation-axis refactor. Verdict by the unchanged `call_ship` rule on `w8a8_row` vs same-depth `fp32`. **Log-collision hazard closed before launch:** `launch_slot.sh` names logs `${STEP_NAME}__${SLOT}.log` from the *script* filename, so this third use of `vlm_step010_quant_tables.py` would have overwritten step013's log; the old one is preserved as `..._5060ti_cuda_ckpt-step004_arms-fp32-int4_row-int4_g64-int4_g192.log`. Same class as step012's TAG hazard — an artifact path that does not encode every input it depends on. (The JSON was already safe: `TAG` includes the arms.)
- **vlm_step017 (does FUSING the dequant scale into the epilogue recover the write traffic step015 paid? the FLOOR step015 named): DONE — YES. The floor lifts from 1.17–1.98× to 1.45–2.14× vs cuBLAS fp16, bit-exact, and T=64 BREAKS the 2.00× bandwidth ceiling step014 derived. (5060ti_cuda, microbenchmark, ~3 min).** Script: `scripts/frontier/vlm_step017_fused_dequant.py` (new, 183 lines). step015 reported its own 1.55× as a FLOOR and named why: the kernel stores **int32** (4 B/elem vs the fp16 baseline's 2), so at T=576 it moved 142 MB against fp16's 113 MB — it won while moving MORE total bytes than the thing it beat. A deployment does not do that; it multiplies by the per-token and per-column scales inside the kernel and writes fp16. **Results** ([T,576]@[576,49280], sm_120, median of 50, ms): T=64 cuBLAS16 0.1527 / tri_i32 0.1016 / unfused 0.1987 / **fused 0.0714** = 1.42× vs i32, 2.78× vs unfused, **2.14× vs cuBLAS**. T=128 0.1773 / 0.1504 / 0.5553 / **0.1226** = 1.23× / 4.53× / **1.45×**. T=576 0.6898 / 0.4471 / 2.6331 / **0.3975** = 1.12× / 6.62× / **1.74×**. **CONFIRMED — fusion wins at every T, and it is a genuine one-variable measurement:** ONE `@triton.jit _gemm` carries a `FUSE: tl.constexpr` that switches only the epilogue, so the two arms are not merely equivalent in the main loop, they are *literally the same code*. First draft used two kernels and hit the 200-line limit at 210 lines; merging them fixed the limit **and improved the science** — a copied main loop must be trusted to match, a shared one cannot diverge. **Correctness gate EXACT at all three T** — fused fp16 output bit-identical to `torch._int_mm(...).float() * s_a * s_w` cast to fp16. Both sides accumulate exactly in int32 and scale in fp32, so the only admissible difference was fp16 rounding of the final store, and there is none. Nothing was bought with precision. **The cross-session drift gate passed for free:** the `FUSE=0` arm is step015's kernel verbatim (same tiling, same `CFGS`), and `tri_i32` reproduced step015's column to within 0.4–1.0% (0.1016/0.1504/0.4471 vs 0.1012/0.1519/0.4458) and cuBLAS to within 0.2% — two separate sessions on the same card, so the gain is the epilogue and not a warmer GPU. Keeping the control as an arm rather than a memory bought this at zero extra cost. **HYPOTHESIS (not ablated) — T=64's 2.14× exceeds step014's 2.00× bandwidth ceiling because of L2 RESIDENCY, not because the ceiling was wrong.** Measured `L2_cache_size` = **33.55 MB** on this RTX 5060 Ti (36 SMs): the int8 weight table is 28.4 MB and **fits**; fp16's 56.7 MB does **not**. Halving the bytes changes *where they live*, and a ceiling derived from DRAM bandwidth cannot bound an arm that never goes to DRAM. The direct test is to shrink N until fp16 also fits and check the ratio falls back toward 2.00× — until then this is an explanation, not a fact. **The unfused column is a bigger deployment warning than the fusion win is a victory:** a second-pass scale costs 2.6331 ms at T=576 = **5.9× the GEMM it decorates** and 3.8× slower than simply keeping fp16 — the same failure shape step014 found in dequantize-then-matmul, which is the recurring lesson that quantization arithmetic must live INSIDE the kernel or it eats the win whole. **Scope limit stated rather than buried: that baseline is eager PyTorch** (materializes full-size fp32 temporaries), so `fus/unfused` (2.78–6.62×) is an **UPPER BOUND** on what fusion buys and a compiled unfused path would sit between it and `fus/i32`. The defensible numbers are `fus/i32` (the one-variable epilogue effect) and `fus/cuBLAS` (what a deployment quotes). **The kernel consumes exactly the scales step016 measured accuracy under** — per-token activation scale on rows of A, per-row `lm_head` weight scale landing on the COLUMNS of B (B being the transposed weight) = `vlm_quant.ARMS['w8a8_row']` — so §26 and §28 describe ONE scheme, not two. **What it does NOT license: still not one end-to-end run.** This is a microbenchmark of a single GEMM with activations fed pre-quantized, so the per-forward `[T,576]` quantization cost is excluded and `vis_ms` is unmeasured. Write-up: part7 §28; part7 §29 item 4 now reads DONE with the end-to-end number as its residue. **Operational notes:** ran on the free 5060ti; a foreign process held 13711/16311 MiB at 0% util and was **left alone** (teammates' work, never killed) — step017 peaks ~300 MB and fit in the 2.6 GiB free. mini_cpu was deliberately left idle despite being FREE, because it shares a physical box with the mini_mps slot running the decisive step012 distillation and added CPU load would perturb that measurement. Two launch faults, both the same class as prior ones: `--device` was missing (`launch_slot.sh` ALWAYS passes it) and the CUDA checklist gate fired (pre-empted with `--unsafe-cuda-launch`, correct here because this is a benchmark, not SGNNET training). Triton ships inside torch 3.6.0, so nothing was installed into the teammates' shared venv.
- **vlm_step018 (what shape does `lm_head` ACTUALLY run at in the eval harness?): DONE — T=1, NOT T=576. Every kernel number in part 7 was measured on a shape this pipeline never executes, and the true deployed number is BIGGER: 3.05× at T=1, peaking 3.45× at T=8. (5060ti_cuda, microbenchmark, ~3 min).** No new script — `scripts/frontier/vlm_step017_fused_dequant.py` re-run with `--T 1 2 3 8 16 64` (plus a reference fix, below), the same pattern as step016 re-running step010's script. **Found by READING the harness, not by running anything:** `vlm_eval.VLMEval` calls `lm_head` in exactly two places and neither is a prefill-length GEMM — `forward()` does `lm_head(o.last_hidden_state[:, -1])` = **T=1**, and `choose()` does `lm_head(o.last_hidden_state)` over one label's token run = **T≈1–3, ten times per image, entirely OUTSIDE the timed region**. The 64 image tokens and the prompt traverse the *text tower* at full length but never reach `lm_head`, because a classification readout needs one distribution, not T of them. **Results** ([T,576]@[576,49280], median of 50, ms): T=1 cuBLAS16 0.1470 / tri_i32 0.0487 / unfused 0.0602 / **fused 0.0482** = 1.01× vs i32, **3.05× vs cuBLAS**; T=2 **3.31×**; T=3 **3.41×**; T=8 **3.45×**; T=16 **3.17×**; T=64 **2.13×**. **Correctness gate EXACT at every T.** The error ran in the SAFE direction — part 7 quotes 1.45–2.14× and *understates* the deployed win by ~1.4×, rather than claiming a win that is not there — but it was still the wrong shape and would have gone into the paper as a deployment claim. **CONFIRMED — fusion buys NOTHING at the deployed shape:** `fus/i32` = 0.98–1.01× for every T ≤ 16, becoming real only at T=64 (1.41×). Mechanism is arithmetic, not mysterious: the epilogue saves *output* bytes, and at T=1 the output is 49,280 elements (197 KB int32 vs 98 KB fp16) against a **28.4 MB weight read both arms pay identically**. Fusion's benefit scales with T; the weight read does not scale with T at all. **Deployment consequence, and it is the cheaper option winning: the drone can ship the SIMPLER int32 kernel plus a separate scale and lose nothing** — §28's fused epilogue is right for a batched-prefill serving path and an unnecessary complication for this one. That inverts part 7 §29 item 4. **CONFIRMED — the fp16 baseline is FLAT in T (0.147–0.166 ms from T=1 to T=64), i.e. weight-bandwidth bound:** a 576×49280 fp16 table is 56.7 MB and reading it costs the same whether one row or sixty-four are multiplied against it. This is why the readout is a MEMORY problem and why quantization — not sparsity, not a smarter GEMM — is the lever that moves it. **HYPOTHESIS strengthened, still not ablated — L2 residency, not mere byte-halving:** §28 measured `L2_cache_size` = 33.55 MB, the int8 table (28.4 MB) fits and fp16's 56.7 MB does not; a pure byte-halving argument caps the win at 2.00× (step014's ceiling) and **3.45× exceeds it by 1.7× at FIVE separate T values**, so it is not a lucky cell. Named ablation unchanged: shrink N until fp16 also fits in L2 and check the ratio falls back toward 2.00×. **Two validity gates passed, one free:** (1) the **T=64 overlap cell reproduces step017 within 1%** — cuBLAS 0.1522 vs 0.1527, tri_i32 0.1006 vs 0.1016, fused 0.0715 vs 0.0714, fus/cuB 2.13× vs 2.14× — a third session on the same card; (2) that same cell re-passed the correctness gate **after the reference implementation was changed**, so the change did not quietly weaken it. **The gate had to be REBUILT to reach T=1, and the fix is a category fix:** `torch._int_mm` raises `self.size(0) needs to be greater than 16, but got 1` — the reference itself could not evaluate the deployed shape. Replacement is `(x8.float() @ w8.float()) * s_a * s_w`, **bit-exact rather than merely close**: with int8 operands and K=576 every partial sum is an integer bounded by 127·127·576 = 9.29e6 < 2^24, hence exactly representable in fp32 *regardless of accumulation order*. The bound is `assert`ed in the script rather than assumed so a larger K fails loudly instead of silently degrading to an approximate gate, and `allow_tf32` is pinned off so exactness does not depend on a global flag. **ONE code path for every M** — deliberately not a branch keyed to the M ≤ 16 case that happened to break, which would have left the identical trap for the next shape. **What it does NOT license — still a microbenchmark of one GEMM in isolation, and four limits are now sharper:** (1) the **end-to-end number is still unmeasured and is now KNOWN to be Amdahl-limited** — `prefill_ms` times the whole text tower plus one T=1 `lm_head`, so saving 0.099 ms inside a multi-millisecond prefill is a small fraction of it, and quoting 3.05× end-to-end would be exactly the composition error §24.1 warned about one level up; (2) the **ten `choose()` calls per image are untimed by construction** — real `lm_head` work the harness has never counted, so an honest per-image readout cost is *larger* than `prefill_ms` shows and the omission is NOT conservative in the direction one would assume; (3) the harness runs the model in **fp32**, so its actual baseline is an fp32 GEMM — fp16 is the right *deployment* baseline and the honest comparison, and an fp32 comparison would flatter int8 further and must not be quoted; (4) activations are fed pre-quantized, so the per-forward quantization cost is excluded (at T=1 that is 576 elements, negligible, but excluded rather than measured). Write-up: **`learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part8.md` §30** — part 7 hit the 200-line limit at 199 lines, so part 8 opens here; §31 supersedes part 7 §29 where they conflict, and part 7 §24/§28 remain correct AS MEASURED (they are the batched-prefill numbers and should be labelled as such, not deleted). **Consequence: an int4 kernel is now MORE attractive, not less** — the readout is weight-bandwidth bound and flat in T, so halving weight bytes again is the lever that keeps paying, and step016's `int4_g64` already wins accuracy (+0.67pp at d6) and bytes; the part 7 §29 item 5 trade is now lopsided enough to be worth building rather than merely stating. **Log-collision hazard handled again:** `launch_slot.sh` names logs from the *script* filename, so this second use of `vlm_step017_fused_dequant.py` would have overwritten step017's log — the canonical one is preserved and this run's is `logs/vlm_step017_fused_dequant__5060ti_cuda_T-1-64.log`. Same class as step013/step016. The teammates' 13711/16311 MiB GPU allocation was left untouched.

Write-up: `VLM_TRAJECTORY_part19.md` section 56. tmux `sgn-vlm039`, log `logs/vlm_step039_control_rerun__5060ti_cuda.log`; monitor `bwdwe6cu0` armed on departures + failures. `scripts/frontier/vlm_step037_tail_shape.py --modes compile --repeats 16 --n_eval 500 --tag_suffix _rerun` -- the SAME script that drew 5/16, byte-identical, no instrumentation added. **Why:** step038's `rebuild` arm used step037's protocol and drew **0/16**. That is a non-replication of the effect section 55 called deterministic, and it has exactly two readings which this arm separates. **Pre-registered:** step037's script departs >=1/16 while step038's rebuild arm did not -> the difference is step038's ADDED INSTRUMENTATION (per-image `memory_reserved()`, per-pass `memory_stats()`), which would localise the mechanism to allocator timing -- a probe that suppresses the effect is itself strong evidence about the effect. step037's script ALSO draws 0/16 -> the instrumentation is exonerated and the ENVIRONMENT changed between 20:42 and 20:50; section 55's Finding 4 then degrades from 'deterministic across processes' to 'deterministic within a window', and its promotion of Finding 3 to CONFIRMED must be withdrawn. Either way one of section 55's two load-bearing claims falls. **This arm has no third outcome, which is why it is worth the card time.** **CORRECTION to my own first note on this row:** I initially wrote that 0/16 'is not a rare event' under the historic rate and that a clean rerun would be merely suggestive. That is wrong and the arithmetic says so -- at the historic 5/16 incidence, P(0 departures in 16) = (11/16)^16 = **0.0025**, and pooled over step038's two clean arms (32 passes) = **6e-6**. Under section 55's own stronger claim (deterministic at fixed rep indices) the probability is 0. So step038's double-clean is ALREADY strong evidence that something material changed between 20:42 and 20:50 -- it is not absorbable as an unlucky draw under either model. What step039 adds is not significance but ATTRIBUTION: which of the two changes (added instrumentation vs environment) did it. The iid-Bernoulli p above is itself model-dependent and section 55 argues the draws are not iid, which is why the arm still runs rather than the number being quoted as a result. **Cost:** 16 timed passes, ~16 min, no training.
- **vlm_step038 (stall MECHANISM + no-rebuild soak): DONE 2026-08-15, 5060ti_cuda. RESULT: BOTH arms 0/16 departures -- a NON-REPLICATION, and (given step039's rep0) evidence that THE PROBE SUPPRESSED THE EFFECT.** `soak` arm: prefill 4.20-4.28 (gate 4.5), `reserved` pinned at 592 MB for all 16 passes, 0 stalls >=50 ms, 0 alloc retries, tower 4.05-4.09, top1 0.6880 every pass, wall 55.4-59.6 s, load1 0.98-1.07. `rebuild` arm below. **Neither pre-registered read is reachable** -- both the 'EVAL-PROTOCOL ARTIFACT' and the 'deployment defect' branch required the rebuild CONTROL to be dirty, and it was clean, so a control that failed to reproduce voids its own experiment's reads. The script's terminal line printed `soak CLEAN ... EVAL-PROTOCOL ARTIFACT` because it hardcoded the assumption that rebuild would be dirty; **that printed conclusion is WRONG and must not be quoted.** `rebuild` arm: all 16 passes 4.03-4.28 (gate 4.5), 0 stalls >=50 ms, **0 alloc retries in 8000 images**, `reserved` 606 MB at rep0 then pinned at 628 MB for reps 1-15, tower 3.97-4.09, top1 0.6880 on every pass, wall 58.0-59.8 s, load1 0.95-1.07. **The arm was designed to reproduce the stall by construction so the counters would have something to explain; it reproduced nothing.** The allocator counters are therefore UNINFORMATIVE rather than negative -- zero retries across a pass containing no stall says nothing about whether retries accompany a stall, and reading it as 'allocator hypothesis KILLED' would be reading a null from a cell with no signal in it. The pre-registered allocator read is VOID for this arm, exactly as section 53's void-premise rule requires. What this DOES bear on is section 55's Finding 4: two processes agreed to 0.047 ms at fixed rep indices, a third with the same protocol drew nothing, so 'deterministic' is now at n=2-agree / 1-disagree. See vlm_step039 for the arm that separates instrumentation-suppression from environment-change -- **its rep0 came back DEPARTURE 4.55 ms / img max 250.59 ms against step037's 4.53 / 246.1, so the environment did NOT change and the suppression is attributable to this script's instrumentation.** `scripts/frontier/vlm_step038_stall_mechanism.py --arms rebuild soak --mode compile --repeats 16 --n_eval 500` (199 lines; `--help` + `--smoke_test` both passed before launch -- smoke run confirmed the soak arm loads weights ONCE for its whole rep loop while the rebuild arm loads per rep, which is the arm's entire premise; card verified EMPTY at launch, 48 MiB / 0%). tmux `sgn-vlm038`, log `logs/vlm_step038_stall_mechanism__5060ti_cuda.log`. **Why:** section 55 left the mechanism UNKNOWN after falsifying section 49's dispatch-jitter attribution -- the excursion is ONE image stalling 246-802 ms, deterministic in the rep axis (step036 vs step037 agree to 0.047 ms over 16 paired reps) and random in the image axis. Any mechanism must spend a FIXED budget per rep at an UNPREDICTABLE moment. **Arm 1 `rebuild`** = step037's protocol plus allocator instrumentation (per-image `reserved` bytes, per-pass `num_alloc_retries`) -- reproduces the stall by construction so the counters have something to explain. **Arm 2 `soak`** = build ONCE, R passes back to back; a deployed drone builds once, so this is the only version of the question the paper needs. **Pre-registered:** soak CLEAN + rebuild DIRTY -> stall is an EVAL-PROTOCOL ARTIFACT of per-rep rebuilding (like the section 44 GraphEval clone), section 55's drone paragraph retracted, no latency guarantee at risk. soak DIRTY -> deployment defect, the >=802 ms per-frame worst case stands. `reserved` jumping on the stall image, or `num_alloc_retries` incrementing on exactly the departure -> allocator reclaim CONFIRMED. Flat reserved AND zero retries -> allocator hypothesis KILLED, hunt moves to host page faults / driver preemption. soak clean at R=16 bounds a RATE, never a mechanism (section 51/53) -- power stated, not assumed. Gate carried over VERBATIM from step036/037 (compile >=4.5 ms). **Cost:** 32 timed passes, ~50 min, no training. Write-up: `VLM_TRAJECTORY_part19.md` section 56.
- **vlm_step037 (SHAPE of the prefill excursion -- spike vs sustained vs jitter): DONE 2026-08-15 20:42 IST, 5060ti_cuda. RESULT: SPIKE, 5/5, not close. `compile: 5/16 departures (gate 4.5 ms)`; every departure is ONE image stalling 246 / 400 / 512 / 736 / 802 ms while the other 499 are unchanged.** Top-image share 1.48 / 1.04 / 0.98 / 0.99 / 1.00 against a 0.50 gate; median-shift share ~0 or negative throughout. top1 0.6880 on all 16 passes; tower 3.96-4.09; load1 0.88-0.99 (no load artifact). **Finding 2 -- the departures are DETERMINISTIC, not stochastic. CONFIRMED.** step037 reproduced step036's departure REP INDICES and MAGNITUDES from a separate process: rep0 4.58/4.53, rep3 4.97/4.97, rep5 5.25/5.24, rep10 5.69/5.68, rep11 5.81/5.80 -- 5/5, all within 0.05 ms -- and matched on the base side too (rep12 4.19/4.17, rep13 4.25/4.24, rep14 4.30/4.28). Two predictions written before their deciding draws both held (rep5: 'a later departure exceeds 512 ms' -> rep10 drew 736; rep10: 'rep11 departs at ~5.81' -> 5.80). **Finding 3 -- section 49's dispatch-jitter attribution is FALSIFIED.** Host-launch jitter does not fire at fixed rep indices, reproducibly across processes, with monotonically growing magnitude, concentrated in one image out of 500. section 54's 50x stage asymmetry still rules out process-global causes, so the localisation survives; only the named mechanism dies. **Finding 4 -- every incidence number since section 49 was a pass-length artifact.** If a departure is one stalled image, departures-per-pass is a function of `n_eval`: 'roughly 1 in 5' at n_eval=500 is ~1 in 1700 per image; at n_eval=100 the same box reports ~1 in 17. 6/20 and 5/16 are the same quantity measured with different-length rulers. The right unit is STALL MAGNITUDE (>=802 ms), not incidence and not mean prefill. **TWO OF MY OWN KILLS REVERSED:** (a) section 50's 'positional lock' was REAL -- dismissed at section 51 as a 5-draw coincidence and carried as dismissed for four sections; the over-read correction was applied one time too many. (b) section 54's 'escalating ramp' kill was MIS-AIMED -- it refuted a GLOBAL ramp (rep12/rep18 returned to base) but the claim was about growth in STALL MAGNITUDE, which is monotone 6/6 in step036 and 5/5 in step037. Kill a claim by testing the claim, not an adjacent one. **In-run hypothesis raised and killed by the run:** rep0's 246 ms spike could have been warmup residue surviving the 20-image warmup; rep3's 399.87 ms spike killed it (not a first pass). **DRONE READING REFRAMED:** not a fat latency tail but a ~250-800 ms FREEZE at a reproducible point -- at 30 fps an 800 ms stall drops ~24 consecutive frames. Deterministic is far more likely fixable than stochastic. Median ship numbers untouched (prefill 2.75, tower 4.01, e2e 6.76, top1 0.6880, 42.6 MB bf16). **LEADING MECHANISM, HYPOTHESIS: caching-allocator reclaim.** Each rep rebuilds the model (`torch._dynamo.reset()`, `del ev`, `torch.cuda.empty_cache()`); the allocation sequence is deterministic, so fragmentation crosses reclaim thresholds at fixed points and reclaim costs more as the pool grows -- fits determinism + monotone growth + irregular spacing (0,3,5,10,11,17) simultaneously; nothing else proposed fits more than one. **NOTE the rebuild is a HARNESS property -- a deployed drone builds once, so this may be an EVAL-protocol artifact like the section 44 GraphEval clone rather than a shipping defect. step038 must distinguish the two.** Artifacts: `results/frontier/vlm_step037_tail_shape_bf16_compile_r16_n500_shape__5060ti_cuda.json` (per-image vectors dumped in full), `logs/vlm_step037_tail_shape__5060ti_cuda.log`. Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part18.md` section 55 (part17 was at 193/200 lines). Original plan follows. tmux `sgn-vlm037`, tmux `sgn-vlm037`, log `logs/vlm_step037_tail_shape__5060ti_cuda.log`.** NEW `scripts/frontier/vlm_step037_tail_shape.py --modes compile --repeats 16 --n_eval 500` (194 lines). Card verified EMPTY at launch (48 MiB, 0%). **Why:** sections 49-54 attributed the excursion to 'per-launch host dispatch jitter' from a per-pass MEAN over 500 images -- a statistic that cannot distinguish the candidate mechanisms. step036's captured rep15 drew 7.08 vs 2.75 base = +2.17 s of extra prefill inside ONE pass (wall 51.6 s vs 48.6/49.9 neighbours, so the excess is real). One 2.2 s stall and 500 images each +4.3 ms produce the identical mean. `ev.forward()` already returns per-image prefill; the harness has been summing it away. Compile cell chosen first: at 6/20 it reaches the shape question ~6x cheaper than the captured cell. Departure gates carried over VERBATIM from step036 (compile >=4.5, capture >=3.0) so this arm cannot retune a threshold to a count. **Pre-registered (excess = pass_sum - median_pass_sum, gate 50%):** SPIKE = one image carries >=50% of the excess -> a one-off STALL, section 49's dispatch attribution FALSIFIED as stated, hunt moves to allocator reclaim / recapture / driver / page fault. SUSTAINED = the pass's per-image MEDIAN shifts by >=50% of the mean shift -> whole-pass state change (clock, cache residency, memory layout), also NOT per-launch jitter. JITTER = neither, excess spread with no dominant contributor -> section 49's mechanism SUPPORTED on evidence that can carry it. Zero departures -> shape question UNANSWERED, explicitly NOT resolved (section 51/53: a zero count bounds a rate, never a mechanism). Per-image vectors of every departure pass are dumped in full so the classification is re-readable without a rerun. **Cost:** 16 timed passes, ~25 min, no training. Write-up: `VLM_TRAJECTORY_part18.md` section 55.
- **vlm_step036 (R=20: pin the compiled-prefill excursion INCIDENCE, section 49 item 3): DONE 2026-08-15 20:13 IST, 5060ti_cuda,** **RESULT: compile 6/20 (rate UPHELD), reduce-overhead 1/20, verdict PARTIAL -- step033 called this same comparison WIN at R=5.** Compile prefill median 4.23 (4.17-6.35), tail 2.12; captured 2.75 (2.73-7.08), tail 4.33; tower 4.07/4.01 with tails 0.04/0.03 (50x stage asymmetry -> dispatch, not thermal/load). top1 0.6880 on all 40 passes. Pre-registered read APPLIED: >=4/20 upholds 'roughly 1 in 5' -- satisfied at 6/20 (95% CI ~12-54%). **Incidence and tail-depth point OPPOSITE ways:** capture cuts incidence 6/20 -> 1/20 and median prefill 1.47 ms, but its one excursion (7.08) is deeper than any compiled one (6.35) and worst-case e2e is WORSE captured (11.09) than compiled (10.43). Depth comparison is 1 draw vs 6 -- UNDERPOWERED, explicitly not claimed. **section 49's 5.36 ms worst case is superseded: >=6.35 compiled, >=7.08 captured** -- every budgeting line quoting 5.36 needs restating with '>='. Two in-run hypotheses raised and killed by the run itself (tower creep = early-rep warmup; escalating ramp = falsified when rep12 returned to base), both predicted in writing before the deciding reps. Next: tail-DEPTH arm (~R=100 or soak), not another incidence arm. Artifacts: `results/frontier/vlm_step033_cudagraph_bf16_compile-reduceoverhead_student_r20_n500_r20__5060ti_cuda.json`, write-up `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part17.md` section 54. Original plan follows. tmux `sgn-vlm036`, tmux `sgn-vlm036`, log `logs/vlm_step036_r20_incidence__5060ti_cuda.log`.** EXISTING `scripts/frontier/vlm_step033_cudagraph.py --modes compile reduce-overhead --towers student --ratio 0.25 --repeats 20 --n_eval 500` (no new script). Card verified EMPTY at launch (48 MiB, 0%). **Why:** this is the longest-standing open item in the VLM line and it is now load-bearing rather than optional. FOUR separate claims here have been wrong on small samples -- section 48 read a null as parity, section 50 read a rep-index coincidence as a positional lock and then two clean cells as the tail being ELIMINATED, section 53 read a 5/5 monotone run as within-process drift. Every one was an absence-or-rate claim made at R=5. The standing rule from section 51/53 is that R=5 fixes a median and says nothing about the rate of a rare event; R=20 is the smallest sample that separates a 1-in-5 rate from a 1-in-20 rate at all. **Pre-registered:** report incidence as a COUNT OUT OF 20 per cell with the raw draw list, plus median and MIN-MAX. Compile-cell incidence >= 4/20 -> section 49's 'roughly 1 in 5' is upheld and the excursion is a standing property of compiled prefill on this box. 1-3/20 -> the rate was overestimated from the early runs and every prior tail claim is restated at the lower rate. 0/20 -> the excursion is not a property of the compile cell at all and its earlier appearances need a confound hunt (thermal, co-tenancy, driver state), NOT another latency arm. **Independently, the graph cell settles section 51 finding 3:** capture drew 1 excursion in 15 at R=5, so any nonzero count here confirms 'cudagraphs REDUCE incidence, not eliminate it' on a sample that can actually carry the claim, and 0/20 leaves it at reduce-or-eliminate-undetermined rather than resolving it. **No absence claim will be made from a zero count at R=20 without stating the power explicitly.** **Cost:** 40 timed passes, ~40 min, no training. Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part17.md` section 54.
- **vlm_step035 (is the tower/width coupling caused by cudagraphs?): DONE 2026-08-15 -- PREMISE VOID; salvaged as a `compile`-mode companion table + 10 excursion draws, 5060ti_cuda, tmux `sgn-vlm035`, log `logs/vlm_step035_towercoupling__5060ti_cuda.log`.** Two sequential invocations of the EXISTING `scripts/frontier/vlm_step033_cudagraph.py` (no new script) with `--modes compile --towers student --repeats 5 --n_eval 500` at r=0.5 and r=0.25 -- identical to step034 in every respect EXCEPT the compile mode, so graph capture is the single variable. Card verified EMPTY at launch (48 MiB, 0%). **Why:** step034 finding 2. The vision tower is byte-identical across ratios and still moved 4.30 -> 3.94 with disjoint bands. Until that is explained, no per-stage latency number in this line can be published, and the tower -- now the larger half of e2e and the obvious next optimisation target -- cannot be worked on, because it is not clear what the tower number measures. **Pre-registered read:** GAP VANISHES (r=0.5 and r=0.25 tower medians agree within their bands under plain `compile`) -> the coupling is a cudagraph shared-pool artifact; the HYPOTHESIS is promoted, cudagraph per-stage attributions get a standing caveat, and e2e stays the only trustworthy cudagraph latency number. GAP SURVIVES -> it is NOT cudagraphs, and the harness has a stage-attribution bug predating section 50 that must be found and fixed before ANY stage-level millisecond is published. **No verdict is printed by design** (single `--modes` = no baseline cell); this arm emits cells and the comparison is against step034's table. Reported as median with MIN-MAX. **Cost:** 10 timed passes, no training. Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part17.md` section 53. **OUTCOME: the pre-registered read is VOID and was NOT applied.** Section 52 showed mid-run that there is no coupling to explain -- `--ratio` narrows the VISION tower, so a tower that moves with ratio is the width axis working as designed. Under the pre-registration the surviving gap would have been declared 'a harness measurement bug older than section 50' requiring the stage split to be rebuilt before publication: **a false alarm aimed at the foundation of the paper, avoided by reading three lines of source instead of trusting the run.** **RESULT (R=5, n=500, bf16, `compile`, student only; `load1` 0.91-1.00 across all 10 passes) -- median with MIN-MAX:** r=0.5 tower 4.37 (4.33-4.39) / prefill 5.03 (4.11-5.20) / e2e 9.42 / top1 0.6940 | r=0.25 tower 4.03 (3.98-4.06) / prefill 4.22 (4.16-4.96) / e2e 8.27 / top1 0.6880. **Read the right way round:** the tower gap between ratios IS the width effect -- 0.36 ms captured (step034) vs 0.34 ms uncaptured, so narrowing 0.5 -> 0.25 buys ~0.35 ms of tower in EITHER mode; and capture additionally buys 0.07-0.09 ms of tower plus ~2.3 ms of prefill. top1 identical to step034 in both cells, so the two modes compute the same thing. **Excursion draws, the one genuinely new datum:** r=0.5 prefill 4.11/4.17/5.03/5.06/5.20 (spread 1.09), r=0.25 prefill 4.53/4.19/4.16/4.96/4.22 (spread 0.80). r=0.25 keeps the section 49 shape (4.16-4.22 base, two upward departures, 2/5); r=0.5 does NOT -- two draws near 4.1 and three near 5.1, no base to depart from -- so it is reported as SPREAD and deliberately not classified as base+tail. Prefill medians also differ by ratio (5.03 vs 4.22) although `--ratio` does not touch the text model; at these spreads that is within instrument noise but it is a loose end for the R=20 arm to close. **RETRACTED mid-run, logged:** r=0.5's prefill rose monotonically 5/5 by rep index; I called it within-process rebuild drift, computed 1/120 from the rank order, and said that if it replicated every median back to section 44 needed re-reading. r=0.25 then ran 4.53/4.19/4.16/4.96/4.22 -- non-monotone, largest draw at rep3. KILLED at n=2. The probability was computed on the pattern that suggested the hypothesis, so it was not a probability. **Standing rule: a pattern seen inside a 5-draw cell is a hypothesis for the NEXT cell, never a finding.** **Method note:** sections 48/50/51 were over-reads of small samples; section 52 was a different failure -- draws and statistic both fine, but the arm asked what a knob did without reading what it was wired to, then spent GPU time explaining the answer. **Check the source before buying an experiment.** Results `results/frontier/vlm_step033_cudagraph_bf16_compile_student_r5_n500_nograph_r{0.5,0.25}__5060ti_cuda.json`, log pulled back. Write-up DONE: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part17.md` section 53. **INTERPRETATION VOID (see part16 section 52, written while this arm was still running):** the arm exists to explain a tower/width coupling that is not a coupling -- `--ratio` narrows the VISION TOWER, not the text model (`vlm_eval.py:91,103` + `vlm_width.py:41`), so the tower moving with ratio is the designed effect and section 51's Finding 2 is RETRACTED. The gap WILL survive under `--modes compile`, and section 51 pre-registered that outcome as 'a harness measurement bug older than section 50 -- the stage split needs rebuilding before any of it is published', which would be a false alarm at the foundation of the paper. Do NOT read the verdict as printed. The run remains useful as a compile-mode companion table at two ratios and as 10 more draws toward the overdue R=20 excursion count.
- **vlm_step034 (re-time the operating-points table under the 5-repeat protocol WITH cudagraphs): DONE 2026-08-15 -- CONTROL PASSED; FINDING 2 RETRACTED at section 52 (see below); the usable output is a re-timed operating-points table, 5060ti_cuda, tmux `sgn-vlm034`, log `logs/vlm_step034_optpoints__5060ti_cuda.log`.** Three sequential invocations of the EXISTING `scripts/frontier/vlm_step033_cudagraph.py` (no new script) with `--modes reduce-overhead --towers student --repeats 5 --n_eval 500` at r=0.5 / r=0.25 / r=0.125 (ckpts `vlm_step023_mlp_r0.5_r0.5_e25_t9352_n500_d6.pt`, `..._r0.25_..._e50_...`, `..._r0.125_..._e50_...`; note r=0.5's is e25, the others e50 -- the only 9352/n500/d6 checkpoints on disk for each ratio, so the epoch budget is NOT matched across rows and the accuracy column of this table is not a clean width comparison. It is a LATENCY re-timing; accuracy claims stay with sections 44/45/48). Card verified EMPTY at launch (48 MiB, 0%). **Why:** section 49 item 2, now upgraded by section 50 -- the operating-points table still carries SINGLE-DRAW numbers taken before both section 49's >=5-repeat rule and section 50's mode change to cudagraphs, so every millisecond in it is unpublishable twice over. **No verdict is printed by design:** a single `--modes` entry means there is no baseline cell to compare against, so this arm emits cells only. That is intended -- the comparison against default-inductor `compile` was already made at r=0.25 in step033 (WIN/WIN) and re-running it per ratio would spend 2x the passes to re-confirm a settled result. **Pre-registered read:** r=0.25 is the CONTROL -- it must reproduce step033's `reduce-overhead student` cell (prefill median 2.73, min-max 2.73-2.80, e2e 6.74, top1 0.6880 +/- the known ~1-2 image cross-process bf16 wobble). If it does NOT land inside that band, the other two rows are not trustworthy either and the arm is discarded rather than reported. Reported as median with MIN-MAX, never IQR. **Cost:** 15 timed passes, no training. Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part16.md` section 51. **RESULT (R=5, n_eval=500, bf16, `reduce-overhead`, student only; `load1` 0.72-1.08 across all 15 passes) -- median with MIN-MAX:** r=0.5 tower **4.30** (4.26-4.31) / prefill 2.73 (2.67-2.78) / e2e 7.04 / top1 0.6940 | r=0.25 tower **3.94** (3.90-3.96) / prefill 2.72 (2.69-2.78) / e2e 6.66 / top1 0.6880 | r=0.125 tower **3.81** (3.77-3.82) / prefill 2.72 (2.67-**3.60**) / e2e 6.52 / top1 0.6740. **CONTROL PASSED, so the arm is reportable:** r=0.25 here vs step033's cell -- prefill median 2.72 vs 2.73 (agree to 0.01 ms), min-max 2.69-2.78 vs 2.73-2.80 (overlapping), e2e 6.66 vs 6.74, top1 0.6880 vs 0.6880 (EXACT). **FINDING 1 and FINDING 2 as originally written here were RETRACTED on 2026-08-15 by section 52, before either reached the paper.** Both rested on the premise that `--ratio` rebuilds the TEXT student and leaves the vision tower byte-identical. **That premise is false: `--ratio` narrows the VISION tower and never touches the text model** -- `vlm_eval.py:91` binds `self.full` to `model.model.vision_model.encoder.layers`, `vlm_eval.py:103` `set_layers` assigns back into `vision_model.encoder.layers`, and `vlm_width.py:41` `build_width_student` slices `ev.full[:depth]`. Section 50's own ship table says it directly (tower params 85.05M -> 21.28M at r=0.25). **Corrected FINDING 2:** there is NO tower/width coupling and nothing unexplained. The tower medians move 4.30 / 3.94 / 3.81 with disjoint bands because the tower was NARROWED 4x -- the width axis working exactly as designed since section 44. **The additive tower+prefill decomposition is INTACT**, no per-stage attribution in this line needs a caveat, and section 50's 'the vision tower is now the larger half of e2e' stands as measured. **Corrected FINDING 1:** prefill IS flat at 2.72-2.73 across the three cells, but that is the NULL EXPECTATION rather than a discovery, because `--ratio` does not touch the text model. It is a harness sanity check that passed. The prefill-is-dispatch-bound claim rests on section 44's roofline and step022's token sweep, which are untouched; it gains nothing from this arm. **The usable result of step034 is therefore the re-timed table plus finding 3, not a mechanism.** **Table restated correctly:** narrowing the tower 0.5 -> 0.25 -> 0.125 buys tower 4.30 -> 3.94 -> 3.81 and e2e 7.04 -> 6.66 -> 6.52 at top1 0.6940 -> 0.6880 -> 0.6740. **FINDING 3 -- the excursion SURVIVES cudagraphs; section 50 AMENDED.** r=0.125 rep2 drew prefill **3.60** against a 2.67-2.72 base (+0.9 ms), one-sided upward, with tower and `load1` flat through it (3.82, 1.00) -- the section 49 shape, inside a CAPTURED cell. Section 50's heading claimed the tail disappears; it measured two clean cells and generalised. Corrected claim: **cudagraphs REDUCE excursion incidence, they do not eliminate it** -- cudagraph cells so far 1 in 15 (r=0.125 only; r=0.5 and r=0.25 clean at 5/5 each) vs 3 in 10 for the compiled student baseline. The dispatch attribution SURVIVES (a replay issues far less host work than N launches, so a lower rate is what that mechanism predicts); the elimination claim does not. That is the THIRD over-read of a handful of draws in this line (section 48 read a null as parity; section 50 read a rep-index coincidence as a positional lock, then read two clean cells as elimination). Named rule: **R=5 establishes a median and comes nowhere near establishing the ABSENCE of a rare event** -- absence claims need the R=20 arm (section 49 item 3), now overdue and load-bearing rather than optional. **Ship cell UNCHANGED:** r=0.25 -- prefill 2.72, tower 3.94, e2e 6.66, top1 0.6880, 42.6 MB bf16. r=0.125 buys 0.14 ms of e2e for -1.40pp top1 at an unmatched epoch budget; not a trade worth taking on this evidence. Results `results/frontier/vlm_step033_cudagraph_bf16_reduceoverhead_student_r5_n500_r{0.5,0.25,0.125}__5060ti_cuda.json`, log pulled back. Write-up DONE: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part16.md` section 51.
- **vlm_step033 (CUDA graphs on prefill: does removing per-launch host dispatch kill BOTH the median and the tail?): DONE 2026-08-15 -- **WIN / WIN** (both cells), 5060ti_cuda, tmux `sgn-vlm033`, log `logs/vlm_step033_cudagraph__5060ti_cuda.log`.** `scripts/frontier/vlm_step033_cudagraph.py --modes compile reduce-overhead --towers teacher student --repeats 5 --n_eval 500` (NEW, 202 lines, plus NEW shared library `scripts/frontier/vlm_graph.py`, 78 lines). Card verified EMPTY at launch (48 MiB, 0%). **Why:** section 49 item 1 -- the only remaining prefill lever, and the tail is itself the argument for it, since graph capture removes exactly the per-launch host dispatch that section 49's one-sided excursion is consistent with. **Route:** `torch.compile(mode='reduce-overhead')` (inductor cudagraph trees), not hand-rolled capture; shapes are static by construction (fixed prompt + 64 image tokens, nosplit), which is the precondition capture needs. **PREDICTED CORRECTNESS RISK FIRED ON FIRST SMOKE TEST, exactly as pre-registered in the docstring:** `RuntimeError: accessing tensor output of CUDAGraphs that has been overwritten by a subsequent run` -- because `VLMEval.choose()` deliberately REUSES one prefill KV cache across 10 teacher-forced label passes through the same text_model, and cudagraph outputs live in graph-owned memory a later replay overwrites. PyTorch RAISED rather than corrupting silently, which is the good case. **Fix:** new `vlm_graph.GraphEval` subclass -- `torch.compiler.cudagraph_mark_step_begin()` before each invocation plus a copy of the cache out of graph memory after it. **The clone sits OUTSIDE the timed prefill window (after the post-prefill sync), so it neither flatters nor penalises the prefill number; it is an artifact of this EVAL protocol, not of deployment** -- a deployed pipeline decodes inside the captured region and never hands a cache back to Python. `GraphEval` is used ONLY for graph modes, so the baseline cell is unchanged and the comparison stays one-variable. **Pre-registered vs the `compile` (default inductor) baseline:** WIN = prefill median AND max-median (tail) both fall by >0.03 ms (section 49's measured base-mode dispersion) -> dispatch hypothesis SUPPORTED; PARTIAL = median falls, tail survives -> dispatch is NOT the cause and section 49's cause hypothesis is FALSIFIED; NULL = neither moves -> prefill is not launch-bound and the axis closes; UNSAFE = top-1 moves >0.02 -> the cache clone failed, latency discarded regardless of value. Reported as median with MIN-MAX, never IQR (section 49's corrected reporting rule). **SMOKE TEST (R=2, n=20) PASSED and is already encouraging:** prefill 4.07 -> **2.90 ms** median with top-1 IDENTICAL (0.7000 both, acc delta 0.0000), e2e 8.05 -> 6.87 ms; verdict printed PARTIAL only because at R=2/n=20 neither cell drew an excursion so there was no tail to remove. **Known low-power caveat, stated up front:** with R=5 and ~1-in-5 incidence the baseline cell may draw zero excursions, in which case `tail = max - median` is small for BOTH cells and the WIN/PARTIAL split is uninformative on the tail axis -- the median result stands either way, and pinning incidence needs the R=20 arm (section 49 item 3). **Cost:** ~20 timed passes, no training. Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part15.md` section 50. **RESULT (R=5, n_eval=500, bf16, `load1` 0.80-1.11 across all 20 passes so nothing is a load artifact) -- median with MIN-MAX:** compile teacher 4.17 (4.11-4.25, tail 0.07), e2e 13.23 | **reduce-overhead teacher 2.80 (2.73-2.82, tail 0.02), e2e 11.71** | compile student 4.27 (4.18-**5.65**, tail **1.38**), e2e 8.36 | **reduce-overhead student 2.73 (2.73-2.80, tail 0.06), e2e 6.74**. Tower medians 9.06->8.90 (teacher), 4.06->4.01 (student). Verdicts printed verbatim: `reduce-overhead_teacher vs compile: top1 delta 0.0020 (gate 0.02)  median gain +1.38 ms  tail gain +0.05 ms -> WIN`; `reduce-overhead_student vs compile: top1 delta 0.0000 (gate 0.02)  median gain +1.54 ms  tail gain +1.32 ms -> WIN`. **Accuracy gate held on both, so the KV-cache clone worked and the latency is admissible.** **The student cell is the decisive test and it passes cleanly:** a baseline that excursed TWICE in five (5.43, 5.65 against a 4.18-4.27 base) becomes 2.73-2.80 across all five -- median and tail removed by one change. CONFIRMED: the compiled-prefill excursion is caused by per-launch host dispatch, not by GPU work; the finer mechanism stays HYPOTHESIS. **The teacher WIN is median-only** -- its baseline drew ZERO excursions, so its 0.07 'tail' was base-mode dispersion and shrinking it to 0.02 is the graph cell merely being tighter, not the excursion being removed. The low-power caveat above therefore fired on the teacher cell and did not fire on the student cell. **Incidence revised and now cell-dependent:** combined over step032+step033, teacher **1/10**, student **3/10** -- section 49's 'roughly 1 in 5' was one run's worth of evidence about a rate. Reinforces section 49 item 3 (R=20). **RETRACTED mid-run, logged so it is not resurrected:** I read a 'positional lock' into 5.39 (step030) / 5.36 (step032 rep1) / 5.43 (step033 rep1) and claimed the excursion was a deterministic function of position in the rebuild sequence; step033 **rep4** then excursed to 5.65 and killed it. Three draws, over-read. The one-sided SHAPE has survived every look; the PLACEMENT has not. **Ship numbers restated on cudagraphs (within-process, R=5):** tower 25.72->4.01 (6.41x), prefill 13.58->**2.73** (**4.97x**), e2e 39.30->**6.74** (**5.83x**), weights 340 MB fp32 -> 42.6 MB bf16 (8.00x), top1 0.7040->0.6880 (-1.60pp). **Section 49's 5.0 ms prefill bar now passes at EVERY draw, not four of five** (max 2.80); the 'state it with the worst draw' caveat is discharged for this config. e2e improves 8.26 -> 6.74 median, a further 1.52 ms. Student top1 reads 0.6880 vs 0.6860 (step032) / 0.6840 (step030) -- 1-2 images of 500, the cross-process bf16+compile nondeterminism of section 47; identical across all 5 repeats within the run. **Prefill is no longer the leading cost: 2.73 ms prefill vs a 4.01 ms tower, so the vision tower is now the larger half of e2e** and further latency work should target it. Script trimmed 202 -> 200 lines post-run (docstring sentence dropped, one print merged); the copy that ran is functionally identical. Results `results/frontier/vlm_step033_cudagraph_bf16_compile-reduceoverhead_teacher-student_r5_n500__5060ti_cuda.json`, log pulled back. - **vlm_step032 (measure the harness's own latency resolution: R repeats per cell): DONE 2026-08-15 18:05 IST. VERDICT: the instrument is FINE; the STATISTIC I pre-registered was not.** Card verified EMPTY at launch, `load1` flat at 0.94-1.06 across all 20 timed passes, so nothing below is a load artifact. **Both automatic prefill-invariance gates PASS by two orders of magnitude** -- fp32:eager |teacher-student| = 0.02 ms, bf16:compile = 0.01 ms, against a 0.5 ms bar. The LM is byte-identical across towers so this delta is zero by construction; the harness reproduces zero to 0.01 ms. **Every stage is deterministic to ~0.1% EXCEPT ONE: compiled prefill, which has a rare ONE-SIDED UPWARD EXCURSION.** Base mode is tighter than anything else in the run (+/-0.03 ms, 0.7%); one repeat in five jumps +0.45 ms (bf16_compile_teacher rep0 4.67 vs 4.18-4.23) or +1.13 ms (bf16_compile_student rep1 5.36 vs 4.17-4.23). Never downward, never the tower -- including the COMPILED tower, which is as tight as the eager one. **This explains section 47 exactly and RETIRES its hypothesis.** step030 measured the ship cell at prefill 5.39 / e2e 9.41; this arm's rep1 measures 5.36 / 9.40 -- agreement to 0.01 ms -- while its other four repeats sit at 8.19-8.27. step030 did not catch a slower configuration, it took ONE sample and landed in the tail. **The +/-0.5 ms SYMMETRIC FLOOR of section 47 is RETIRED:** it is not a floor on all measurements, it is a one-sided heavy tail on COMPILED PREFILL ONLY at ~1-in-5 incidence. Section 47's four within-pair deltas (+0.40/-0.52/+0.54/+1.21) are exactly what that tail produces when differencing two single draws. Distribution SHAPE is CONFIRMED; the CAUSE (host-side dispatch jitter on the launch-bound stage, exposed once compile shrinks the GPU work) remains HYPOTHESIS. **SELF-CORRECTION -- the automated verdict is an artifact of my own pre-registration.** The script printed `worst relative IQR 1.0% vs bar 5.0% -> RESOLVABLE`, which is true and misleading: **IQR is robust to tails BY CONSTRUCTION, so it discards precisely the events this arm existed to find** (a 1-in-5 excursion sits at the 80th percentile and never enters the interquartile range). I pre-registered a robust statistic to hunt a tail -- a category error, same species as section 45 (bar finer than its own SE) and section 48 (null read as parity). **CORRECTED REPORTING RULE for this line: quote median with MIN-MAX, never IQR.** **Ship numbers restated on 5 draws instead of 1** (all ratios within-process): tower 25.72 -> 4.04 ms = **6.37x**; prefill 13.58 -> **4.22 ms = 3.22x** median, worst draw 5.36; e2e 39.30 -> **8.26 ms = 4.76x** median, range 8.19-9.40; params 85.05M -> 21.28M; weights 340 MB fp32 -> **42.6 MB bf16 = 8.00x**; top1 0.6860 identical across all 5 repeats. **The 5.0 ms prefill WIN bar holds at the median and at 4 of 5 draws but NOT at the worst draw (5.36) -- state it that way.** The e2e claim is unaffected: even the tail draw (9.40) beats the compiled teacher's 13.27 and the fp32-eager teacher's 39.30 outright. Also: ship cell top1 0.6860 here vs 0.6840 in step030 = 1 image in 500, consistent with compile+bf16 being non-bit-deterministic ACROSS processes while perfectly stable WITHIN one. **UNBLOCKS:** section 47 blocked CUDA graphs, `torch.export` and the section 32 prologue ablation on the grounds that the instrument could not resolve them -- **that block is LIFTED**, the base mode resolves 0.03 ms. The standing requirement is now procedural, not instrumental: **>=5 repeats, report median and min-max, treat any single-draw latency number as unpublishable.** Script `scripts/frontier/vlm_step032_repeat.py` (187 lines), result `results/frontier/vlm_step032_repeat_fp32eager-bf16compile_r5_n500__5060ti_cuda.json`, log `logs/vlm_step032_repeat__5060ti_cuda.log`. Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part15.md` section 49 (part14 closed at 176 lines).** `scripts/frontier/vlm_step032_repeat.py --cells fp32:eager bf16:compile --repeats 5 --n_eval 500` (NEW script, 187 lines, smoke-tested on box before launch: `--smoke_test` PASS, all three gates fired correctly). **Why:** section 47 found the ship cell's e2e moving 7.95 -> 9.41 ms (+18%) between two runs of an IDENTICAL config on a clean card, and inferred a ~+/-0.5 ms repeatability floor from an internal control (the LM is byte-identical between teacher and student cells, so within-pair prefill deltas are pure instrument error). That floor is ~4% of a 13 ms fp32 prefill but **~25% of a 4-5 ms compiled one**, i.e. the 5.0 ms WIN bar sits inside its own noise. This arm MEASURES the floor instead of inferring it. **Three changes, exactly as section 47 pre-registered:** (a) R=5 repeats of every cell inside one process, reported as median + IQR, with the model REBUILT every repeat -- reusing the compiled object would measure one compile session five times and report variance zero; (b) CPU load1 and per-repeat wall-clock logged, because an empty GPU is demonstrably not a sufficient validity gate for a dispatch-bound stage; (c) the teacher-minus-student prefill delta promoted to an AUTOMATIC gate (bar 0.5 ms) -- it is zero by construction and would have flagged both the step030 duplicate and the ship-cell spread before any number was written down. **Pre-registered:** worst relative IQR over all cells/stages <= 5% -> the 7.95-9.41 range collapses to a point and section 47's WIN is restored or retired ON EVIDENCE; > 5% -> absolute latency on this box is NOT publishable and the paper quotes RATIOS with intervals, never ms. Either way every downstream latency claim (CUDA graphs, torch.export, the section 32 prologue ablation) is unblocked or correctly killed. **Early signal from the smoke test:** fp32-eager repeats are near bit-identical (IQR 0.0-0.3%, prefill gate 0.07 ms PASS), consistent with section 47's finding that fp32 and bf16-EAGER reproduce bit-identically while both bf16-COMPILE cells moved -- so the variance, if any, is expected to be concentrated in the compile cells, which is precisely where the 4-5 ms numbers live. **Cost:** ~20 timed passes, no training. Results: teacher 0.7145; d6 x r=0.25 **0.6785** (was 0.6840 at n=500), gap **-3.60pp**; d6 x r=0.125 **0.6420** (was 0.6680), gap **-7.25pp**. **All three paired McNemar tests SIGNIFICANT** at SE~1.04pp: teacher vs r=0.25 592 discordant 332/260 **p=0.0035**; teacher vs r=0.125 607 discordant 376/231 **p<1e-5**; r=0.25 vs r=0.125 381 discordant 227/154 **p=0.00022**. Every one of these was NULL at n=500 (p=0.148, p=0.451). **Pre-registered branch SIGNIFICANT fires, but wider than pre-registered:** the teacher-vs-r=0.25 test also fires, so there is NO parity arm left. **Section 45's CONFIRMED parity was a TYPE-II ERROR, not a finding** — it was inferred from nulls produced by a test whose SE (2.05pp) was wider than the 2.0pp bar it was testing, i.e. absence of significance read as absence of effect. The width axis carries a real, MONOTONE capacity cost at both steps. Point estimates were sample-dependent too (r=0.125 moved -2.60pp, >2 SE), so no n=500 VALUE from this line should be quoted, only directions. **MECHANISM, and it bounds the cross-n reading:** `sample_images` sets `per = max(1, n // len(wnids))`, so n=500 draws 50/class and n=2000 draws 200/class -- `rng.sample` is called with a DIFFERENT k, the RNG state diverges at the first class, and the n=2000 set is therefore a DIFFERENT balanced draw, NOT a superset of the n=500 one. So the n=500 -> n=2000 movement confounds resolution with sample identity and must not be reported as 'the gap grew'. What is NOT confounded is everything INSIDE the n=2000 column: all three McNemar tests are paired image-by-image on the same 2000 images against a bit-identical teacher. Correct reading: n=2000 SUPERSEDES n=500 as the estimate (4x data, SE 2.05 -> 1.01pp); the two are not a controlled before/after. **Untouched:** the bytes argument is counted, not measured -- 21.28M params / 42.6 MB bf16 = **8.00x fewer bytes** than the fp32 teacher's 340 MB stands. The decision to ship r=0.25 rather than r=0.125 also survives and is now better supported: declined on bytes when its accuracy cost was unproven, that cost is now CONFIRMED at -3.65pp. **SHIP CONFIG RESTATED: d6 x r=0.25 bf16+compile, 8.00x fewer bytes, top-1 0.6785 = -3.60pp below teacher (CONFIRMED, n=2000). NOT parity — any paper text claiming parity for this line must be rewritten.** Ran via the rerouted `vlm_step028_runtime.py` path, both ratios, results `results/frontier/vlm_step028_runtime_fp32_eager_n2000_step031r025__5060ti_cuda.json` and `..._step031r0125__5060ti_cuda.json`.
