Caught by reading the script before launching rather than trusting the queued command. REROUTED through `vlm_step028_runtime.py`, which already loads a checkpoint, builds the width student via `build_width_student` and evaluates it: `--dtypes fp32 --compile eager --n_eval 2000 --ratio R --ckpt <R's 50ep checkpoint>`, run once per ratio (_step031r025, _step031r0125 tag suffixes). Identical measurement, already-validated harness, zero new code, and the teacher is re-scored in BOTH passes which gives a free consistency check. Not a timing arm, so the fp32-eager cells are all that is needed and contention would not invalidate it. **LESSON: a pre-registered command is not a checked command -- pre-registration fixes the HYPOTHESIS, it does not verify the CODE PATH.** Original pre-registration (unchanged, still the standard this is judged against) follows. `scripts/frontier/vlm_step023_mlp_width.py --student_depth 6 --ratios 0.25 0.125 --epochs 0 --n_eval 2000` -- **eval-only, NO retraining**: both existing checkpoints (step027 r=0.25, step029 r=0.125) are re-scored against the same teacher on a 4x larger sample. **Why this is now the top item in the line:** section 45 showed the script's WIN bar (teacher - 2.0pp) is FINER than one binomial SE of its own measurement (2.05pp at n=500, p~0.70). Every width verdict since step024 has therefore been a coin-flip label on a point estimate, and three arms (0.7040 / 0.6840 / 0.6680) now sit inside one SE with EVERY pairwise McNemar null (p=0.148, p=0.451). No further width or depth arm at n=500 can return an interpretable answer -- it will read 'not significant' regardless of what is true. n=2000 halves SE to ~1.02pp, which is the minimum that makes the bar the script already uses mean anything. **Pre-registered:** if r=0.25 vs r=0.125 goes SIGNIFICANT at n=2000 -> a real capacity cost exists between them, section 45's CONFIRMED parity narrows to 'parity down to r=0.25 only', and the 100ep ladder at r=0.125 becomes worth running; if it stays NULL at SE~1.02pp -> parity at 0.209x tower params is established at 2x the resolution and the width axis closes on the ACCURACY argument as well as the bytes one. Either outcome is decisive, which is what n=500 could not deliver. **Cost:** ~4x one eval pass, no training, so it is the cheapest high-information arm left. **Guard:** the teacher must reproduce its own top1 within 2.0pp on the larger sample or the 500-image teacher line is itself sample-dependent and every prior cross-arm comparison needs re-examining. **Card check before launch** -- this is not a timing arm so contention does not invalidate it, but it queues behind step030, which IS a timing arm and needs an empty card. Tower 25.72 -> 3.28 ms = 7.84x (arch 2.70x x dtype 2.90x = 7.83, near-orthogonal; dtype gain shrinks 3.16x -> 2.90x as the tower shrinks and goes relatively more overhead-bound). The two levers hit DIFFERENT stages: compile moves prefill (-31% fp32) and not the tower (-3%); bf16 mirrors it (tower 3.16x, prefill -20%); neither alone broke 8.27 ms. **Two material caveats:** (1) COMPILE, not bf16, perturbs numerics - both bf16-eager cells reproduce fp32 exactly (0.7040 / 0.6880) while both compile cells drift (teacher -0.60pp, student +0.20pp), inside the 2.0pp guard so the verdict stands but the drift is real and its sign is inconsistent; (2) the compile is LEAKY - dynamo hit `config.recompile_limit (8)` on transformers' `output_capturing.wrapper` and fell back on part of the graph, so 4.21 ms is a FLOOR on a partially-compiled model. Also negative: compile makes the bf16 tower SLOWER (8.13 -> 8.99, +11%) - guard cost on a stage with no dispatch overhead to recover. Watch-item RESOLVED as variance: the fp32 compile pair's 1.18 ms student-under-teacher prefill gap did not repeat in bf16 (4.21 vs 4.28). Retro-validates step027's quarantine: fp32-eager student tower re-measured 9.52 ms vs section 43's uncontended 9.53 ms. **Ship config: student bf16+compile - 7.95 ms e2e, 21.28M params, 42.6 MB bf16 weights (8.00x fewer bytes than the fp32 teacher), top1 0.6860 = -1.80pp.** Tower latency work is FINISHED (3.67 ms tower vs 4.28 ms prefill); further narrowing is a params/bytes argument only. Results: `results/frontier/vlm_step028_runtime_fp32-bf16_eager-compile_n500__5060ti_cuda.json`.** Was blocked on a TEAMMATE job holding the card - PID 151328, 12940 MiB, 100% util as of 2026-08-14. A timing arm needs an EMPTY card, and step027 already proved why: its own latency block had to be thrown away because that same job landed mid-run. Teammate processes are never killed; a persistent monitor polls `nvidia-smi --query-compute-apps` every 120s and fires when the card clears.)** `scripts/frontier/vlm_step028_runtime.py` (189 lines, `--help` + `--smoke_test` both passed on the box 2026-08-14). **Checkpoint repointed after section 45:** defaults are now `--ratio 0.25` on `vlm_step023_mlp_r0.25_r0.25_e50_t9352_n500_d6.pt` (the step027 student) rather than step025's r=0.5 one, because section 45 moved the drone operating point to d6 x r=0.25 (0.2502x tower params at -2.00pp, McNemar p=0.4437). The VERDICT is stated on prefill and is tower-independent either way, so this does not move the bar - but the tower cells should be timed on the tower we actually intend to ship. Launch: `--n_eval 500` (defaults give the full 2x2x2 grid). **Motivation - section 44, CONFIRMED by two independent methods:** measured against the fp32 roofline the pipeline's two halves are bound by DIFFERENT things. Vision tower = 50-62% of roofline (teacher 212.8 GFLOP / 16.37 ms ideal vs 26.21 measured; step025 54.7%; step026 50.8%) so it is COMPUTE-bound, which is exactly why step025/026's FLOP cuts bought real wall-time. LM prefill = **10.5%** (19.1 GFLOP / 1.47 ms ideal vs 14.03 measured), so ~90% of it is kernel-launch and Python dispatch across 30 thin layers at batch 1, seq ~90 - not arithmetic. **step022 confirmed this independently without meaning to:** varying ONLY image token count, prefill went 14.10 ms @ 64 tokens -> 13.90 ms @ 4 tokens, i.e. a 16x sequence cut bought 1.4%. A compute-bound prefill cannot do that. **Two roadmap consequences already banked:** (a) roadmap item 4 (learned token selection) is DEAD as a latency lever - premise falsified by data we already had; it may still run as an accuracy/memory study but never again on a latency justification; (b) end-to-end is CAPPED at ~2.8x with prefill pinned near 14 ms however far the tower is squeezed - step025 hit 1.61x of that cap, step026's much harder cut only 1.70x - so **the tower is FINISHED as a latency axis** (it remains the params/bytes axis, which is what the drone goal actually cares about). **Compounding rule satisfied:** bf16/compile change dispatch cost and memory traffic, step025 changed layer count and MLP width - disjoint mechanisms, disjoint signal paths, no isolation ablation owed. The arm still measures BOTH towers under ALL four runtimes so orthogonality is MEASURED rather than assumed. **Pre-registered on PREFILL** (the thing section 44 diagnosed): WIN iff best accuracy-clean prefill < 5.0 ms -> overhead diagnosis CONFIRMED, end-to-end cap lifts ~2.8x -> ~7x, and further tower work becomes worth doing again; PARTIAL in [5.0, 10.0) ms -> overhead real but only partly removable by these two levers, residual must be named (CUDA graphs, ONNX/TensorRT, batching); LOSS >= 10.0 ms -> **the overhead diagnosis is FALSIFIED**, prefill is structurally bound, and section 44's consequence 3 is wrong. A LOSS overturns the section that motivated the arm, which is what makes it a real prediction. **Accuracy guard, per cell:** bf16 is a numerics change, so any cell more than 2.0pp below its own fp32-eager counterpart is reported as an ACCURACY REGRESSION regardless of latency, and regressed cells are excluded from the verdict - speed bought with broken numerics is not a win. **Cost: no training** (inference-only re-timing, reuses the step027 checkpoint), so this is the cheapest high-information arm left in the line. Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part12.md` section 44 (pre-registration) -> section 46 (result).
- **vlm_step027 (the epoch ladder step026's PARTIAL pre-registered: does d6 x r=0.25 recover with 2x the budget?): DONE (2026-08-14, 5060ti_cuda). VERDICT: WIN - it recovers, and step026's knee is WITHDRAWN as a budget artifact.** GATE PASS: teacher 0.7040, fifth identical reproduction. Distilled **0.6840 = -2.00pp**, landing exactly on the pre-registered WIN bar, +59.80pp over naive 0.0860, at **21.28M = 0.2502x** tower params. Final rel_mse 0.1776 / cosine 0.9077 vs step026's 0.2234 / 0.8824, and **still falling at ep50** (0.1786 -> 0.1776), so 0.6840 is a floor on this architecture, not a ceiling. Epochs 1-25 reproduced step026 bit-for-bit (ep5 0.3237, ep10 0.2900, ep25 0.2239 vs 0.2234 - ~1e-4 nondeterministic reduction drift), so only ep26-50 carried new information. **McNemar, the first paired test in this line: p = 0.4437** (278 both-right, 84 both-wrong, 74 teacher-only, 64 student-only) - the -2.00pp gap is NOT distinguishable from zero, and roadmap item 3 is CLOSED. The 138 discordant images say the two towers are equally accurate and are NOT the same function; aggregate parity is not behavioural equivalence. **Drone operating point moves from step025's d6 x r=0.5 (0.3335x) to d6 x r=0.25 (0.2502x)** - a further 25% off the flash footprint for no measured accuracy cost. **LATENCY FROM THIS RUN IS INVALID and is not quoted:** a teammate job landed on the shared card mid-run and the eval ran under contention - student prefill reads 25.0 ms vs the teacher's 13.72 ms on an IDENTICAL text stack, and tower 26.17 -> 29.01 ms (0.90x, student 'slower' than its own teacher). Both are physically impossible and are pure contention. Use step026's uncontended 9.53 ms / 2.75x for this architecture - valid because wall-time depends on the architecture, not the weights, and step026 timed the same d6 x r=0.25 tower on a free card. Accuracy is unaffected (deterministic eval). Results: `results/frontier/vlm_step023_mlp_width_r0.25_e50_t9352_n500_d6__5060ti_cuda.json`. Same script, `--student_depth 6 --ratios 0.25 --epochs 50 --n_train 9352` - ONLY epochs change vs step026 (25 -> 50), so this is strictly one-variable. **Motivation:** step026 landed -5.00pp (PARTIAL) with the fit STILL FALLING at ep25 (rel_mse 0.2234, vs step025's 0.1915 and step024's 0.1570), trailing step025's curve by ~0.03 at every matched epoch from ep10 on. That is the exact signature step023 showed before step024 falsified it as a budget artifact worth +18.20pp - so -5.00pp is an UPPER BOUND on the loss, not a floor, and the pre-registered PARTIAL text said this outcome "buys an epoch ladder, not a verdict". This is that ladder. **Pre-registered:** WIN (>= teacher - 2.0pp, i.e. >= 0.6840) -> the -5.00pp WAS budget, 0.250x reaches parity, and 0.250x/2.75x tower becomes the drone operating point over step025's 0.333x; PARTIAL but improved (0.6540 < distilled < 0.6840) -> capacity and budget BOTH bind, and the knee stands roughly where step026 put it; PARTIAL and flat (<= 0.6540 + noise) -> **capacity binds, not budget** - this is the outcome that CONFIRMS step026's knee as a real capacity limit rather than an artifact, and closes the width axis below r=0.5 at d6. **Byproduct that closes roadmap item 3:** the `correct` per-image hit-vector fix was written after step025 launched but was NEVER SYNCED to the 5060ti - the box was still running the pre-fix 189-line script, so step026 has no vectors either and McNemar stayed unavailable. Script synced 2026-08-14 (196 lines, `--help` smoke-tested on the box); **step027 is the first arm that will actually record per-image predictions**, making a paired McNemar test possible against the teacher for the first time in this line. Lesson recorded: an edit to a local script is not a change to the experiment until it reaches the machine that runs it. Launched on an empty card (48 MiB / 16311 MiB, no sgn sessions - no teammate job displaced). Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part12.md` **section 44** (part11 closed full at 196 lines; the roofline decomposition anticipated as section 44 was never written, so step027 takes that number).

- **vlm_step026 (COMPOUND, harder: d6 x r=0.25 - how far does the width factor stretch once depth is already halved?): DONE (2026-08-14, 5060ti_cuda). VERDICT: PARTIAL - the knee is LOCATED, and it lies between r=0.5 and r=0.25.** Gate PASS (teacher d12 0.7040, fourth identical reproduction, so step023/024/025/026 all sit on one comparison line). Naive 0.0860 (chance) -> distilled **0.6540 = -5.00pp vs teacher**, gain over naive +56.80pp. Params **21.28M = 0.2502x** (predicted 0.25x, matched). Tower **26.21 -> 9.53 ms = 2.75x**; prefill unchanged 13.77 -> 14.03 ms so **end-to-end 39.98 -> 23.56 ms = 1.70x**. Final rel_mse 0.2234 / cosine 0.8824. **Marginal economics, which is the finding:** going r=0.5 -> r=0.25 buys 0.083x params and 0.35x tower speed for **3.80pp**, where step025's own marginal step bought 0.33x params and 1.12x speed for nothing. The compound stops being free between the two. **step025's d6 x r=0.5 (0.3335x, 2.40x tower, 1.61x end-to-end, parity) stays the recommended drone operating point**; r=0.25 is only for a hard flash budget. **Second finding, on the latency axis:** 2.75x tower yields just 1.70x end-to-end vs step025's 2.40x -> 1.61x, i.e. extra tower speed barely survives to end-to-end now that prefill dominates the remainder - **further tower squeezing has diminishing end-to-end return, and the next real latency win must come from outside the tower** (section 32 prologue) or from the token count. **PARTIAL is the pre-registered 'closes nothing' outcome and that reading is honest:** fit still falling at ep25 (0.2234 vs step025's 0.1915, step024's 0.1570), trailing step025's curve by ~0.03 at every matched epoch - the same signature step023 showed before step024 falsified it as a budget artifact - so -5.00pp is an UPPER BOUND on the loss at this budget, not a floor for r=0.25. Unlike step023 the budget here is d6's, r=0.5's AND the compound's own parity budget, so the number is interpretable as stated. Per-image predictions still not recorded (roadmap item 3 stays open; folding it in would have made this two-variable against step025). Results: `results/frontier/vlm_step023_mlp_width_r0.25_e25_t9352_n500_d6__5060ti_cuda.json`. Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part11.md` section 43. **ORIGINAL PRE-REGISTRATION:** Same script, `--student_depth 6 --ratios 0.25 --epochs 25 --n_train 9352`, every other knob identical to step025 (lr 1e-4, bs 8, seed 42, n_eval 500, magnitude-ranked slice init, teacher = FULL d12 tower). **Motivation:** step025 CONFIRMED the two levers compose (0.333x params at 2.40x, parity), and step023's r=0.25 arm (-26.80pp) was starved by exactly the ~16x budget deficit that step024 falsified for r=0.5 - so its number is uninterpretable and must NOT be cited as a floor. This run gives r=0.25 the budget both winning arms needed. **Predicted:** 6 x 3.54M = **21.2M tower params (0.25x)**. **Pre-registered:** WIN (>= teacher - 2.0pp) -> 0.25x becomes the new drone operating point; PARTIAL (<=10pp) -> the compound has begun to pay for itself, and the arm LOCATES THE KNEE between r=0.5 and r=0.25 rather than closing anything; LOSS (>10pp) -> bounds how far the width factor stretches once depth is already halved, and is only meaningful because this is the budget at which r=0.5, d6 and d6xr0.5 all reached parity. Launched on an empty card (48 MiB / 16311 MiB, no sgn sessions - no teammate job displaced). Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part11.md` section 43.

- **vlm_step025 (COMPOUND: depth d6 x MLP width r=0.5 - do the two levers multiply?): DONE (2026-08-13, 5060ti_cuda). VERDICT: WIN - the levers COMPOSE, CONFIRMED.** Gate PASS (teacher d12 0.7040, bit-identical to step023/024, so all three arms sit on one comparison line and the student is the only variable). Naive 0.0920 (at chance, as every naive structural cut to this tower has been) -> distilled **0.6920 = -1.20pp vs teacher**, inside both the pre-registered 2.0pp bar and the ~2.05pp binomial SE at n_eval=500, so it is **PARITY, not superiority** (per-image predictions still not recorded, so McNemar remains unavailable - deliberately not bundled, since folding it in would have made this run two-variable against step024). Gain over naive **+60.00pp**. **Both factors held at their measured value: 28.36M tower params = exactly 0.333x (the predicted 0.5 x 0.667), and tower 26.20 -> 10.89 ms = 2.40x** against a predicted 2.00 x 1.28 = 2.56x - slightly sublinear, expected once the tower stops dominating its own fixed overheads, NOT a sign the cuts interfere. **The orthogonality argument survived its own falsification test:** this arm was pre-registered so that a LOSS would have impugned the compounding rule's paper-wide "disjoint W_* => safe" criterion; instead two cuts argued orthogonal a priori (whole layers vs intermediate channels inside surviving layers) compounded to the product of their param savings with no accuracy interaction, at the same budget each needed alone. That is now MEASURED, not merely argued. **Caveat, pointing the same way step023 did:** final rel_mse 0.1915 vs step024's 0.1570, still falling at ep25 (0.2069/0.2002/0.1974/0.1945/0.1915) - the compound student ran ~0.04 behind the width-only fit curve at every matched epoch yet still reached parity, so this arm is budget-limited too and more epochs are cheap upside, not a requirement. **NEW DRONE OPERATING POINT: 0.333x tower params at 2.40x tower latency, at teacher parity** - first arm in this line to move both drone axes at once. Results: `results/frontier/vlm_step023_mlp_width_r0.5_e25_t9352_n500_d6__5060ti_cuda.json` (the `_d6` TAG suffix appears only when student_depth != depth, so step023/024's width-only filenames stay byte-identical). Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part10.md` section 42. **ORIGINAL PRE-REGISTRATION:** Same script as step023/024, `--student_depth 6 --ratios 0.5 --epochs 25 --n_train 9352`, every other knob identical (lr 1e-4, bs 8, seed 42, n_eval 500, magnitude-ranked slice init, teacher = FULL d12 tower so distillation targets are unchanged and the student is scored against the same 0.7040 teacher as step023/024). **Motivation:** both factors reached PARITY alone at exactly this budget - d6 at 25ep/9.3k (step004) and r=0.5 at 25ep/9.3k (step024) - and they are ORTHOGONAL under the compounding rule: depth removes whole layers, width removes intermediate channels inside the layers that survive. Different params, different signal paths, so no isolation ablation is owed. **Predicted:** 6 x 4.72M = **28.3M tower params (0.333x)** and roughly 2.0x * 1.28x ~ **2.5x tower latency**. This is the only arm that moves BOTH drone axes at once, and both ingredients are already CONFIRMED individually. **Pre-registered:** WIN iff distilled >= teacher - 2.0pp -> the levers compose and 0.333x/2.5x becomes the new drone operating point; PARTIAL (<=10pp) -> they compose but not for free, and the budget question reopens (step024's own lesson is that a starved compound reads as a false LOSS, so a PARTIAL here demands an epoch ladder before any closure); LOSS (>10pp) is the informative failure -- it would mean the orthogonality argument, which is ARGUED from disjoint parameter sets and never MEASURED, does not survive contact, and that the two cuts interact through the residual stream they share. That is a real falsifiable prediction, not a safe bet. **Known gap, accepted:** per-image predictions still are not recorded, so if this arm also lands near teacher, McNemar is again unavailable and the result must again be read as PARITY rather than superiority. Fixing that is queued as its own change and was deliberately NOT bundled into this run, which is otherwise a strict one-variable extension of step024. Filename carries a `_d6` suffix (added to TAG for compound runs only, so the width-only step023/024 filenames already quoted in write-ups stay byte-identical). Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part10.md` section 42.
- **vlm_step024 (tower MLP width at d6's WINNING budget - the rematch step023 demands): DONE (2026-08-13, 5060ti_cuda). VERDICT: WIN - distilled 0.7320 vs teacher 0.7040 (+2.80pp, i.e. PARITY within noise), naive 0.1160, gain over naive +61.60pp (largest in this line), 56.72M (0.667x), tower 26.16 -> 20.45 ms (1.28x), final rel_mse 0.1570. GATE PASS, teacher 0.7040 identical to step023's so the two runs are directly comparable and the ONLY variable is the training budget. **step023's LOSS is CONFIRMED a budget artifact**: same slice, same init, same seed, same eval, 15ep/1k -> 25ep/9.3k moved the arm -15.40pp -> +2.80pp. The "depth strictly dominates width" claim step023 deliberately withheld is now positively FALSIFIED, not merely unproven. Read +2.80pp as PARITY not superiority - at n_eval=500, p~0.70 the binomial SE is ~2.05pp so it is ~1.4 SE, inside noise. **Consequence:** width is a live lever and is ORTHOGONAL to depth (depth drops whole layers, width drops intermediate channels within surviving layers - different params, different signal paths), so compounding d6 x r=0.5 is permitted with no isolation ablation -> vlm_step025, target ~0.33x tower params. **Pareto split now sharper: width is the params/bytes lever (0.667x params, only 1.28x speed), depth is the latency lever (0.5x params, 2.0x speed)** - the compound arm is the one that serves both drone axes. Results: `results/frontier/vlm_step023_mlp_width_r0.5_e25_t9352_n500__5060ti_cuda.json` (step024 reuses the step023 script, so the TAG `e25_t9352` is what distinguishes the file - exactly why the TAG was added to the checkpoint path before launch). ORIGINAL PRE-REGISTRATION:** Same script as step023, `--ratios 0.5 --epochs 25 --n_train 9352`, every other knob identical. **Motivation: step023's LOSS is a budget artifact, not an axis verdict** - step004's d6 ladder shows d6 itself scored -18pp at 10ep/2k, -6pp at 15ep/6k and only reached PARITY at 25ep/9.3k, while step023 gave width just 15ep/1k. 25ep/9352 is exactly d6's parity budget, so this makes the depth-vs-width comparison matched-budget. **Pre-registered:** WIN iff distilled >= teacher - 2.0pp (width joins depth as a live lever, and the two are orthogonal - different params - so compounding is permitted); PARTIAL (<=10pp) = "recoverable, but depth is the better spend at equal budget"; LOSS at THIS budget is the one that CLOSES the axis, and only then is the closure CONFIRMED rather than budget-limited. Also fixed before launch: the checkpoint path now carries the run TAG, since unconditional `torch.save` to a ratio-only path would have silently overwritten the very 15ep weights step023 quotes. Launched on an empty card (48 MiB / 16311 MiB - no teammate job displaced). Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part10.md` section 40.

- **vlm_step023 (can the vision tower's MLP WIDTH be sliced and distilled back?): DONE (2026-08-13, 5060ti_cuda, d12 stock, 15 ep / 1000 train / 500 eval). VERDICT: both ratios LOSS by the pre-registered absolute bar - but the bar is NOT the finding, and the axis is NOT closed.** Validity gate PASS (teacher d12 0.7040 vs anchor 0.711; tower 85.05M, mlp intermediate 3072). Cells: **r=0.5 (3072->1536)** naive 0.1160 -> distilled 0.5500 (-15.40pp vs teacher, **+43.40pp over naive**), 56.72M (0.667x), tower 26.22 -> 20.41 ms (1.28x); **r=0.25 (3072->768)** naive 0.0860 -> distilled 0.4360 (-26.80pp, +35.00pp over naive), 42.56M (0.500x), tower 26.22 -> 17.76 ms (1.48x). Both naive controls at chance, as every naive structural cut to this tower has been. **Why the LOSS is a BUDGET artifact (HYPOTHESIS, and the reason step024 exists):** (1) neither loss curve converged - r=0.5 ran 0.3421/0.2769/0.2357 at ep 5/10/15 and r=0.25 ran 0.3696/0.3275/0.2852, both still falling at the last epoch; (2) decisively, step004's own d6 ladder scored **-18pp at 10ep/2k, -6pp at 15ep/6k, PARITY at 25ep/9.3k** - so at rel_mse 0.2357 on an even SMALLER budget (15ep/1k), width's -15.40pp is actually BETTER than depth's -18pp at comparable fit. The depth win was bought with ~16x the training budget width was given. **CORRECTION RECORDED DELIBERATELY: "depth strictly dominates width" was nearly filed as CONFIRMED** on the grounds that r=0.25 and d6 sit at the same 0.500x param budget while d6 got parity - that comparison is matched on params and UNMATCHED on training budget, which the ladder shows is worth ~18pp on its own. The claim is an artifact and is NOT made. **Explicitly NOT the mirror of step022:** resolution died against a training-free ORACLE ceiling no amount of training could lift; width died against an epoch count, and no oracle exists for a sliced MLP. **What IS CONFIRMED, budget-independent:** (a) the +43.40pp naive->distilled recovery at r=0.5 is the LARGEST any arm in this line has produced, so a magnitude-ranked MLP slice is recoverable-in-principle, not destroyed; (b) **width buys speed poorly** - 0.667x params -> 1.28x and 0.500x params -> 1.48x, while depth got 2.0x at 0.5x, so the tower is not MLP-bound in wall-time even though the MLP is 67% of its params. For the drone that split matters: **width is the params/bytes lever, depth is the latency lever, and they are not the same lever.** Design notes: MLP intermediate chosen over residual width because slicing it needs NO adapters (fc1 loses rows, fc2 loses matching columns, residual stream stays 768), keeping the run strictly one-variable; init = magnitude-ranked slice keeping top-m channels by ||fc1_row|| * ||fc2_col||, the width analogue of depth truncation, so "too narrow" is not confounded with "started from nothing". Scripts: `scripts/frontier/vlm_step023_mlp_width.py` (new, 176 lines) + new library `scripts/frontier/vlm_width.py` (47 lines, extracted at the 200-line limit; split verified behaviour-preserving by a smoke that reproduced the pre-split run exactly). Results: `results/frontier/vlm_step023_mlp_width_r0.5-0.25_e15_t1000_n500__5060ti_cuda.json`. Write-up: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part10.md` section 39.

- **vlm_step022 (is the wall the TOKEN BUDGET or the PIXEL GRID? training-free ceiling test): DONE (2026-08-13, 5060ti_cuda, k in {64,36,16,9,4} x 500 images).** **VERDICT: MIXED - both walls real, and the result CAPS the entire low-resolution branch.** Oracle (full 512-px pixels, tower's 64 output tokens average-pooled to k, frozen decoder): k=64 0.7040 / k=36 0.5720 / k=16 0.4160 / k=9 0.2580 / k=4 0.1780. pixel_cost_pp vs step020's naive res-ladder at matched k: +0.00 / +11.60 / +25.60 / +16.40 / +7.20. Validity gate PASS (k=64 identity pool 0.7040 vs anchor 0.711, 0.7pp). Monotone in k (reported, not required). **The decomposition is exactly additive:** at k=16 the total naive gap of 54.40pp (0.7040 -> 0.1600) splits into 28.80pp of TOKEN-BUDGET cost (0.7040 -> 0.4160, pixels perfect) plus 25.60pp of PIXEL-GRID cost (0.4160 -> 0.1600, tokens held fixed). **CONFIRMED, and this one is a ceiling rather than a budget-limited null:** no res-256 student can ever exceed 0.4160, because that is the oracle handed the real teacher's features. So the best possible low-res arm is -28.8pp for 2.93x tower, and at res 384 it is -13.2pp for 1.80x - whereas d6 held PARITY (0.720 vs 0.711) at 2.0x. **Depth strictly dominates resolution as a tower lever.** Re-reads step021 correctly: its 0.2980 student recovered 13.80pp of the 25.60pp pixel cost (54%) with 11.80pp of headroom left to its own oracle - the still-falling loss was real headroom, but buying it maxes out at 0.4160, so it is not worth the epochs. **Interpretation limit held exactly as pre-registered:** this bounds AVERAGE-POOLED token budgets, NOT all k-token encodings - a learned/content-selective 16-token encoder is not covered by this ceiling. **Consequence for the roadmap: resolution is CLOSED; do NOT run the d6 x res-256 2x2 that the MIXED branch pre-registered, since its ceiling is now known to sit below d6-alone at 512. Next lever = tower WIDTH (never touched; also the only structural axis that shrinks params).** Results: `results/frontier/vlm_step022_token_vs_pixel_k64-36-16-9-4_n500__5060ti_cuda.json`. Original block:
  - **vlm_step022 ... RUNNING (original pre-registration follows).** Script: `scripts/frontier/vlm_step022_token_vs_pixel.py` (new, 177 lines). **Motivation — step021's PARTIAL is confounded and the confound is mine.** The step021 student fit its target unusually well (rel_mse 0.0545, cosine 0.9738 — tighter than any depth distillation in this line) yet scored only 0.2980. A student that matches its target that well is not failing to optimise; the TARGET is the limit. And the target was the 512-px teacher's 64 tokens AVERAGE-POOLED to 16, i.e. a blurred teacher — so "256 px destroys information" (H1) and "16 tokens cannot carry the answer" (H2) are inseparable in step021. **The test needs NO training:** run the tower at full 512 px (pixels perfect by construction), pool its 64 output tokens to k, feed the frozen decoder. That is an ORACLE k-token model — no student can beat it. oracle@16 near 0.30 => token budget is the wall and step021's student is at its ceiling; near 0.70 => the pixel grid is the wall and the still-falling loss says buy more epochs. Matched-k comparison against step020's resolution ladder decomposes the two costs, which step020 could not do alone. **Pre-registered gates:** (1) k=64 is an identity pool and MUST reproduce the anchor within ±2.0pp of 0.711; (2) monotonicity reported, NOT required. **Stated interpretation limit:** this bounds AVERAGE-POOLED token budgets, not every possible k-token encoding. **Smoke (n=20, NOT a result):** gate PASS (k=64 -> 0.700); k=16 oracle 0.4500 vs step020's res-256 cell 0.1600 (+29.00pp) and vs step021's distilled 0.2980 — reads MIXED, both factors contributing, with ~15pp of headroom still open to step021's student. **Process lesson: this should have run BEFORE step021 — when a distillation underperforms, first bound what its target could possibly deliver.**

- **vlm_step021 (can a tower be DISTILLED to run at a smaller pixel grid?): DONE (2026-08-13, 5060ti_cuda, 1000 train / 500 eval, 15 ep). VERDICT: res 256 PARTIAL (+13.80pp), res 384 IRRECOVERABLE (+4.20pp) — distillation helps but does NOT restore the teacher at either grid.** Validity gate PASS (teacher d12@512 = 0.7040 vs 0.711). Naive control re-measured in-run at res 256 reproduced step020's independent draw EXACTLY (0.1600), so the two experiments' eval paths agree. Cells: **res 256 (16 tok)** naive 0.1600 -> distilled 0.2980 (+13.80pp, PARTIAL: clears the 5pp bar, misses the 20pp RECOVERABLE bar), final rel_mse 0.0545 / cosine 0.9738; **res 384 (36 tok)** naive 0.4560 -> distilled 0.4980 (+4.20pp, IRRECOVERABLE by the <=5pp rule), final rel_mse 0.0611 / cosine 0.9706, student 14.66 ms vs teacher 27.35 ms = 1.87x tower. Pattern: distillation recovers MORE where the naive arm is more broken (+13.8pp at 256 vs +4.2pp at 384), but neither cell reaches the teacher's 0.7040. **Caveat that must travel with this:** the loss was still falling monotonically in BOTH cells at the pinned 15-epoch budget, so these are verdicts *at this budget*, not ceilings. **CONFOUND FOUND AFTER THE FACT (drives step022):** the excellent feature fit alongside a poor top-1 means the student was matching a target that is itself lossy — the teacher pooled 8x8 -> 4x4 — so this experiment cannot separate "the 256-px grid is the wall" from "16 tokens are the wall". step022 settles it training-free. **BUG FOUND AND FIXED MID-RUN (by my own pre-registered assert):** `pool_to` used `avg_pool2d` with an integer stride and asserted `g % k == 0`, which holds for 8x8 -> 4x4 (res 256) but CRASHED on 8x8 -> 6x6 (res 384). Fixed to `adaptive_avg_pool2d`, verified **bit-identical** for the divisible case (`torch.allclose` PASS on 8->4), so the res-256 cell is unaffected and only res 384 was re-run. The assert did its job: a silent wrong-shaped pool would have produced a quotable but meaningless 384 number. Script: `scripts/frontier/vlm_step021_lowres_distill.py` (new, 197 lines) + new library `scripts/frontier/vlm_res.py` (ResEval extracted so step021 can import it without running step020's module-scope argparse). **Motivation:** step020 showed NAIVE resolution cuts land at chance, but naive DEPTH did exactly the same twice (naive d6 0.120, naive d3 0.100, both chance) and distillation lifted them to 0.560/0.460. A stock tower fed interpolated position embeddings it never trained on is off-distribution by construction, so the naive arm is a rejection filter, not a verdict. **One variable:** student is a full-DEPTH (d12) trainable copy, so depth is held fixed and resolution is the only structural change. **The non-obvious piece:** teacher (64 tokens, 8x8) and student (16 tokens, 4x4) are shape-incompatible for the feature MSE; resolved by average-pooling the teacher grid to the student grid (8x8 -> 2x2 pool -> 4x4), which mirrors what the connector's own 4x4 pooling already does one level up. Loss stays relative MSE so numbers compare to step004/007/008. **Pre-registered gates:** (1) teacher d12@512 within ±2.0pp of 0.711 — **PASS at 0.7040**; (2) naive control re-measured in THIS run and eval sample, not imported from step020's separate n=500 draw. **Pre-registered verdict rule:** RECOVERABLE iff distilled >= naive + 20pp at 256 (the depth-distillation recovery size, 0.120->0.560 — the precedent's own bar, not fitted); PARTIAL if >5pp but <20pp; IRRECOVERABLE if <=5pp, which is a real publishable negative (the 512 grid carries information the task needs). T0 scout budget, so a null is "at this budget" — loss curve reported so a still-falling curve is not read as convergence. **Smoke (1 ep / 40 img, plumbing only, NOT a result):** gate PASS, res-256 naive 0.200 -> distilled 0.200.

- **vlm_step020 (does cutting INPUT RESOLUTION buy tower latency at acceptable accuracy?): DONE (2026-08-13, 5060ti_cuda, 5 cells x 500 images). VERDICT: naive resolution REJECTED, and the "two-for-one" motivation below is FALSIFIED by this run's own data.** Ladder (d12 stock, fp32, n=500): res 512 / 64 tok -> top1 0.7040, vision 26.18 ms, prefill 13.87; 384 / 36 -> 0.4560 (-24.80pp), 14.55 / 13.67, tower 1.80x; 256 / 16 -> 0.1600 (-54.40pp), 8.92 / 13.63, 2.93x; 192 / 9 -> 0.0940 (-61.00pp), 7.00 / 13.30, 3.74x; 128 / 4 -> 0.1060 (-59.80pp), 4.61 / 13.59, 5.68x. Both gates PASS (validity 0.7040 vs 0.711, delta -0.0070; token-count assert fired on no image in any cell). **CONFIRMED — cutting image tokens buys NOTHING in prefill:** prefill_ms is flat 13.3-13.9 ms while image tokens go 64 -> 4, so prefill is dominated by the TEXT prompt. **Resolution is a TOWER-ONLY lever, exactly like depth — do not re-quote the two-for-one claim written in the motivation below.** **CONFIRMED — naive resolution reduction is catastrophic:** 192/128 sit at 0.094/0.106 against a 0.100 chance floor (destroyed, not degraded), and even 1.8x costs 24.8pp. But that is the naive-depth signature, so this rejects naive resolution only and graduates to vlm_step021 (distilled arm). Also retro-explains why section 9's token pruning was worth less wall-time than its token counts implied. Original block:

- **vlm_step020 (original motivation, kept verbatim; the two-for-one claim in it is FALSIFIED above):** Script: `scripts/frontier/vlm_step020_resolution_ladder.py` (new, 200 lines). **Motivation — the Amdahl target was misidentified for three parts of the trajectory.** step006's own numbers: at d12 `vision_ms` 64.85 of 79.18 total (82%); at d6 35.05 of 49.59 (71%). The tower is the bottleneck and depth was the only tower lever ever pulled. The entire int8 `lm_head` line (part 8 sections 24-32) optimized a ~0.3 ms sliver and moved ZERO ms end-to-end. **The lever:** SigLIP is patch-16 on a 512 grid = 1024 patches, connector pools 4x4, so decoder tokens = patches/16. Cutting the pixel grid cuts BOTH vision_ms AND prefill_ms — something depth never did. 512->64 tok, 384->36, 256->16, 192->9, 128->4. **POC (5060ti, fp32, tower only, 30 iters after 8 warmup):** d12 24.68/13.71/8.10/6.27/3.83 ms and d6 12.63/7.04/4.21/3.30/2.09 ms at 512/384/256/192/128 — so d6@256 is 5.86x under d12@512 on the tower, resolution alone worth 3.0x at d6 on top of depth's 2.0x. Scaling is SUBLINEAR (4x patches -> 3x time), i.e. an overhead floor below ~192. **One variable: depth 12 STOCK, no distilled checkpoint** — the d6 student was distilled at 512, so feeding it 256 would confound "resolution hurts" with "student off-distribution", which is what vlm_step011/012 separately measure. **Implementation note (a POC killed the naive design):** the processor's `size={'longest_edge': N}` knob is INERT — every N in {512,384,256,192,128} returns a 512x512 tensor. Resolution is therefore set model-side by bilinear-downsampling the processor's own normalised pixels, which keeps mean/std preprocessing identical across arms. **Pre-registered gates:** (1) validity — res-512 d12 top-1 within ±2.0pp of step006's 0.711; (2) token-count — every image asserts connector tokens == (res/16)^2/16 and that exactly that many image slots survive the merge, so a fast cell cannot look good for the wrong reason. T0 SCOUT = rejection filter: every res with delta >= -0.5pp advances to T1. **Smoke (n=20, NOT a result):** both gates PASS (res512 top1 0.700, delta -0.0110; no assert fired) but res 256 fell to 0.200 at 4.29x tower speedup — **early warning that resolution is NOT free**, unlike depth. Full run at n=500 settles the curve rather than confirming a foregone win.
- **vlm_step019 (does the 3.05× int8 readout survive COMPOSITION inside the real harness?): DONE (2026-07-30 01:18, 5060ti_cuda, 5 arms × 3859 images). ANSWER: NO — the 3.05× kernel is a 0.94× module.** Full-run result, written up as trajectory **part 8 §32**; result JSON `results/frontier/vlm_step019_e2e_readout__5060ti_cuda.json`. Both pre-registered gates PASS at full n: kernel fidelity `agree(w8a8_tri, w8a8_fake) = 1.0000` (identical predictions on all 3859 images, vs a 0.99 bar), validity `d12 fp32 top1 = 0.711` vs step006's 0.711 (Δ −0.0002, tighter than the smoke test's 0.700). `calls = 11.0` exactly. Head time over all 11 calls: fp32 3.2471 / fp16 1.9471 / w8a8_fake 3.8684 / w8a8_tri 2.0694 / w8a8_trif 2.0179 ms; top-1 0.711 / 0.711 / 0.710 / 0.710 / 0.710 with `agr_fp32` 0.989 for every int8 arm; `head_MB` 113.5 / 56.8 / 28.6 / 28.6 / 28.6. **CONFIRMED — against the honest fp16 deployment baseline the int8 readout LOSES: `w8a8_tri` 0.94× head / 0.81× forward, `w8a8_trif` 0.96× / 0.83×.** The smoke test's inversion held and got slightly worse at full n (0.96× → 0.94×). Against fp32 it "wins" 1.57–1.61×, which is exactly the flattering comparison §30 note 3 forbade quoting. **The HYPOTHESIS is now supported but still not ablated:** `w8a8_fake` (3.8684 ms) is SLOWER than `fp32` (3.2471 ms) with no Triton kernel involved at all, so ~0.62 ms is pure per-token activation-quantization cost — 5–6 elementwise launches over 576 elements at T=1. Named next test: time the quantization prologue separately from `tri_run`, or hoist it into the preceding layer. **CONFIRMED — fusion is worth 1.03× at module level** (2.0179 vs 2.0694 ms) against 1.01× at kernel level, so §30's "ship the simpler int32 kernel" survives, but for a new reason: fusion is not where the time is. **CONFIRMED — the byte win is untouched:** 3.97× vs fp32, 1.99× vs fp16, arithmetic on the weights, at −0.1pp. **Amdahl as predicted:** `prefill_ms` 14.08–14.28 flat across all five arms against 1.9–3.9 ms of total head time — the readout is NOT this pipeline's bottleneck. Scope limits: one card, eager PyTorch (a compiled/CUDA-graph module would amortize exactly the launch overhead this arm pays, so this is a statement about this deployment path, not about int8); depth 12 stock; 10-way whole-object Imagenette per §17. Original block: **RUNNING (5060ti_cuda, 5 arms × 3859 images).** Script: `scripts/frontier/vlm_step019_e2e_readout.py` (new, 200 lines) — part 8 §31 item 2. Swaps `lm_head` for a `Head` module whose `mode` switches ONLY the arithmetic (shared weights, shared scales, shared activation-quantizer, so an accuracy difference between arms cannot come from a different quantizer) and times all **11** `lm_head` calls per image, split into the 1 `forward()` call (inside the harness's `prefill_ms`) and the 10 `choose()` calls (which the harness has NEVER timed). Arms: `fp32` (harness baseline + validity gate) / `fp16` (the honest DEPLOYMENT baseline, per §30 note 3 — an fp32 comparison flatters int8 and must not be quoted) / `w8a8_fake` (the accuracy scheme, none of the kernel) / `w8a8_tri` (real Triton int8 → int32 + separate dequant, §30's recommended deployment form) / `w8a8_trif` (same kernel, FUSED epilogue — added after the smoke test, because §30's "fusion buys nothing" was measured in isolation and this asks whether that survives composition, where the separate pass also costs a launch and a 49280-wide output round-trip). The kernel is IMPORTED verbatim from `vlm_step017_fused_dequant.run` — one definition, no re-typed variant to drift. Depth 12 stock, so no distillation checkpoint is involved and vision depth cannot touch `lm_head` latency. **Pre-registered gates:** (1) `agree(w8a8_tri, w8a8_fake) ≥ 0.99` or no latency number may be quoted; (2) d12 `fp32` top-1 within ±2.0pp of step006's 0.711. **Smoke (n=20, NOT a result) already inverts the headline:** both gates PASS (agreement 1.0000, fp32 0.700, Δ −0.0110) and `calls = 11.0` confirms the 1-forward + 10-choose pattern exactly — but `w8a8_tri` is **0.96× vs fp16** on head time and 0.83× on the forward call, i.e. **SLOWER end-to-end than the fp16 baseline it beats 3.05× in isolation**, and `w8a8_trif` only reaches 0.98×. `prefill_ms` ≈ 13.7 ms against 2–4 ms of total head time confirms §30's Amdahl warning. **HYPOTHESIS (to be tested, not asserted): the module-level cost is per-token activation quantization plus kernel-launch/dispatch overhead, neither of which the microbenchmark included** (§30 note 4 named the exclusion) — `w8a8_fake` at 3.86 ms is slower than `fp32` at 3.25 ms, which is quantization cost with no kernel involved at all. Run at n=3859 to settle the number rather than to confirm a foregone win. Environment note: the shared venv has transformers 4.44.0 and cannot load SmolVLM, so this runs `PYTHONPATH=vlm_libs` against the repo's existing `pip install --target` directory — **the teammates' venv is never modified**.
- **vlm_step012 + vlm_step011-rerun: DONE (2026-08-13 02:14, mini_mps, 6 cells x 3859 images). VERDICT: CAPACITY CONFIRMED (at step004's epoch budget) — scale augmentation did NOT close the small-object gap.** Result JSON `results/frontier/vlm_step011_fine_detail_d6_f100-f50-f25__mini_mps.json`. Grid (top1 / agree / vis_ms): d12_f100 0.711 / 1.000 / 65.7 | d12_f50 0.672 / 0.705 / 64.9 | d12_f25 0.494 / 0.554 / 64.8 | d6_f100 0.720 / 0.685 / 35.4 | d6_f50 0.666 / 0.670 / 35.9 | d6_f25 0.423 / 0.504 / 36.6. Double difference I(f) = [d6 shrink cost] - [d12 shrink cost]: **I(0.25) = -0.0801, boot95 [-0.1003, -0.0593]** — the upper bound sits far below the pre-registered -2.0pp bar, so by the rule fixed before the run this is **CAPACITY, not DISTRIBUTION**. I(0.5) = -0.0143, boot95 [-0.0334, +0.0052] straddles zero, so the break lies between f=0.5 and f=0.25 and must be reported at that scale, not rounded to "d6 fails on small objects" generally. **Both validity anchors PASS:** d12_f100 = 0.711 exactly (step006), and the second independent criterion — d6_f100 within 2.0pp of 0.711 — passes at 0.720, i.e. augmentation did not buy small-object skill by spending whole-object parity; it simply did not buy small-object skill. **This is the confound-free direction, as pre-registered:** the training distribution matched the test manipulation exactly (same gray canvas, same centring, continuous U(0.2,0.9) vs discrete test scales) and the gap survived, which no confound rescues. **The one stated caveat holds and must travel with the claim:** step012's loss was still falling monotonically at the pinned budget (rel_mse 0.3795 -> 0.1514, last five epochs 0.1643/0.1615/0.1588/0.1561/0.1535/0.1514), so this is CAPACITY *at step004's epoch budget* — the budget the control had — and not a proven ceiling. **Consequence for the drone:** the d6 tower's -16.6% params is scoped to whole-object framing; small-object scenes need d12 or a different recovery mechanism than more scale-augmented feature distillation. Original block: **vlm_step012 (CAPACITY or DISTRIBUTION? re-distil d6 WITH scale augmentation): TRAINING DONE (2026-07-30 01:04, mini_mps, 457.3 min = 7.6 h, 9352 × 25 ep). VERDICT PENDING — `vlm_step011` rerun RELAUNCHED on mini_mps 2026-08-13 00:59 with `--ckpt` pointed at this checkpoint.** **The first verdict attempt DIED:** it reached cell 1/6 (`d12_f100`) at 3000/3859 images on 2026-07-30 01:16 and the box then restarted, taking the whole tmux server with it (no `/private/tmp/tmux-501/default` socket, all five slots FREE). No partial result was written; the dead log is preserved at `logs/vlm_step011_fine_detail__mini_mps_ckpt-step012_deadpartial_reboot.log`. Nothing about the experiment changed — same script, same `--ckpt`, same defaults (d6, scales 1.0/0.5/0.25, n_eval 3900 → 3859, seed 42) — so this is a restart, not a redesign. Training converged monotonically: rel_mse 0.3795 → 0.1514, cosine 0.8159 → 0.9219 over 25 epochs, no plateau in the last five (0.1643 → 0.1615 → 0.1588 → 0.1561 → 0.1535 → 0.1514), so the student was still improving when the pinned budget ran out — a **confound on a NULL that must be stated: if the verdict is CAPACITY, it is CAPACITY *at step004's epoch budget*, which is the budget the control had, not a proven ceiling.** Augmentation fired as designed: `frac_untouched` 0.4952 against the p_keep = 0.5 target and `mean_scale` 0.7737 over U(0.2, 0.9) restricted to the shrunk half. As pre-registered, `rel_mse` is **NOT** comparable to step004's — different (shrunk) target images — so nothing here is a result; the verdict is step011's six-cell grid. Checkpoint `results/frontier/vlm_step012_scale_augment_d6_25ep_aug20-90__mini_mps.pt` (162 MB). **Overwrite hazard closed for the log too:** step011's control log was moved to `logs/vlm_step011_fine_detail__mini_mps_ckpt-step004.log` before relaunch, since `launch_slot.sh` derives the log name from the script filename and would otherwise have destroyed it exactly as `TAG` would have destroyed the control JSON. Original block: **RUNNING (mini_mps, training-only, 9352 × 25 ep; teacher cache 878 s, ~17.7 min/epoch, ETA ~01:10 on 2026-07-30).** `scripts/frontier/vlm_step012_scale_augment.py` (~170 lines). **The decisive follow-up to step011 and the single thing standing between the trajectory and an unscoped drone claim** (part 5 §18 item 1). step011 CONFIRMED that d6's teacher parity dies on small objects but cannot say *why*, and the two live HYPOTHESES point opposite ways: (a) CAPACITY — the six deleted vision layers carry fine spatial detail, so nothing recovers it and the drone ships d12 + int8 only; (b) DISTRIBUTION — d6 only ever saw whole-object Imagenette, so scale-augmented distillation restores it and the −33.1%-bytes config is back. **One variable: the train-time object-scale distribution.** Random scale ~ U(0.2, 0.9) with p_keep = 0.5 left untouched, pasted centred on the same GRAY (128,128,128) canvas step011 tests with. **The control is the EXISTING step004 d6_25ep9k checkpoint, not a new arm** — so this costs one distillation, not two. That required care: `sample_images` floors to per = n // 10, so step004's literal `--n_train 9469` yields **9352** images while a nominal 10000 yields 9469; the script's default is therefore 9469 (verified: returns exactly 9352), because passing the round number would silently train on a 117-image-different set and turn a one-variable run into a two-variable one. epochs/batch/lr/seed pinned to step004's (25 / 8 / 2e-4 / 7), and it runs on **mini_mps — the same physical box that trained the control**, removing a hardware variable too. **The augmentation is deterministic per path, which is a correctness requirement not a style choice:** teacher targets are cached ONCE while the student is re-encoded every epoch, so a per-call random transform would train the student to match teacher features computed from a *different view* — a silently corrupted objective. Scale is seeded from the file name; the `tf=` hook threaded through `vlm_distill.tower_feats/cache_teacher/train_student` forwards it to the teacher too, so both see the identical view. Trained on a **continuous** range while step011 tests at discrete f ∈ {1.0, 0.5, 0.25}, so a recovery is generalisation across scale, not memorisation of the test manipulation. **Pre-registered rule (docstring, pre-run).** No eval logic is duplicated — the verdict comes from re-running `vlm_step011_fine_detail.py` UNCHANGED with `--ckpt` pointed here. DISTRIBUTION CONFIRMED iff I_aug(f) ⊆ ±2.0pp at BOTH f = 0.5 and 0.25; CAPACITY CONFIRMED iff I_aug(0.25) upper still < −2.0pp; PARTIAL if it recovers at 0.5 but not 0.25 — report the breaking scale, do not round into either verdict. **Second, independent criterion and a real way to fail:** `d6_f100` must stay within 2.0pp of the teacher's 0.711 — augmentation that buys small-object skill by spending whole-object parity has not helped, since that parity *is* the original claim. **Asymmetry stated up front: a NULL here is confound-free and therefore decisive** (the training distribution would have matched the test manipulation exactly — same canvas, same centring — and still failed, which no confound rescues); **a POSITIVE is weaker**, confounded between genuine scale-invariance and mere adaptation to OOD gray padding, and needs a follow-up on real small objects before the drone claim is un-scoped. Note `rel_mse` will NOT be comparable to step004's — the targets are features of *different* (shrunk) images, so a higher loss here does not mean a worse student; the verdict is step011's grid, not the loss curve. Smoke PASSED (mps, n=40, 1 ep): 17 shrunk / 23 untouched, mean scale 0.788, cache + train + checkpoint save all clean. **Not on 5060ti despite the standing preference: it is running vlm_step008 (d3 at ep 22/25, then d9).** **Overwrite hazard closed before the rerun:** step011's `TAG` is `d{depth}_{scales}` only — it carries no checkpoint identity, so re-running it with `--ckpt <step012>` would have written to the same path and **destroyed the control grid it is compared against**. The original is preserved as `results/frontier/vlm_step011_fine_detail_d6_f100-f50-f25__mini_mps_ckpt-step004.json`; the rerun takes the bare name. Same class as the resume-by-existence hazard of part6 §20.1 — an artifact path that does not encode every input it depends on.
- **vlm_step011 (does d6's parity survive when the object is SMALL?): DONE (2026-07-29, mini_mps, ~12 min, n=3859 x 6 cells). SUB-ADDITIVE at BOTH scales and not marginally so -- CONFIRMED: depth truncation destroys fine spatial detail, and d6's teacher parity is an artefact of whole-object framing. The vlm_step010 drone headline is hereby SCOPED, not retracted.** **Validity anchors exact for the fourth independent time:** `d12_f100` = 0.711, `d6_f100` = 0.713. Cells (top1 / agree-vs-teacher): d12 0.711 / 0.672 / 0.494 and d6 0.713 / 0.546 / 0.252 at f = 1.0 / 0.5 / 0.25. **THE CALL (pre-registered double difference, shared-index bootstrap): I(0.5) = -12.78pp boot95 [-14.77, -10.75] -> SUB-ADDITIVE; I(0.25) = -24.41pp boot95 [-26.64, -22.21] -> SUB-ADDITIVE.** Both sit 5-11x outside the +/-2.0pp tolerance and neither CI comes near it, so this is not a rounding call. Descriptive main effects (confounded by the gray padding, which is why they are not the registered quantity): the teacher loses 3.89pp at f=0.5 and 21.66pp at f=0.25; d6 loses 16.66pp and 46.07pp, collapsing to 0.252 top-1 on a task whose chance floor is 0.10. McNemar p < 1e-5 on every cell. **The obvious counter-argument is pre-empted: this is not an additive-scale artefact.** In *ratio* terms the teacher retains 69.5% of its f=1.0 accuracy at f=0.25 while d6 retains 35.3% -- the truncated tower loses roughly twice as much on either scale, and d6 never approaches the 0.10 floor, so no saturation is compressing the interaction. `agree`(d6, d12) falls 0.699 -> 0.565 -> 0.320 as the object shrinks: the two towers do not merely differ in accuracy, they diverge further the smaller the target gets. **Two explanations remain live and the run does not separate them (HYPOTHESIS, both):** (a) CAPACITY -- the six deleted vision layers are the ones carrying fine spatial detail, in which case no amount of retraining recovers it; (b) DISTRIBUTION -- d6 was distilled exclusively on whole-object Imagenette and has simply never seen a small target, in which case scale-augmented distillation recovers most of it. These have opposite consequences for the drone, and (b) is directly testable by re-distilling d6 with random-scale augmentation and re-running this exact grid. **What is CONFIRMED regardless of which holds: for this checkpoint, teacher parity does not transfer to small objects, so no unscoped drone claim may be made from step010.** Note the teacher degrades badly too (0.711 -> 0.494), so small-object robustness is a weakness of SmolVLM-256M generally, not only of the truncation -- the truncation just makes it about twice as bad. Writeup `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part5.md` §17. Result: `results/frontier/vlm_step011_fine_detail_d6_f100-f50-f25__mini_mps.json`. **Defect found and fixed by this run:** `vlm_quant.call_ortho` hardcoded its SUB-ADDITIVE verdict string to quantization wording, so step011 printed "quantization costs the truncated tower more" in a run containing no quantization; the helper now takes a `lever` argument (statistical rule unchanged, wording only) -- fixed in the shared library, not special-cased. Original block: **RUNNING (mini_mps, launched on the slot vlm_step010 freed; eval-only, 3859 × 6 cells, ~1.3 h).** **Now the single load-bearing open question:** step010 just produced the drone headline (−33.1% bytes at −0.31pp), and *every* number in it was measured on whole-object images. `scripts/frontier/vlm_step011_fine_detail.py` (182 lines). part 3 §11.6, "before any drone claim" — **the last open validity question ahead of the drone write-up.** Every accuracy claim in this trajectory, including the load-bearing "d6 at teacher parity with 16.58% of the model deleted", was measured on 10-way **whole-object** Imagenette where the target fills the frame. A drone sees the target at *distance*. If the layers truncation deletes are the ones carrying fine spatial detail, parity is an artefact of the test images and the whole recipe is scoped to big objects. **One variable: object scale.** Each val image is resized to fraction f and pasted centred on a gray canvas of the ORIGINAL size, so the object occupies f² of the pixels while the processor's input resolution never changes; f=1.0 is the untouched image. Grid {d12, d6} × f {1.0, 0.5, 0.25}, one eval set, per-image records. **Confound stated up front:** f<1 changes scale *and* adds OOD uniform padding, so the main effect [acc(f) − acc(1.0)] is descriptive only — but the padding is applied identically at both depths, so it **differences out of the interaction**, which is why the double difference is the pre-registered quantity, not the main effect. **Pre-registered rule:** I(f) = [acc(d6,f) − acc(d6,1.0)] − [acc(d12,f) − acc(d12,1.0)], shared-index bootstrap as in step009/010. **ORTHOGONAL (parity survives; drone claim generalises)** iff CI ⊆ ±2.0pp; **SUB-ADDITIVE (depth truncation destroys fine detail; drone claim SCOPED to large objects)** iff upper < −2.0pp; SUPER-ADDITIVE iff lower > +2.0pp; else INCONCLUSIVE, not rounded. Validity anchors: `d12_f100` must reproduce 0.711 and `d6_f100` 0.713. `agree` read in every cell — step009's 0.499 means matched top-1 at small f still would not license "the student sees what the teacher sees". Timing recorded but **not** a claim (may share a box). Smoke PASSED (cpu, n=20): 6 cells, both verdict blocks, and **the manipulation bites** — top-1 0.700 → 0.450 at d12 and 0.650 → 0.350 at d6, so the design has real effect size to interact with; `vis_ms` flat across f (127/126/127) confirms canvas size is held fixed. **Not launched on mini_cpu despite the slot being free: mini_cpu and mini_mps are the same physical box, and a 2.4 h CPU eval would contend with vlm_step010.**

- **vlm_step010 (quantize the two lookup tables — the largest untouched block): DONE (2026-07-29, mini_mps, ~1.6 h, n=3859 × 8 cells). int8 SHIPS, int4 KILLED — and this produces the drone's shipping configuration.** **Validity anchors exact for the third independent time:** `d12_fp32` = 0.711, `d6_fp32` = 0.713. **The block is 22.13%, bigger than depth truncation's 16.58%** — `tie_word_embeddings=False`, so `embed_tokens` and `lm_head` are two independent 49280×576 tensors (56,770,560 params, 227.1 MB fp32). **int8 is free (CONFIRMED):** `int8_row` d12 +0.00pp boot95 [−0.29, +0.29] → SHIP, d6 −0.52pp [−0.86, −0.18] → SHIP; `int8_tensor` d12 −0.05pp, d6 −0.67pp → SHIP. **int4 is not (CONFIRMED KILLED):** −10.34pp [−11.45, −9.25] at d12 and −8.01pp [−9.07, −7.00] at d6, McNemar p < 1e-16 — an order of magnitude outside the ±2.0pp tolerance, at both depths, with bit-width the only variable. The weight-space pre-flight predicted the ordering exactly (int8_row rel_rmse 0.0121, int4_row 0.219). 4 bits is not enough for these tables *without* group-wise scales or a calibrated method (GPTQ) — neither tried, so the *scheme* is killed, not all of 4-bit. **CONFIRMED NULL — per-row scales are NOT load-bearing:** `int8_tensor` was the one-variable granularity control (identical bit-width, one scale per tensor instead of per vocabulary row) and lands within 0.15pp of `int8_row` at both depths, both SHIP, while storing 0.4 MB less and needing one less lookup → **the drone should use tensor-scale int8**, and int8_tensor's 2.7×-worse weight-space rel_rmse (0.0327 vs 0.0121) is a distinction with no task-level difference — weight-space fidelity is a proxy, the goal metric is not. **Compounding with depth:** I(int8_row) = −0.52pp boot95 [−0.96, −0.08] and I(int8_tensor) = −0.62pp [−1.24, −0.03] → **ORTHOGONAL, levers compound** — read honestly, both CIs *exclude zero*, so the truncated tower does pay ~half a point more for quantization than the teacher; that is well inside the registered tolerance, so ORTHOGONAL **by the rule as written, not by rounding**. I(int4_row) = +2.33pp [+0.93, +3.71] → INCONCLUSIVE (moot, int4 killed). **THE DRONE NUMBER — d6 + int8 tables vs the untouched teacher, paired on the same 3859 images: top1 0.7077 vs 0.7108, Δ −0.31pp boot95 [−1.76, +1.19], McNemar p = 0.708, at 685.9 MB vs 1025.9 MB = −33.1% of the model's bytes.** 42,527,232 params deleted by truncation (170.1 MB) + 56,770,560 params stored at 8 bits (169.9 MB). **One third of the bytes gone, statistically indistinguishable from the teacher.** **SCOPED 2026-07-29 by vlm_step011 -- read before quoting:** every cell above was measured on WHOLE-OBJECT images, and step011 shows the accuracy half does not survive small targets (d6 falls to 0.252 top-1 at f=0.25 where the teacher holds 0.494, I = -24.41pp). The BYTE half is unconditional -- -33.1% is arithmetic on the weights. What is scoped is "at -0.31pp". The levers separate cleanly: int8 on the tables is input-independent (text-side lookups, not the vision tower), while depth truncation is where the scale sensitivity lives -- so the unconditional drone config is **d12 + int8 = 856.0 MB, -16.6%, delta +0.00pp**, and d6 stays conditional on target size pending scale-augmented re-distillation (part 5 §18 item 1). Bytes measured not proxied (meditation-005). **Timing did not move and NO timing claim is made** — `vis_ms` 64.8–64.9 at d12 / 35.1 at d6 across every arm, `prefill_ms` 14.4 everywhere; fake-quant dequantizes to fp32 before the matmul, so this is the expected null and a true int8 kernel's speed is untested. **Caveat surviving everything:** `agree`(d6_int8_row, d12_fp32) = 0.698, unchanged from d6_fp32's 0.699 — quantization does not alter the student's relationship to the teacher, but that relationship was never equivalence. Matched top-1 is parity, not equivalence. Full writeup `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part5.md` §16 + §16.1. Result: `results/frontier/vlm_step010_quant_tables_d6_fp32-int8_row-int4_row-int8_tensor__mini_mps.json`. Original block: **RUNNING (mini_mps, eval-only, 3859 images × 8 cells, ~2 h).** `scripts/frontier/vlm_step010_quant_tables.py` (179) + `scripts/frontier/vlm_quant.py` (76, extracted for the 200-line limit). **The prize is bigger than the trajectory said.** part3 §11.7 costed the embedding table at 11.60%; probing the real model shows `tie_word_embeddings = False`, so `embed_tokens` and `lm_head` are two *separate* 49280×576 tensors — 28,385,280 params each, **22.14% together, a bigger block than depth truncation's 16.58%**. Both are on the readout path (`vlm_eval.merge/forward/choose` all call one or both), and both are lookups/readouts, not transforms — the one param-side win the profile endorses. Weight-only symmetric PTQ, quantize→dequantize to fp32, so **accuracy is measured exactly as an int kernel would produce it while the byte saving stays analytic** (bits/weight + one fp32 scale per row or per tensor). Full 2×4 grid {d12 teacher, d6 distilled} × {fp32, int8_row, int4_row, int8_tensor} on ONE eval set, per-image records, quantization applied once per arm (not per image — writing 56.8M weights per image would dominate the run). `d12_fp32` must reproduce step006's 0.711 and `d6_fp32` its 0.713 or the run is unreadable. **`int8_tensor` is a deliberate one-variable control against `int8_row`:** identical bit-width, only scale granularity differs — it asks whether per-row scales are load-bearing, and they cost only 0.4 MB, so if tensor-scale holds accuracy there is no byte reason to prefer per-row. **Pre-registered rule (docstring, pre-run):** an arm SHIPS at a depth iff its paired boot95 **lower** bound ≥ −2.0pp vs same-depth fp32; KILLED iff **upper** < −2.0pp; INCONCLUSIVE between, reported as such. Compounding via the same double difference as step009, I(q) = [d6 quant loss] − [d12 quant loss], shared resample index: ORTHOGONAL iff CI ⊆ ±2.0pp. Depth acts on the vision tower and quantization on the text tables — different modules, but they meet at the logits, so orthogonality is HYPOTHESIS, measured not assumed. **Pre-flight on the real weights (weight-space only, says nothing about task accuracy):** int8_row rel_rmse 0.0121 / row-cos 0.99993 (should be ~free), int8_tensor 0.0327 / 0.99940 (2.7× worse at identical bit-width — the control has real contrast), int4_row 0.219 / 0.9775 (**the arm that will actually decide something**). Bytes, measured not proxied (meditation-005): tables 227.1 → 57.2 (int8) / 28.8 MB (int4); whole model 1025.9 → 856.0 (−16.6%) / 827.6 MB (−19.3%). **Stacked with d6 (−42.5M params ≈ 170 MB), d6 + int4_row projects to ~657 MB, ≈ −36% of the fp32 model — the drone headline, if the accuracy holds.** Smoke PASSED (cpu, n=20): all 8 cells populate, byte columns print, both verdict blocks run.

- **vlm_step009 (do depth truncation and token pruning COMPOUND, or cancel?): DONE (2026-07-29, mini_mps, ~1.6 h, n=3859 × 8 cells).** **Validity anchors exact: `d12_k64` = 0.711 and `d6_k64` = 0.713, both reproducing step006 to three decimals on an independent eval pass.** **No interaction detected at any budget** — I(32) = +0.0013 boot95 [−0.0109, +0.0132] → **ORTHOGONAL** by the pre-registered rule; I(16) = +0.0057 [−0.0111, +0.0225] and I(8) = −0.0034 [−0.0223, +0.0155] → **INCONCLUSIVE as registered**, missing only because their CI half-widths run 0.2–0.3pp past the ±2.0pp tolerance (all three point estimates within ±0.6pp of zero, every McNemar p ≥ 0.35). Reported as registered, not rounded into a pass. **The decisive result is the timing column, and it KILLS pruning at this operating point (CONFIRMED):** `vision_ms` is flat to the third digit across k (65.0/64.9/64.9/64.9 at d12; 35.1 ×4 at d6) because pruning happens *after* the tower, so only prefill moves — **14.3 → 11.2 ms, ~3 ms = 3.9% of total**. The smoke's 41.6 → 24.2 ms was n=20 warm-up noise and **overstated the payoff ~6×**. Against that 3 ms the accuracy bill is −5.2pp at k=32, −13.4pp at k=16, −28.9pp at k=8, identical at both depths. Depth buys 29.9 ms (37.7%) for **+0.2pp**; tokens buy ~3 ms for −5.2pp. **Drone ships d6 unpruned.** Scope: SmolVLM-256M prefills only 64 image tokens into a small text stack — the *method* is not killed, this operating point is. **Carry forward: `agree`(d6_k64, d12_k64) = 0.499** — matched top-1, disagreement on half the images; the distilled tower is an equally-accurate *different* classifier, not a functional copy (step004's warning, now confirmed at full n on the shipping config). Consequence: learned token selection (§11.5) DEPRIORITISED to the parking lot — its ceiling is a 3 ms envelope. Full writeup `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part4.md` §14. Result: `results/frontier/vlm_step009_compound_tokens_d6_k64-32-16-8__mini_mps.json`. Original block: **RUNNING (mini_mps, eval-only, 3859 images × 8 cells).** `scripts/frontier/vlm_step009_compound_tokens.py` (194 lines). The Compounding Rule's isolation ablation for the drone's two confirmed levers — depth (d6 at parity, −16.58% params, 1.60× vision) × top-k tokens (+18pp over stride at equal budget, vlm_step002). Adjacent signal paths (depth changes what the tower emits, top-k selects among what it emitted), so orthogonality is HYPOTHESIS until measured. Full 2×4 grid {d12, d6} × k {64, 32, 16, 8} on ONE eval set with per-image records, so all four cells of every comparison are paired. k=64 is a no-op prune, so `d12_k64` must reproduce step006's 0.711 or the run is unreadable. **Quantity of interest is the double difference** I(k) = [acc(d6,k) − acc(d6,64)] − [acc(d12,k) − acc(d12,64)], bootstrapped with one shared resample index across all four terms. **Pre-registered rule:** ORTHOGONAL iff I(k)'s 95% CI ⊆ ±2.0pp; SUB-ADDITIVE iff upper < −2.0pp; SUPER-ADDITIVE iff lower > +2.0pp; else INCONCLUSIVE. ±2pp inherited from step006. `agree` read alongside top-1 in every cell (step004's agree 0.420 vs top-1 0.560 is the standing proof that closed-10-way top-1 can be right for the wrong reason). Smoke PASSED (cpu, n=20): grid populated, d12_k64 ref exact, I(k) computed. **Incidental from the smoke — check at full n:** vision_ms is flat across k (58 at d6, 112 at d12) and only prefill moves (41.6 → 24.2 ms at k=8), so token pruning is a *prefill* lever worth ~12% of total, while depth is worth ~35%. If that holds, the drone's stacking order is depth-first.

- **vlm_step008 (is 16.58% a floor or a ceiling? d3 + d9 at the budget that made d6 work): DONE — d3 KILLED, d6 stands as the shipping depth; d9 never trained (external OOM). (5060ti_cuda, `--depths 3 9`, 25 ep, 9469 train, 3859 eval).** `scripts/frontier/vlm_step008_depth_budget.py` (200 lines). d6 anchor **loaded not retrained**, so if it fails to reproduce step006's 0.713 nothing else in the run is readable. **Pre-registered rule (in the docstring, before the run):** a depth SHIPS in place of d6 iff its paired boot95 **lower** bound ≥ −2.0pp vs teacher; KILLED iff the **upper** bound < −2.0pp; INCONCLUSIVE between, reported as such and not rounded. Independently: rel_mse still falling over the last 5 epochs ⇒ that depth's accuracy is a **floor**, licensing a longer run; flat ⇒ it is the ceiling. Rationale: d3 read −28pp at T0, but the *same* T0 budget read −18pp for d6 and d6 recovered all of it at full budget (0.2573 → 0.2011 → 0.1639, monotone, never flat), so the binding constraint is CONFIRMED to be budget not depth over the range tested. d3 is the 24.87%-params / 2.33×-vision row — the one the drone actually wants. **Getting it onto the 5060ti took four fixes, all recorded in `VLM_TRAJECTORY_part4.md` §13.1** — the reusable one: batch 8/4/2 all OOM'd at the *identical* allocation point, which is the signature of a fixed-footprint problem, so accumulation could not have helped; `vlm_distill.park()` now parks the text stack + frozen teacher layers on CPU during training (~1.19 GB freed). **Slot-free ≠ GPU-free:** 13.36 of 15.48 GiB is held by another user's idle Jupyter kernel that `slot_status.sh` cannot see. Budget caveat: sampler floored to 9469, not 9352, so d3/d9 get 1.25% more data than the anchor. **CRASHED then SALVAGED (2026-07-29).** d3 finished its 25 epochs and its checkpoint was written (`vlm_step008_d3_25ep__5060ti_cuda.pt`, 17:51); d9 then died on `torch.OutOfMemoryError ... Tried to allocate 24.00 MiB ... 5.94 MiB is free` at `modeling_idefics3.py:318`, because the other user's kernel had grown to 13.36 GiB. **The cause was external, so nothing of ours was killed** — but the blast radius was ours: `main()` trained every depth first and ran ONE `evaluate()` at the end, so a failure in the last arm discarded the finished one too. **Fixed at the category, in the shared library:** `vlm_distill.train_or_resume()` reloads any depth whose checkpoint already exists instead of retraining it, and step008 now skips `cache_teacher` entirely when no depth needs training. A depth sweep is therefore restartable at the arm granularity, for this run and every future one. `hist` is None on a resumed arm so `verdict()` reports the floor/ceiling read as *unknown* rather than inventing a curve from weights that do not contain one — for d3 the curve is in the crashed run's log (0.2370 → 0.2236 over the last 5 epochs, ~0.0035/ep, **still falling ⇒ FLOOR**, so any KILL verdict kills *d3-at-25-epochs*, not d3). Second hazard the resume logic introduced and closed in the same edit: a 1-epoch `--smoke_test` checkpoint would have silently resumed as a real 25-epoch arm, so `SLOT` now gets a `_smoke` suffix and every smoke artefact is unmatchable against a real one. Resume path verified locally (2nd smoke run: no cache, no training, both arms loaded). **Relaunched `--depths 3` only** — d9 is not retried while the foreign kernel holds 13.7 of 16.3 GiB, and eval-only fits in the 2.1 GiB that leaves because inference needs no gradients or optimizer state. **Then a SECOND, unrelated bug ate the same run — `vlm_eval.mcnemar_exact` raised `OverflowError: int too large to convert to float` AFTER a successful 38-minute eval.** `p = sum(comb(n,i) …) * (0.5**n) * 2` builds the tail sum as a Python int of order 2^n and then converts it to float, which overflows past n ≈ 1020 discordant pairs. Not hypothetical and not about d3 specifically: **d6 survived only because its n was 872**, i.e. every prior VLM result sat just under a cliff that any larger or noisier arm crosses. Fixed as one exact int ratio, `2 * sum(...) / 2**n` — int/int division is correctly rounded without ever materialising either side as a float, so it is exact AND overflow-proof. Verified it reproduces d6's p to all printed digits (0.81263 from b=440, c=432) and now returns finite p at n=1129 (8.6e-58) and n=20000 (2.0e-45), which were previously unreachable. **The eval numbers are deterministic (seed 42, fixed checkpoints), so the crashed run's table stands and the rerun reproduces it:** teacher_d12 0.711 / 25.8 ms, distil_d6 **0.713** / 13.6 ms / 1.44×, distil_d3 0.631 / 7.5 ms / 1.84×. **The anchor gate PASSED to the digit** — d6 delta +0.0021, b=440 c=432, p=0.81263, boot95 [−1.27pp, +1.74pp] → SHIP, matching step006's [−1.3, +1.7]; the run is therefore readable. **VERDICT: `distil_d3 delta −0.0796, b=426 c=733, p=0.00000, boot95 [−9.67pp, −6.19pp] → KILLED`** under the pre-registered rule (upper bound −6.19pp < −2.0pp, so not a marginal call — the whole interval is outside the band). Agreement columns agree from a second direction: d3 matches the teacher on 0.609 of images, argmax agrees on 0.422, KL 2.721, vs d6's 0.699 / 0.499 / 1.973 — d3 is a different function, not a noisier teacher. **n = 1159 discordant pairs, i.e. ABOVE the ~1020 overflow threshold — the McNemar fix was load-bearing for this exact number, not defensive.** **Scope limit travels with the KILL:** d3's rel_mse was still falling at ep 25 (FLOOR), so this kills *d3-at-25-epochs*, not d3 — the same signature d6 showed before its budget grew. Reopening d3 needs a longer budget and is only worth it if 8.29% more params becomes load-bearing for the drone target. JSON `results/frontier/vlm_step008_depth_budget_d3_25ep__5060ti_cuda.json` (626 KB, per-image records) pulled local; stale `*_1ep__local.*` smoke artefacts deleted from the remote. Written up as `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part6.md` §19 (result) + §20 (both harness fixes, recorded as reusable findings rather than step008 incidents).

- **vlm_step007 (does the d6 PARITY survive off-domain?): DONE — DOMAIN-SCOPED, CONFIRMED. "Distil once, ship anywhere" is KILLED.** (mini_mps, ~8 h.) Held-5 seen5_d6 top1 **0.168** vs teacher 0.683 = **−51.5pp**, b=5 c=520, boot95 [−54.7, −48.3] — against a 0.100 chance floor, i.e. the held classes are gone from the tower, not degraded. Seen-5 only −3.6pp. **Interaction +47.9pp**, vs the all-10 control's **−9.5pp** — *opposite sign*, so this is not the two halves differing in difficulty. Clears step006's ±2pp power caveat by 24×. **Mechanism confirmed by the loss:** seen-5 finished at rel_mse **0.1071** (< step004's 0.1639 on all 10) — it fit its own distribution strictly *better* while losing the other half, so low distillation loss is a within-domain fit statistic and **cannot** be used for cross-domain model selection. **Confound, as pre-registered:** budget matched on updates ⇒ seen-5 saw each image 50× vs 25×; domain restriction and 2×-repetition overfit are not separable here, so *pure* domain attribution is HYPOTHESIS while the direction and scale are CONFIRMED. Matched-epoch rerun parked (same drone conclusion either way). **Drone consequence:** collect unlabelled target-domain frames before distilling — cheap (a drone makes them by flying), but mandatory. Full write-up `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part4.md` §12. `results/frontier/vlm_step007_domain_split_d6_50ep__mini_mps.json`.

  Original block: **RUNNING (mini_mps, launched after step006, ~8 h).** Smoke PASSED (cpu, n=40/20: split correct, control checkpoint loaded, and the **control arm read 0.700 vs teacher 0.700 on seen / 0.700 vs 0.750 on held — interaction +0.05, ≈0 exactly as step006 predicts**; the 1-epoch seen5 arm was correctly garbage at rel_mse 5.85). Live run: **4809 train images over 5 classes = 240,450 image-updates**, +2.8% vs step004's 233,800 — close enough to call the budget matched, and the direction favours the seen-5 student, so it cannot manufacture a *negative* held-out result. `scripts/frontier/vlm_step007_domain_split.py` (166 lines) + new shared `scripts/frontier/vlm_distill.py` (89 lines). **Reframed after vlm_step006's null** — with the overtake dead, the question is no longer "better tower vs domain-adapted tower" (there is no gain to attribute) but whether **16.58% of the model can be deleted at parity on images the distillation never saw**. That is the single most load-bearing open question in the trajectory, because it picks the drone recipe:

  | outcome | drone recipe reads | reading |
  |---|---|---|
  | Δ_held ≈ 0 (and Δ_seen ≈ 0) | distil once on **any** unlabelled images, ship the d6 tower anywhere | parity is a property of the method |
  | Δ_held < 0, Δ_seen ≈ 0 | you **must** collect unlabelled target-domain frames before deploying | parity is domain-scoped |
  | both Δ < 0 | neither — half-data budget hurt regardless | confounded; re-read against the all-10 control |

  Both of the first two are shippable; they cost wildly different amounts of fieldwork. Design unchanged: hold 5 of the 10 classes out of the **distillation data only**; the readout stays the same constrained 10-way choice over all 10 labels, so nothing about the task changes — only which classes the student's unlabelled images contained. Eval on the two halves separately.

  **Free control that makes it tight:** the existing all-10 student (`_d6_25ep9k__mini_mps.pt`) is evaluated on the same two halves. It saw every class, so its seen−held gap is the "no domain restriction" baseline — without it, a seen/held gap could just be the two halves differing in difficulty. Eval-only, so it costs no training. **step006 makes this control quantitative:** the all-10 student is now known to sit at +0.2pp over the full val set, so it should read ≈0 on **both** halves; if it does not, the halves differ in difficulty and the seen-5 deltas must be read against that offset, not against zero. **Power caveat inherited from step006:** n=1000/group has ~half the resolving power of the n=3859 run that just overturned a 5.5pp claim — call only interaction effects well outside ±2pp. **Budget matched on UPDATES not epochs:** 5 classes floor to ~4676 train images, so `--epochs 50` lands on the same 233,800 image-updates as step004's 9352×25. **Caveat to state with any result:** 50 passes over half the images overfits more than 25 over all of them, biasing *toward* looking domain-scoped. Wnid split is alternating over sorted order (seen = tench/cassette/church/truck/golf, held = springer/chainsaw/horn/pump/parachute), not first-5/last-5.

- **vlm_step006 (does distil_d6 > teacher_d12 survive a real n?): DONE — NO. Pre-registered null branch fired.**

  | arm | params | %save | top1 | same | agree | KL | vis_ms | total | speed |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | teacher_d12 | 256,484,928 | 0.00 | 0.711 | 1.000 | 1.000 | 0.000 | 64.9 | 79.2 | 1.00× |
  | **distil_d6** | 213,957,696 | **16.58** | **0.713** | 0.699 | 0.499 | 1.973 | **35.0** | 49.6 | **1.60×** |

  McNemar b=440 c=432, **delta +0.0021, p=0.813**, paired bootstrap 95% **[−0.0127, +0.0174]**. `results/frontier/vlm_step006_eval_scale_d6_n3900_s42_val__mini_mps.json`.

  **CONFIRMED — d6 distilled ≡ d12 teacher on top-1 at −16.58% params and 1.60× vision.** The +5.5pp overtake was n=200 noise: *both* n=200 numbers were wrong in opposite directions (teacher 0.685→0.711, student 0.740→0.713). Not an underpowered null — the bootstrap CI **excludes degradation beyond 1.3pp**, so it bounds the effect rather than failing to find one. Three carry-forwards: (1) **discordance is huge and balanced**, 872/3859 = 22.6% split 440/432 — the student is a differently-wrong network of equal skill, not a compressed copy, and this directly refutes the "capacity-limited regression = denoiser" HYPOTHESIS (a denoiser predicts b ≫ c). **DROPPED.** (2) KL and vis_ms reproduced across eval sets (1.986→1.973, 35.5→35.0 ms) — **latency/distributional claims survive small n, accuracy claims do not**; treat every n ≤ 200 accuracy claim in this line as provisional. (3) `same` 0.699 vs `agree` 0.499 — the constrained 10-way readout recovers ~20pp of agreement the free argmax loses, so all claims here are scoped to *this* readout. Docs: `learnings/frontier/VLM_TRAJECTORY_part3.md` §9 (§8/§10 downgraded to parity per the pre-registered rule).

  Original block: **RUNNING (mini_mps, `--n_eval 3900`, ~30 min).** `scripts/frontier/vlm_step006_eval_scale.py` (176 lines). **Spends zero training compute** — loads `_d6_25ep9k__mini_mps.pt` and re-runs eval only, at 3859 balanced val images (the `n//10` floor again), keeping **per-image records** so the teacher-vs-student comparison is paired.
